Eight turns, roleplaying their answers yourself (you know them best — you're the world's foremost expert on your character, which is *why* the interview's rules are yours to enforce). Time-box: fifteen minutes. Harvest: three scene-usable details + one refusal. If all three details could appear in anyone's story, your sheet's field 5 was an adjective — go strengthen it *before* the next interview.

## Arcs across chapters

Chapters 3 chose the arc; this chapter *sustains it across a draft that takes months* — the tracking problem is logistical, not theoretical: by chapter 14 you will not remember what Mara's false belief sounded like in chapter 3, whether Theo's "silent labor" pattern escalated or plateaued, or which of your outlines' flaw-choices actually made it to prose. **The arc tracker** is the instrument — one table, updated at every draft mint (Ch 7's bible-sync ritual, extended):

> PROMPT CARD PC-19 — Arc tracker
>
> ```text
> 
> Role: continuity auditor for CHARACTER CHANGE (not plot).
> Context: character sheet (arc type, false belief, flaw-pattern,
> climax choice) + my chapter-by-chapter notes below + any drafted
> chapters I paste (bible-first: paste the sheet FIRST, always).
> Task: for each chapter-note/passage, extract in a table:
> chapter | pressure applied (what forced the pattern or its
> alternative) | pattern response (flaw-behavior or growth-behavior —
> quote the triggering line if pasted) | false-belief status
> (reinforced / challenged / unaddressed) | cost logged? (Y/N —
> which cost) | rung moved (P/R/E/—).
> Flag: (a) 3+ consecutive chapters with pattern UNADDRESSED (arc
> stalling — the character is coasting); (b) growth-behavior
> appearing BEFORE any cost paid for the flaw's alternative (arc
> *pre-paid* — change unearned, the classic false-dawn); (c) any
> row contradicting the sheet's arc type (if negative arc: flag
> unexplained kindness-spike; if positive: flag unexplained
> surrender-spike). NO recommendations — flags are my inventory to
> adjudicate.
> ```

The table's column design *is* the craft: **pressure → response → belief-status → cost** mirrors Chapter 3's chain running in *time* — the tracker's job is to prove the chain has chapters to stand on, and its three flags catch the two classic arc failures plus one: **stalling** (pattern never pressured — the draft's equivalent of the Act II hole, but for character: plot moves, person doesn't; readers feel this as "I liked her better at the start"); **pre-paid change** (growth appearing *before* the climax's price — the false dawn, usually from writing the *intended* end-state into early scenes because you've already *felt* the arc complete in your head — the sheet's payoff-aspiration leaking into act one); and **arc-type contradictions** (your chosen arc fighting the prose's drift — a negative-arc protagonist acquiring unearned warmth the prose finds easier than corrosion — *smiling characters are cheap*; the flag's job is to make the cheapness visible as rows).

**Updating discipline:** the tracker fills *from the hub* — chapter notes from the session log's one-liners, passages from draft-current — never from memory of memory (months-old recollection of "when did Theo last do the silent-labor thing" is exactly the amnesia the instrument exists for); one table-row pass per draft mint takes ten minutes and *is* the character half of Ch 12's continuity pass already queued (PC-22 there reads this table as its character input — the passes share documents, not vibes); flags adjudicate like any critic output (decisions rows: fix now / fix at character pass / *flag is wrong, prose is right* — the last one possible because the sheet can be amended *deliberately*; every arc-tracker flag that ends in a sheet-v-next is the system working *backwards* from pages, which is how real characters outgrow plans honestly rather than through silent drift).

**Supporting-cast tracking (cut-down form):** cast members with functional roles (Ch 3's field 12) get a *presence/contrast* row instead of arcs: chapter | appeared Y/N | contrast beat shown (the inversion still legible?) | one new detail (or — : stale since Ch N). A foil who vanishes for eight chapters or stops *contrasting* (their choice no longer legible against the protagonist's) is a structural dropout — catchable in seconds from the cut-down table, expensive if first noticed by a beta reader in Ch 13 ("I forgot Casi existed" is a triage row nobody wants).

## Distinct dialogue voices

The blind test, formalized — the chapter's falsification instrument:

> PROMPT CARD PC-18 — Voice differentiation test
>
> ```text
> 
> Role: blind attribution examiner.
> Input: 6–10 dialogue EXCHANGES from my draft, speakers labeled
> only A/B (strip tags first — or paste tags and instruct me to
> cover them while you reason — blind either way).
> Task 1: for each exchange, guess the speaker from character sheets
> (paste the cast's voice notes: diction / syntax / habit /
> never-say). Show confidence 1–5 per guess.
> Task 2: list every line that could have been said by EITHER
> speaker without meaning changing (swap-violations), quote them.
> Task 3: for each cast voice, output a ONE-LINE "fingerprint":
> the pattern that identifies them (e.g., "clipped negatives +
> tool metaphors + never asks questions").
> Output: guess table, swap list, fingerprints. NO rewrites —
> fixing is my pass.
> ```

Task 1's confidence scores are the nuance that makes the test real: **guesses at confidence 5 are fine even if technically ambiguous** (strong fingerprints make attribution *obvious* — that's success), while **guesses at 1–2 on *major* characters are failures to fix** (the machine couldn't tell your leads apart using their *own* notes — the sheets and the prose have diverged, or both are soft); Task 2's swap-list gives you the surgical worklist (each quoted line gets rewritten *in that speaker's fingerprint* — your hands, per the attribution-pass rule of Ch 10); Task 3's fingerprints graduate into the bible §2 voice-notes (one line each — the most-pasted character data after the names list: PC-15's context pack and PC-14's dialogue locks *both* draw from them).

**The three levers, worked —** because "make them sound different" needs mechanics beneath the test. Build from Example B's cast (detective **Inspector Nkechi Bello**; xenobotanist **Dr. Leila Haddad** — the station-story protagonist from Ch 3's split examples) and see one situation run through each:

*Situation: told the plan will fail, response starting "That's not—":*

- **Diction (the word shelf):** Nkechi takes the shelf of *procedure and record* — "That's not in the report, and the report is what we have." Leila takes the shelf of *measurement* — "That's not within tolerance; the loop's been bleeding two percent since shift change." Vocabulary *domains* separate characters harder than vocabulary *levels* (both are precise professionals — level-matching is why the intern slips past naive checks; **domain** is the lever).
- **Syntax (the sentence skeleton):** Nkechi: fragments welded by conjunctions ("That's not in the report, and that's the end of it — file it or don't."). Leila: conditional ladders, clauses stacking like rack hardware ("If the plan assumes the loop holds, then the plan assumes something the sensors don't say."). Same intelligence, opposite architectures — *syntax under stress is the fastest tell in the business*, which is why Ch 3's field 10 puts sentence-length-under-pressure on the sheet *before* any dialogue exists.
- **Rhythm + habit + never-say (the metronome and the bans):** Nkechi's habit: answers questions with the *record's* version of events; never: says "I think" (record-voice bans speculation-markers). Leila's habit: trails into unit-mutter ("—two percent, two percent…"); never: says "trust me" (trust is a *measurement* to her, not a plea). Habits are *visible under dialogue-tags-covered reading* — the blind test's natural allies; never-says are checkable by search (Ch 12's line pass runs the list — bans enforced mechanically, not by memory).

Run those three levers *down your own cast* now: two characters with different *domains* + different *skeletons* + one *habit* and one *never* each are distinguishable at confidence 5 while speaking about unrelated topics — which is exactly what the swap-test will verify. Voice differentiation is not a talent; it's *four specified variables per character, maintained across every line* — and the maintained-part is where the machine both helps (searching for swap-violations across 90,000 words faster than any human) and hurts (its own generation *wants* to converge every voice toward the house register — Ch 10's locks and your attribution passes are the wall).

## The flattening problem

Now the chapter's honesty section — the pitfall named as a *systematic tendency*, not a scare story, with the mechanism and the countermeasures, because everything above is *detection*; this is *prevention*.

**Why the machine flattens characters toward the agreeable average.** Four forces, stacked: (1) **training mass** — likable, competent, quip-capable characters have more *text* written about them (the helpful-intern prior isn't a bug from nowhere: it's what a model's "character" most often *is* across its diet); (2) **sycophancy's cousin** — generation that might displease a user's *implied* sympathies (your beloved protagonist's assistant-generated scenes getting *cruel* to him) completes toward warm-safe; (3) **constraint poverty** — Ch 6's core lesson applied to people: adjectives on a character sheet ("complex," "tragic past") resolve to center; only *behavioral* constraints (flaw-first interview rules, fingerprint bans) push off it; and (4) **averaging across your own pasted context** — if the desk holds your *kind* scenes, kindness is the style-condition next scene inherits; the desk has no memory that your book's *point* is the characters' unkindnesses unless you *say so* (bible §5 story-rules, restated).

**Countermeasures, one per force — the chapter's stack:**

1. **Flaw-first everything (against training mass):** PC-17's rule 3 extended to every generation: character-touching prompts include the *flaw-behavior* line from the sheet in their context pack, phrased as *behavior* ("answers grief with calendar logistics"), so the completion's prior includes *your pattern* as a probable continuation. The intern doesn't fight your sheet; your sheet has to *be* in the room, in pattern-language, every time.
2. **Adversarial casting audits (against warm-safe):** monthly, one pass: "list every interaction in these chapters where a character was *kinder/wiser/funnier than their sheet's flaw permits* — quote each" — the mirror-image of PC-11's critique (here: hunting *unearned warmth* the way structure-hunting went after "and then"). Findings adjudicate normally; the typical result is a cluster of early-chapter smiles to strip (your drafting-speed days lean on the intern most — the audit's clusters reveal your *calendar* too).
3. **Behavioral, never dispositional, specification (against constraint poverty):** sheets specify *what they do under X pressure*; if a field could be satisfied by a personality-quiz answer, rewrite it until it *wouldn't* — this is Ch 3's pattern-not-label rule again, and it is the single highest-leverage anti-flattening edit that exists (a good flaw-pattern is a *generator* of un-average behavior; an adjective is a permission slip for it).
4. **Desk hygiene for character work (against context-averaging):** character-sensitive generation opens with the cast *extract relevant to this scene only* — not your whole pleasant chapter folder; anti-relevant context (scenes where your antagonist *won*) gets pasted *when you need the edge*, not left to accumulate warmth; and the story-rules §5 bans ("no character is reassured in this scene" / "cruelty must go unanswered once per act") are the standing constraint-pack version.

**Stereotype drift — the adjacent hazard, handled bluntly.** When "distinctiveness" gets sought through *group* markers instead of *individual* pressure — dialect-as-costume, culture-as-flavor-packet, trauma-as-backstory-accessory — characters stop being people and become *types with authenticating detail*, a failure that is both craft-dead (type = flat by definition, Ch 3) and *ethical* (Ch 15's territory: caricature of real groups is harm, not risk-free style — the ETHICS NOTE this chapter has been owed, spent here): **fictional individuals may be awful; groups they *represent* must be as particular and as granted interiority as your protagonists' group.** Operational guards, since principle needs mechanism: (a) the *specificity audit* — for any character carrying group-markers, name three *individual* traits that have nothing to do with the group-marking (if you can't, the group *is* the characterization — a stereotype in evidence's clothing); (b) *self-check the source* — if your character's group-knowledge comes from media rather than from contact, memory, or research, the sheet gets a `research/` entry (first-person accounts, history — sourced, dated, Ch 5's type-split applied to *people's* histories) or the marker comes *off*; (c) *the machine's role is bounded here* — generation is fine for *plot behavior* of characters whose group-context you've grounded, and is **not consulted as authority on the lived texture of groups you don't know** (it completes *representations* of groups — the same distribution-flattening that produces clichés, now aimed at humans who exist); your sources for that texture are human and primary, full stop. One line for the style guide's inclusivity promise, now load-bearing: *vary your cast across cultures, ages, genders, bodies — and give every member of every group the flaw-chain depth you give your leads, or don't write them in.*

> REALITY CHECK — PC-18's blind test, PC-17's flaw-first rules, and the audits above raise the floor substantially; they cannot *guarantee* un-flattened characters, because the generator's center-of-mass pull never turns off — it is subtracted from, never absent. What this means for your workflow: machine-*drafted* character dialogue always passes through your attribution+voice pass (Ch 10's standing rule — *the seam where flattening enters is sentence-level, so the seam where it exits is too*); interviews and trackers shape *material and continuity*, not final lines; and your cast's ultimate un-average-ness has exactly one source — Ch 3's sheets written from *your* specific humans-and-observations (the model cannot supply the one ingredient it doesn't have: your particular life's evidence about particular people. No prompt fixes a sheet built from vibes; every prompt above *amplifies* a sheet built from observation).

### A situational interview, run live (Theo, before the fight beat)

To show the interactive format's texture before you run your own — abbreviated, but the *shape* of turns and corrections intact. Context pack: Theo's cut-down sheet (flaw-pattern: substitutes silent labor for conversation; want: keep the house as-is) + scene pressure: *"the kitchen fight — Mara is inventorying; the December fact is about to land."*

> **[Interviewer]:** Q1 — It's the night before you'll say the thing about December. You're fixing something in the kitchen that doesn't need fixing. What exactly is broken, and what's actually wrong?
>
> **[Theo]:** The drawer runner on the far side. It sticks. I could do it in my sleep — I have. [flaw speaking first: the *wrong* repair — camera detail present: the runner.]
>
> **[Interviewer — verdict line]:** In-character ✓ (labor-substitution, concrete). One probe deeper —
>
> **[Interviewer]:** Q2 — Mara offers to hold the flashlight. What do you say, and what does your hand do while you say it?
>
> **[Theo]:** "Don't need it — there's the window light." And my hand keeps the screwdriver working, doesn't stop when I answer. Stops *after*, when she's gone. [habit captured as *timing* — refusal expressed in logistics, the pattern's exact grammar; the pause-after-her-leaving is a beat-ready detail.]
>
> **[Interviewer — verdict]:** In-character ✓✓ — the stopped-hands detail serves scene 5's physical business; filing to beats, not bible (consumed = scheduled).
>
> **[…Q3–Q6 compressed: the December answer arrives as logistics again — "She asked. I said you were on the survey. Two things in one sentence — that's how I told her." — flagged and kept: flaw-pattern under *memory* pressure, worst-case framing passed…]**
>
> **[Q7 — the probe the sheet warned about]:** What won't you talk about tonight?
>
> **[Theo]:** [drift caught live] ~~Whether I'm angry.~~ → **[Interviewer correction]:** DRIFT: "angry" is abstract self-diagnosis (rule 2). Restate as camera.
>
> **[Theo]:** …The two weeks after the funeral. Nothing specific — *nothing*. [the refusal itself becomes the Q8-output: "one refusal the character wouldn't touch — and why": the emptiness *is* the content; pushing would break the 60-word cap's spirit and the sheet's dosing schedule — wounds arrive on outline, not on interviewer curiosity.]
>
> **[Q8 + harvest]:** Three usable: the runner; the hand-keeps-working beat; the two-things-in-one-sentence self-report. One refusal: the funeral fortnight (reason: dosed at fight beat, not interview). **Sheet verdict:** no field changes — Q2's "window light" line *may* join setting-facts §4 as a checkable (kitchen has north light — consistency: verify against Ch 4's "thin harbor light" description before bible-minting).

*Annotation of what just happened:* two of eight turns needed correction (drift and abstraction — the intern's two doors), corrections *strengthened the sheet's operational vocabulary* (the verdict line's "flaw speaking first" phrasing now matches how the pattern *answers* under memory pressure — field 5 got one word richer via a decisions row); the refusal honored the dosing schedule (archaeology rule held under temptation); and the last consistency-check note — *verify north light against drafted prose* — shows interviews **serving** continuity rather than outrunning it (an interview-only "fact" contradicting chapters 4's light would be canon drift's birth; the check ran *before* filing, which is the whole discipline in one gesture). Total session cost: fourteen minutes. Scene-usable yield: three beats + one calibrated refusal. Your runs will look messier — mess is fine; *verdict lines and harvest sections are what make the mess legible six weeks later*.

### The unearned-warmth audit, run live

Countermeasure 2 deserves one concrete pass, since "monthly" arrives faster than expected. Input: three recent chapters, one prompt:

```text
Role: adversarial warmth-auditor for this cast.
For each chapter passage below: quote every moment a character is
kinder, wiser, funnier, or more reassured than their SHEET (pasted)
would license under the scene's pressure. Table: quote | character |
sheet-tension (which field it strains) | severity (1–3).
Also flag: reassurances that END scenes (comfort-resolutions —
banned per bible §5 "no character is reassured in this scene"
for scenes 5–9's pressure band) | humor that undercuts a beat
marked [cost] in the outline.
NO rewrites. End with the three highest-severity clusters by
chapter-range — clusters reveal drafting-calendar patterns.
```

Real findings from the Example A demo (abridged): *ch5: "She squeezed his shoulder — he almost smiled" → Theo, strains flaw-pattern (silent-labor doesn't do comfort-touches on demand), severity 2 — cluster: drafted Sunday evening; ch6: Aurelia "explained gently, as if to a child" → Aurelia strains her own field (never-pities-Mara), severity 1, but the *tag* is narrator-warmth about Mara — flag as narrator-slip; ch9: "they shared the look of two people who had finally understood" → BOTH strain no-reassurance rule, severity 3, comfort-resolution ending a [cost]-marked beat — outline violation smuggled in as prose.* Two patterns the calendar line exposes: **Sunday-evening smiles** (fatigue drafts — you reach for the intern when tired, exactly as predicted) and **narrator-as-comfort** (when characters can't be warm per sheets, the *narration* warms them instead — subtler, caught by the "narrator-warmth" clause). Adjudication: ch5 shoulder-touch → KEEEP-with-repair (touch *stays*, "almost smiled" goes — a refused smile is flaw-compatible); Aurelia tag → cut narrator clause; ch9 look-sharing → REWRITTEN at line pass (the cost-beat ends on Mara *mis*-reading Theo — truer to arc, and feeds the fight's escalation). The audit's third month is when the pattern flips: findings drop *below five* — your drafting-time detection has internalized the floor the audits set. (If they never drop: your sheets are licensing warmth the *story* needs — maybe the sheets are wrong — adjudicate *upward*, deliberately, via versioned sheet edits. The audit polices drift in **both** directions: unlicensed warmth *and*, by its absence of findings, a cast grown uniformly cold.)

### The cast voice-card: a one-page instrument

The sheet's field 10 ("speech + finger + never-say") deserves its physical form, because buried in a character sheet it gets skipped under drafting pressure: the **voice-card** — a *single page per named character*, kept at the drafting station (bible's cast section AND pasted as the first block of any PC-15/PC-18 session — the instrument's whole argument: *voice prep you'll actually consult is voice prep that exists on one page you can see while the scene's running*). Layout, worked for Example B's Insp. Nkechi Bello (the record-voice) so each slot's *fill-quality* is visible:

```text
VOICE CARD — Nkechi Bello (Insp.)           v2 · 2026-09
DOMAIN (the private grammar): police records — charge-sheet
  precision; every observation files as evidence-by-form
FINGERPRINT (3 rows, one sentence each):
  syntax: subordinate clauses stacked BEFORE the main verb —
          conclusions always *arrive after* their grounds
  diction: register-locked — "statement," "account," "the
          complainant" — never street-smooth, never judicial-
          pompous; the middle temperature of procedure
  habit: rounds figures to nothing ("many," not "forty-seven")
          UNLESS the record would carry the count
NEVER-SAY (the hard bans): "I think" / "maybe" / contractions
  on evidential claims / any weather-figure for mood / first-
  person opinion without a cited observation
PRESSURE DIFF (the arc note): under threat to her competence,
  the record-voice *intensifies* (clauses stack deeper) — the
  mask gets thicker, not thinner — until the one scene where
  she speaks plain (ch14's confession beat — the deviation's
  scheduled HERE so it reads as event, not drift)
FIXTURES (stable props of speech): asks for names in full
  before answering; says "noted" as a terminator; names the
  time of day when recalling ("four-forty, my break")
LINE BANK (3 approved quotes — style targets for Few-shot):
  "The account omits what accounts usually carry: a time."
  "Noted. Now the part the form doesn't ask for."
  "You've given me a sequence. I asked for a cause."
```

The card's five design decisions, each earning its slot: **(1) DOMAIN first** — the private-grammar line (Ch 11's deepest layer: *what would this person's model of the world sound like if it filed paperwork?*) sits above fingerprints because *domain generates fingerprint* — syntax and diction choices become *deducible* from it mid-draft (stuck on her next line? Ask: "how would the records-department *describe* this moment?" — the domain answers *faster* than imagination, and more reliably); **(2) PRESSURE DIFF as arc's voice-face** — the sheet's arc-type *translated into speech-behavior* (how the flaw *sounds* under load — Bello's mask-thickening vs. Haddad's conditional-laddening vs. Theo's withdrawal — the three-voices lesson compressed to one line per cast member: **voice-arc = which fingerprint row intensifies, which breaks, and where the break is scheduled** — without this line, a character's mid-book speech-shift reads as *inconsistency* to beta readers and as *your ear slipping* to PC-18's blind tests; with it, the deviation's *planned* and the sheet's own churn-alert ("fingerprint violated" in continuity passes) can distinguish *scheduled breaks* from *drift* — the waiver-paste pattern at character-scale, again); **(3) FIXTURES** — the small *stable* tics ("noted," the time-of-day habit) that do continuity-pass-5's work for free (PC-22's category D checks them by *quote-match* — fixtures are *grep-able voice*, the instrument serving two altitudes: drafting-rhythm *and* verification); **(4) LINE BANK as few-shot source** — three *approved* quotes the writer has signed off (PC-15/PC-14's anchor slots fill *from the bank*, never from thin air: the bank being where "few-shot style transfer" meets cast — *each character gets their own few-shot examples*, the Ch 6 lesson graduated from *one voice per project* to *one voice per person in it*); **(5) one page, versioned, dated** — `v2 · 2026-09` in the header *because cards evolve* (mid-draft discoveries about a voice get *edited into* the card with a version bump — the prompt-card lifecycle aimed at characters: the card that doesn't version becomes the card that *contradicts* the draft's later chapters, and the cast's voice-history ends up living only in prose nobody can paste). Production rule for the full cast: **spine and foil cards hand-written first** (your ear must build them — the machine may *test* them later via PC-18, never *author* them — the golden rule at voice-scale: sheet fields 1/5/10 are *your* prose, typed by you, the way the unaided-baseline paragraph was yours in Chapter 1 — coats and chorus get *half-cards* (domain + one fingerprint + never-say), sized to their screen-time — the cast charter's load-flags from Chapter 3 telling you *exactly* which characters have earned full pages). The instrument's closing test: draft a Bello scene with the card face-up beside the keyboard and *without* it, twice, five sessions apart — the mode-log records which draft's lines survive pass 2 at higher rates — when the answer's consistent (it will be), the voice-card's page count justifies itself: **three fingerprints, one pressure-diff, three quotes — the difference between a voice you *consult* and a voice you *remember inventing* under deadline.**

### Dialogue under load: the confrontation generator-check

The stack's most-tempting shortcut deserves its own worked diagnostic — scenes where *characters fight* (pressure maximal, subtext mandatory, the helpful-interne's siren-song loudest: conflict is exactly where machines most fluently produce *arguments that sound like people winning debates rather than people being hurt*). Take the chapter's four-force stack and point it at a confrontation — the checklist, then a compressed anatomy of what failure and success look like at the line level:

**Pre-generation (forces 1–2, aims and proxy):** the scene's *aim* must be sharper than "they fight" — the stack's form: *what does each party want HERE* (want lines from the sheets, one sentence each), *what will each party NOT say* (the never-say lists *facing each other* — the confrontation's true subject is almost always the *pair* of unsaid things), *what does the fight COST the relationship by its end* (the stakes-line — because a fight with no end-cost is *noise*, and the machine will happily generate *volume* — the aim-line existing so adjudication can ask "did this cost anything?" of every draft row); the proxy: *both* parties' voice-cards pasted (two Line Banks in context — the single most effective anti-flattening move for two-hander scenes: with both banks present, the *swap-test* becomes *structurally visible in the prompt itself* — lines that could belong to either bank get caught *before* generation by the collision the context makes obvious); and the emotional *trajectory* stated as beats (escalation shape: which beat each side's *real* subject surfaces *by accident* — fights are where sheet-wounds leak, and the leak needs *scheduling* or the machine will have both parties be *therapeutically articulate* about their wounds by exchange four — the single most common confrontation-failure signature: **mutual premature insight**, the helpful intern wearing lab coats).

**Generation (forces 3–4, context and shape):** locks for the confrontation species — *no summaries of emotion in narration during the exchange* (interiority only in the *gaps*: a reaction-line after an exit, never mid-volley — the fight's tempo owns the page), *every line carries an action-object* (the keys, the box-tape, the kettle — hands *doing something while mouths fight* — the sensory-channel rule doing subtext's work: what the hand does *contradicts or confirms* the mouth, and that contradiction *is* the craft), *no line states the real subject until the scheduled leak-beat* (the unsaid-things rule, checkable per line: "is this line *about* the boxes, or has it slipped to the funeral?" — slip before the scheduled beat = flag), *turn-count* stated (how many volleys before the leak — without a number, generation drifts toward *resolution-seeking*, the sycophancy-structure again: scenes *want* to end in understanding; your aim said *cost*; the number keeps the gap open as long as the aim requires). **Anatomy of a *failure* exchange** (the diagnostic pattern, anonymized): A accuses (surface-subject), B counters *with a perfectly-formed rebuttal* (debate-logic: responses that *answer* rather than *wound*), A escalates with *a fact from the record* (prosecution-structure — both parties arguing *to a third-party reader*), narration: "anger flashed between them" (the weather-rule's corpse), leak: never — the real subject *never slips* because both voices were *moderators* underneath their volume — total damage: 300 words of *procedural heat*, zero *relationship-cost*, every line swappable (PC-18 would flag every one at confidence 1 — the exchange's failure *total* and *diagnosable*: no banks in context → default-argument-shape; no leak-schedule → resolution-avoidance kept the real subject out; no hand-objects → weather filled the physical layer). **Anatomy of *success*** (the same beats, stack applied): A's first volley *names the wrong subject on purpose* (she's not-ready is *in* her want line); B's counter *does not answer it* — answers the *hand* instead ("you'll rip the tape" — the object absorbing the aggression, subtext's classic misdirection); escalation proceeds through *fixtures* (her "noted"-terminator *weaponized* — "Noted. Filed. Closed." — the fingerprint *performing the wound* — voice-card's fixture-line paying off mid-fight); leak beat hits *on schedule* at turn 7 — B says the funeral's *date* instead of the boxes' count (the real subject arriving *through a wrong-number* — the leak *disguised as an error*, fights' most honest leak-shape: people say the wrong specific thing when the right one's too big); narration *after* the volley: one line of hand-and-object (the tape, torn — the cost *externalized*); exit *without* repair (the end-cost line delivered by *structure*: the scene ends one beat before understanding, because the aim said *cost*, and the beat-count honored it). The compressed stack-checklist for your next confrontation: **want-lines ×2 pasted · never-says facing · leak-beat scheduled with a turn-number · hand-object in every exchange-line · no emotion-weather · no premature insight · end at cost, not repair** — seven locks, one page of prep, and the confrontation that used to take four re-rolls because "the argument felt too reasonable" now takes adjudication rows with *names* (reasonable-by-default was never a mystery: it's the helpful intern *moderating a disagreement it was told to write* — the stack doesn't make machines better at fights; it makes the *aim* specific enough that moderation becomes a *findable violation* instead of a *mood*).

## Worked example: Example B — same scene, three voices

The genre register, for technique demonstration: **"The Clockmaker's Alibi"** (mystery) and a beat from the Europa station story (SF), run through the full stack — interviews' fingerprints, PC-18 structure, verdict annotated. The scene: *someone explains why the locked workshop's bell rang after the theft.*

**Pass 1 — before the stack (the intern's first draft, as shipped):**

> "That's strange," Nkechi said, frowning at the door. "The bell shouldn't have rung. It's a very secure room, and no one could have gotten in. 'Unless,' she thought, 'someone wanted us to think they did.' She had seen this before — in her experience, the truth was always hiding behind an apparent impossibility. 'Tell me about the night watch,' she said kindly."

*Diagnosis, line by line:* "frowning" — camera-feelings tag; "very secure" — dispositional nothing (no *how*: bolt count? bell mechanism? — rule 1's camera failed); "kinds of strange" and "said kindly" — **intern markers** (adverb-comfort, unearned warmth — flag set (b) family: kindness before any pressure earned it); the interior summary — **disposable depth** (the "she had seen this before" gesture at experience *substitutes* for the experience); "apparent impossibility" — **house-register abstraction** where Nkechi's fingerprint (record-voice) demands the *record's* impossibility. Nobody is *distinguishable* in this passage — not because Nkechi's absent, but because **no fingerprint constraints were in the desk's context** — the pass is the countermeasure list *failing by absence*: sheet not pasted, flaw not phrased as behavior, story-rules silent, warm-safe default fully in charge.

**Pass 2 — fingerprints loaded (context pack: Nkechi's §2 line + flaw "substitutes procedure for risk" as behavior + §5 "no reassurance in this scene"; same beats, PC-15's unattributed format first):**

> A: The bell's on the inner frame. Inner frame's bolted from the workshop side. Report says bolted at 21:10, unbroken since.
> B: Then the report's wrong or the bell's not what the report means.
> A: Reports don't ring. [...] "Tell me about the night watch" beats "Tell me about the night watch," kind.

*Annotated:* A's shelf = the record ("Report says"); skeleton = fragments welded by conjunction; habit = *answers the impossible with the document's version* (flaw-as-behavior performing under pressure — procedure substituting for risk exactly as the sheet billed); never-say held (zero "I think"s — searchable at line pass). B's conditional-ladder architecture ("or the bell's not what…") could not swap into A without rewiring meaning — **swap-test passes at confidence 5** *before anyone was named*, because attribution now travels in the *speech* (the tags-free format made the fingerprints the only carriers — which is what "voices distinct" actually means mechanically). The kind-tag deletion at the end: anti-flattening countermeasure 2 catching unearned warmth *mid-pass* (the intern had smuggled "kindly" back in between rewrites — note it: the pull doesn't turn off; the passes subtract).

**Pass 3 — the SF register, same technique, different domain (Leila, abridged):**

> A: Loop's bleeding two percent. Plan's got it at zero. One of those is a wish.
> B: We can compensate at the manifold — reroute through —
> A: Through the secondary line rated fourteen percent below the surge you're planning. That's not compensation. That's arithmetic with hope in it.

*Annotated:* domains held (measurement shelf — "rated fourteen percent below" is Leila's *diction domain*, not Nkechi's record-shelf); syntax held (conditional ladders → the closing syllogism is *her* rhythm of proof: units, tolerances, refusals); the genre's *pressure* (oxygen math) is doing what kitchen-fights do in Example A — generating voice *differences under stress* rather than in biography (situational interviews' payoff: voices demonstrated in the scene that will *publish* them, not in wikis). Same mechanics, two genres — which is the chapter's thesis: the intern is universal, and so is the stack that keeps him out: **flaw-first context + fingerprints + unattributed tests + warmth-audits + your sheets' one irreplaceable ingredient.**

*Decisions-file rows for the demo (your Stretch will have its own):* intern-pass lines → cuts (with dates); "kind-tag" finding → style sheet's growing ban ("no kindness-adverbs on dialogue tags unless sheet-mandated"); Pass 2's non-answer ending → considered for outline-v4 (mystery beat needs Nkechi's *record*-refusal as the scene's turn — flagged, not minted — two-rule stand).

## Exercises

- **Try (15 min):** Run PC-17 once on a major character against one upcoming scene's pressure — you play the character, enforce the rules (camera-detail, no abstract-fear words, flaw speaks first, 60-word cap), harvest three usable details and one refusal; then write the sheet-field each harvested detail *serves* — if you can't name the field, the detail was decoration; file it in `cuts/`-adjacent `backstory-raw` instead of the bible.

- **Stretch (1–2 h):** The full blind-test round: strip tags from 6–10 exchanges across two or more speaking characters (paste sheets' voice-note lines as the exam data); run PC-18; adjudicate every swap-violation (rewrite *by hand* in the offender's fingerprint — diction domain, skeleton, habit); upgrade each cast member's fingerprint to a one-line bible §2 note if missing; run the *reverse* test too: find one exchange the machine attributed *wrong at confidence 5* — that mis-attribution is a sheet-prose contradiction: decide which one's wrong (usually the sheet — prose has *evidence*) and version the sheet deliberately.

- **Project (ongoing):** Arc tracker live: one table-row per drafted chapter (pressure / response / belief-status / cost / rung — ten minutes at every mint, Ch 7's sync ritual extended), flags adjudicated like any critic output, cut-down presence/contrast rows for foils. Milestone: when Chapter 12's character pass begins, your tracker *is* its input — arriving with a live table rather than a memory means the machine's character reading starts grounded in your documented arc instead of its priors about arcs.

## Pitfalls

> PITFALL — **Agreeable average (F-02's root).** *Symptom:* your cast converges on kind-competent-quip-wounded; dialogue exchanges pass the plot but fail the blind test ("who's speaking? does it matter?"); interviews return personality-quiz answers; beta readers later say "everyone was nice" (Ch 13's most-received note worldwide); your antagonist's letters home are unexpectedly *thoughtful*. *Why it happens:* the four stacked forces (training mass / warm-safe completion / constraint poverty / desk-averaging) — plus *your* speed-days drafting over the intern because warm prose is *fast* prose to generate and to accept. *Fix (stack, in order — detection runs on prevention):* (1) **specification:** rewrite sheet fields as flaw-*behavior* patterns until they'd fail a personality quiz (the highest-leverage edit — behavior generators can't complete to average); (2) **context:** flaw-line + fingerprints + §5 bans in every character-touching prompt's pack (flaw-first rules — the intern has to *fight* your pattern, not sail past it); (3) **detection:** PC-17's self-audit verdicts mid-interview, PC-18's swap-list + confidence table, the monthly *unearned-warmth* audit (hunt kinder-than-sheet moments — strip the cluster, usually early chapters); (4) **repair:** attribution pass by hand in fingerprints (Ch 10 rule — seams exit where seams enter: sentence level); (5) **grounding:** when sheets run dry of specificity, the fix isn't more prompting — it's *observation* (your life's evidence, Ch 3's specific-odd rule) and *sourced research* for textures you don't carry (never the model-as-group-authority — stereotype-drift's source). *Prevention:* the blind test becomes a *per-scene* habit for dialogue-heavy chapters (two minutes — tags off, read aloud, "obvious?" — confidence felt by *you*, the original blind examiner), fingerprints filed in the bible *before* drafting starts (PC-15's context depends on them existing — prevention is a *pre-drafting* act), and the cast's group-marking passing the specificity audit (three individual traits beyond the marker, or the marker comes off) — craft defense and ethical floor in one check.

Related failure for Appendix C: **F-02 (all characters sound the same)** — joint entry with Ch 10's locks and Ch 14's voice erosion (F-09): one pipeline (specify → generate under locks → mark → blind-test → audit → repair), three gates; this chapter is where the pipeline's *character* stage lives.

## Checklist

> CHECKLIST — You're ready to move on when:
> - [ ] Each major character passes the voice test: blind exchanges attributable at confidence 4–5 from fingerprints alone — and you've run PC-18's swap-list on your real draft, with violations rewritten by hand.
> - [ ] Arc tracker covers every chapter drafted (live table at the last mint), with zero unadjudicated flags — stalls, false-dawns, and arc-type contradictions each have a decisions row.
> - [ ] No character's backstory exceeds what the story needs: every interview answer is either consumed by a scheduled beat or filed non-canonical in `backstory-raw` (the archaeology rule held).
> - [ ] Fingerprints (one line each) and flaw-*behavior* patterns are in the bible's §2 — pasted into every character-touching prompt since the last session (context packs not skipping the sheet).
> - [ ] The specificity audit passed for every group-marked cast member (three individual traits beyond the marker) — and you can state in one sentence what the machine *cannot* be trusted with regarding groups you don't know from contact or sourced research.

## Key takeaways

- **The helpful intern is the machine's character default** — likable, competent, quip-wounded, available in every biography — produced by training mass, warm-safe completion, constraint poverty, and desk-averaging. It never *fights* your sheet; the sheet has to be in the room, in pattern-language, every time.
- **Interviews extract behavior only under pressure-rules:** situational (scene-pressured), interactive (one question at a time), camera-tested, flaw-first, 60-capped — answers are ideation until adjudicated against the sheet; backstory is need-scheduled and demand-limited, never archaeology.
- **Arcs are tracked, not remembered:** PC-19's pressure → response → belief-status → cost table (flagging stalls, pre-paid change, and arc-type contradictions) updates at every mint and *becomes* Chapter 12's character-pass input — ten minutes of logistics against months of draft.
- **Voices are four specified variables:** diction *domain*, syntax skeleton, habit, never-say — tested blind (PC-18's confidence table + swap-list), fingerprints filed in the bible, enforcement mechanical (search the never-says; tags covered, read aloud).
- **Flattening and stereotype drift are prevented at the sheet and the desk,** detected by audits, repaired at the sentence seam — and *grounded by your life*: the one ingredient no prompt supplies is your particular evidence about particular people; the model amplifies sheets, it cannot manufacture them.


# Chapter 12 — Revision & Self-Editing with AI

## Opening hook

The document is called `final-FINAL-v14-USE-THIS.md`. You know the number is a lie because you remember v9: the month you "fixed" the midpoint by softening the sister's line, which broke the arc's cost beat, which you patched in v11 by *adding* a confrontation — which re-broke the midpoint's map-change — which v13 smoothed by cutting the confrontation's last paragraph, leaving the fight's outcome ambiguous, which you now believe is actually *more ambiguous than you intended* but you cannot reconstruct which version intended what, because every revision since v8 has been one long Tuesday of free-floating tinkering: a word here, a paragraph's rhythm there, then back to Chapter 4 because something *feels* off, then the midpoint again, then words — **revising in a circle, polishing prose whose structure you suspect but have stopped testing.**

This is the most expensive failure mode in fiction: not laziness — *activity without a pass system*. The fix is architectural, and this chapter installs it: **one pass, one job, in order** — structure, character, scene, line, continuity — each pass a *bounded expedition* with its own checklist, its own machine instruments (the chapter's three cards: PC-20 developmental reader, PC-21 constrained line editor, PC-22 continuity checker), its own change-log, and its own **done criteria** so the circle stops on *paper* instead of on exhaustion. By the end you'll have run a full pass on real pages, adjudicated every machine suggestion in writing (the standing rule — now load-bearing at scale), and be able to state, with evidence, whether your draft is done — the question v14's filename has been *performing* instead of answering.

## Learning objectives

By the end of this chapter, you will be able to:

- [ ] Run the pass system: structure → character → scene → line → continuity, one job per pass, in order.
- [ ] Deploy AI as developmental reader (PC-20), constrained line editor (PC-21), and continuity checker (PC-22) — each on its own pass.
- [ ] Keep change logs that make revision decisions reversible, and adjudicate machine suggestions at scale without rubber-stamping.
- [ ] Recognize when a draft is done — written criteria, not vibes.

## The pass system

**One pass, one job.** The entire method in four words, and every part of it matters. *One job* — a pass looking for structure ignores your comma habits (the line pass's job) and your character's eye color (continuity's); single-purpose attention is what lets both you and the machine *find* things, because a mind — or a prompt — asked to "review everything" answers with the most available complaint (fluency-shaped nitpicks) and misses the expensive ones. *In order* — structure before scenes, scenes before sentences: fixing prose inside a structurally broken chapter is *paid polishing of soon-to-be-cut material*, and fixing a scene's beats before its structure is guaranteed rework (the order is also the *money* order — cheapest corrections first, per the Chapter 2 law). *Passes* — bounded: each has an entry condition (checklist version ready, previous pass's changes *applied and minted*), an exit condition (checklist + log complete), and a *never mid-pass* rule: you do not "just quickly fix" a line-level typo you notice during the structure pass — you *note it in the line-pass queue* and keep looking at structure (the noted-typo gets fixed anyway; what matters is your eyes never left altitude).

The five passes, at the resolution this book teaches (each with its question and its instrument):

| # | Pass | The one question | Primary instrument | Scope unit |
|---|------|------------------|--------------------|-----------|
| 1 | **Structure** | Does the *order and pressure* deliver the skeleton's stakes and climax? | PC-20, run outline-vs-draft | whole draft (or per-part for novels) |
| 2 | **Character** | Does every major character's chain/arc show *in the prose* — sheets vs. pages? | arc tracker (PC-19 input) + interviews if dry | cast, per appearance |
| 3 | **Scene** | Does every scene's goal/conflict/outcome land — and the endings turn? | scene checklist (below), Ch 4 anatomy | scene by scene |
| 4 | **Line** | Do the sentences sound like me, tightened to the style sheet? | PC-21 (constrained) + your red pen | paragraph by paragraph |
| 5 | **Continuity** | Does anything contradict — facts, props, voices, rules? | PC-22 + bible-first batches | cross-document |

**Novels split passes by part** (structure pass on Part I before Part II is drafted — earlier detection, cheaper always); short stories and novellas run each pass whole-draft (the unit fits attention). Between passes: *apply kept changes → mint draft number → changelog line → bible/outline sync if facts moved → next pass's entry check*. That inter-pass ritual (Ch 7's mint extended) is what makes passes *reversible*: each draft number corresponds to "structure fixed," "character reconciled," etc. — so a line pass gone wrong restores one number, not one week.

**The per-pass checklists** — the chapter's reusable core (these five, verbatim, are what Appendix C cross-references; adapt wording to your project, keep the *questions*):

```text
PASS 1 — STRUCTURE (entry: draft-N complete; outline-v-current frozen)
[ ] Outline-vs-draft diff: every outline beat has prose — or an
    adjudicated outline change (two-rule stand, changelog'd)
[ ] Stakes sentence paid at climax (Ch 2's invoice — quote the line)
[ ] Midpoint map-change present IN THE PROSE (not just the outline)
[ ] Setup/payoff ledger closed (PC-11's columns, now vs. pages)
[ ] No scene/beat exists only because it was outlined (cut-list
    candidates flagged, not yet cut — cuts happen AT pass end)
[ ] PASS LOG written: findings n, kept k, draft-(N+1) minted
— exit: structure questions all answered YES or explicitly waived
        in the log (waivers are decisions rows, not silence)

PASS 2 — CHARACTER (entry: draft-(N+1); arc tracker filled vs. pages)
[ ] Flaw chain visible: three costed choices QUOTED from prose
[ ] False belief challenged before climax (tracker rows, no stalls
    past 3 chapters without adjudication)
[ ] Blind voice test run on current draft (PC-18 — conf ≥4 on leads)
[ ] Unearned-warmth audit run (Ch 11's — clusters adjudicated)
[ ] Interviews triggered only where sheet-vs-page disagreements
    remain (each answered detail consumed by a beat or filed raw)
[ ] PASS LOG + any sheet v-next changes changelog'd

PASS 3 — SCENE (entry: draft-(N+2); scene list extracted)
[ ] Every scene labeled G/C/O in one line (missing O = cut or fix
    queue — no third category)
[ ] Every scene's last line passes the "cover everything above"
    test (Ch 4's endings — no double-endings, no adverb exits)
[ ] Tell inventory: only the four healthy types (Ch 4's cookbook);
    any tell doubling as emotional climax → staged or moved
[ ] Sequel beats present or consciously cut (reaction not vanished)
[ ] Sequel/scene transitions have no unexplained time-jumps
[ ] PASS LOG + cut-list finalized (cuts executed NOW — into cuts/
    with dates — then mint)

PASS 4 — LINE (entry: draft-(N+3); style sheet CURRENT; banned lists
fresh — this is the only pass allowed inside sentences)
[ ] Style sheet sweep: banned constructions (search strings) — zero
    hits, or waived rows
[ ] Voice: read-aloud of 3 random pages — rewrites only where your
    ear stops (not where habit nags)
[ ] PC-21 run per chapter WITH constraints (length-retention + lock
    list + no-new-information rule — see card); diffs adjudicated
    line-by-line; accept rate logged (see done-ness below)
[ ] Generic-sensation sweep (the twelve "heart hammered"s — one
    channel per scene max)
[ ] PASS LOG + draft-(N+4) mint

PASS 5 — CONTINUITY (entry: draft-(N+4); outline/bible synced)
[ ] PC-22 full battery in bible-first batches (names, dates, ages,
    props/scars, weather, rules §5, never-say lists, POV/tense)
[ ] Arc tracker final rows (all chapters, no unadjudicated flags)
[ ] Cross-scene repetition: phrase-level duplicates within any
    3-chapter window — list and cut-all-but-one
[ ] Ending's planted objects present in prose (ledger from pass 1,
    now location-checked)
[ ] PASS LOG + draft-(N+5) = candidate DONE → done criteria (below)
    checked → beta gate (Ch 13) or one more full cycle if criteria
    unmet (cycle allowed ONCE — two failed cycles = F-07, stop and
    diagnose with Appendix C)
```

The checklists look long written out and take about ninety minutes each *in practice* — because most boxes are already checked by the system (the tracker filled at mints; the bible synced at mints; the style sheet maintained since Ch 14's template — the pass system *collects* the discipline the whole book has been installing, which is why it runs lean for readers who did the Project tiers and feels impossible for readers who didn't. If you're the latter: this chapter's honest advice is *go build the artifacts* (Ch 7's folder, Ch 3's sheets, Ch 14's sheet) — passes run on documents, not on intentions.

> TRY IT — Label your current draft: which pass would it *enter* right now, honestly? If the answer is "none — I tinker" (the hook's diagnosis), run **Pass 1's first three boxes only** on what exists — outline diff, stakes invoice, midpoint-in-prose. Fifteen minutes. Whatever those three findings are, you now have a *pass* instead of a mood; queue the rest with the entry conditions noted in your session log.

## AI in each pass

Three instruments, one per major pass — each card a *bounded deployment* of the same machine whose unbounded use created the v14 problem:

> PROMPT CARD PC-20 — Developmental reader (full pass)
>
> ```text
> 
> Role: developmental reader — FIRST READER, not editor: you
> experience the draft as a reader and report; you do not rewrite.
> Context: SKELETON + outline-current + story rules (§5) + length/
> structure target — pasted from the bible pack. Draft below (batch:
> [part/chapters — full scenes, never summaries]).
> Task: reader's report in this order, each finding location-quoted:
> 1. PROMISES: what does the draft seem to promise (premise, tone,
>    turn)? — quote the evidence lines.
> 2. PAY-OFF TRACK: where did your reading PULSE (lean-in) or
>    SAG (checked-out)? Give chapter/scene numbers + the exact
>    paragraph you noticed it. Sag = where you'd skim as a paying
>    reader — be specific about WHY (pressure / confusion /
>    familiarity).
> 3. CHARACTER READ: for each major character: what you believe
>    about them now + the line that created the belief. Flag any
>    belief that CONTRADICTS the pasted sheet (quote both sides).
> 4. ENDING DEBT: what does the draft's ending owe, given its
>    promises — and does the last quarter start paying?
> 5. BLIND SPOTS: three things a reviewer would raise that you
>    CANNOT evaluate from this batch alone (label: needs next
>    part / needs outline / genuinely unsure).
> Format: numbered sections, quotes required, damage implied by
> order (worst first within each section). NO praise section. NO
> rewritten prose. If a section has no findings: write "none found
> — checked: X."
> ```

PC-20's design talks back to everything Ch 5 taught: **first-reader framing** (experience, don't fix — "sag" is a *reading experience* report, and experience-reports are where the prediction engine's pattern-comprehension genuinely shines: it has "read" ten million pacing curves and can pattern-match your *felt* midsection against them — hedged always as *a reader's report, one reader, who cannot buy your book* — which is exactly the caveat Ch 13 will ram home about simulated feedback generally); **promises-vs-payoff** (grounds the report in *your* skeleton — else it grades against generic expectations and your deliberate kishōtenketsu *ten* reads as "underpowered midpoint" — the desk needs your aims or it audits the average book); **quotes everywhere** (every finding auditable in seconds — unlocated notes are vibes, vibes don't adjudicate); **blind-spot section** (the model *declaring its ignorance boundaries* — trained honesty, not assumed — findings it *can't* make are as valuable as ones it can, because they map your *beta* questions for Ch 13); **no praise section** (F-05's frame at full strength — the wind-down compliment is structurally banned, not just discouraged). Run it **once per structure pass**, fresh session, per-part for novels (batches = full prose, never your summaries — summaries audit the summary).

> PROMPT CARD PC-21 — Line editor (constrained)
>
> ```text
> 
> Role: line editor under STRICT constraints; your changes must be
> justifiable against my style sheet (pasted: banned list, rhythm
> preferences, voice notes) — pretty-for-its-own-sake is a failure.
> Chapter below. Rules:
> 1. TARGETS ONLY: filter words (just/really/quite/somehow),
>    redundant doubles (began to start), passive where agent known,
>    dialogue-tag adverbs, sentences >25 words WITHOUT deliberate
>    rhythm justification (list any you keep and why).
> 2. PRESERVE ABSOLUTELY: proper nouns & spellings, POV/distance,
>    tense, all lock-list constructions, every sentence in a
>    character's fingerprint speech, my unusual syntax if marked
>    [!mine].
> 3. NO NEW INFORMATION: you may delete and tighten — you may NOT
>    add images, metaphors, or intensifiers. Word count: ≤100% of
>    original (shrinking preferred).
> 4. OUTPUT: (a) diff-style list: ORIGINAL → EDITED + reason tag
>    from [filter|double|passive|tag-adverb|long], (b) then full
>    clean chapter, (c) counts: changes n, words before/after.
> I accept/reject per row; rejections are final.
> ```
`

The constraints *are* the safety system: **targets-only** (the machine's line instincts without targets = thesaurus-swap theater, Ch 14's enemy — bounded, it becomes a *mechanical sweep* of items your style sheet already names); **preserve-absolutely** (fingerprint speech and marked-my-syntax protected *by name* — voice anchors' written guarantee extended into the pass; the `[!mine]` marker habit — you tag your deliberate weird sentences *before* the pass, the editor's job starts where your permission ends); **no-new-information** (the deepest rule — unconstrained line editors *add* ("a lantern of hope flickered" — injected lyricism, F-01's cousin at sentence scale); forbid addition and the pass can only subtract and straighten, which is what *editing* means); **≤100% words + per-row reasons + your veto** (adjudication at the row level — the pass's log *is* the decisions file for a chapter, and "I accept/reject per row" stated *in the prompt* keeps the model's default toward wholesale acceptance from framing your own review: the prompt reminds *you* that rejection is the designed outcome for probably half its rows).

> PROMPT CARD PC-22 — Continuity checker
>
> ```text
> 
> Role: continuity auditor — bible-first, quotes-or-nothing.
> Context: BIBLE EXTRACT (names list verbatim + relevant §2 sheets +
> §3 timeline slice + §5 rules + never-say lists) pasted ABOVE the
> chapters — the extract is your ONLY source of truth for "correct."
> Chapters: [batch of 2–3 full chapters — never more on one desk].
> Battery, separate tables per category:
> A. CONTRADICTIONS: passage | bible fact | chapter — only where the
>    pasted extract covers the fact. If unsure whether bible covers
>    it: row marked "uncertain — needs bible check," NOT a guess.
> B. NAMES/SPELLINGS: every proper noun occurrence vs. names list
>    (typo-level precision).
> C. DATES/AGES/TIME: reconstruct the timeline slice from the
>    chapters; flag any sequence impossible under §3.
> D. PROPS/TRAITS: scars/handedness/objects-in-rooms vs. §2/§4 —
>    quote both sides.
> E. RULES §5: banned words/constructions/assurances — hit lists.
> F. POV/TENSE: interior-access sentences (every "felt/knew/
>    remembered") — list; flag any non-anchored.
> Output: six tables, quotes mandatory, no prose commentary, no
> suggestions. Uncertainty is a ROW TYPE, not a failure.
> ```

PC-22 is Chapter 5's type-split made executable — **internal claims checked against your documents, never the world** ("bible extract is your ONLY source of truth": this pass must *never* wander into "actually, February storms in that region…" — external verification is Ch 15's research discipline, separate pass, separate protocol); **batches of 2–3 chapters** (the shared desk's width — 40 chapters pasted = early chapters falling off *silently* = confident misses, exactly the amnesia Ch 5 warned; batching with the extract *re-pasted each batch* is the whole trick); **uncertainty-as-row-type** (the anti-hallucination feature *in the schema*: a model forbidden from guessing marks gaps instead — "needs bible check" rows go to *your* bible work, keeping the pass's precision high enough that you trust its hits — a checker with false-positive mush gets *skipped* next time, and a skipped checker is how v14's scar changed hands in chapter 9); **six tables = six searches** you'd run yourself if you had a month (names: near-mechanical; interior-access: the Ch 4 Oracle check at machine speed; never-says: exact-string) — the machine here isn't judging, it's *grep-ing with quotes*, which is its most trustworthy job in this entire book, and the reason continuity is the pass where AI assistance is *least* optional for long projects.

**Deployment cadence:** PC-20 once (structure pass); character tools from Ch 11 (character pass — already run *during* drafting, *verified* here); scene checklists hand-run (scene pass — the machine's first-reader pulse report from PC-20 already told you *which* scenes sag; the scene pass *fixes* them with your anatomy labels); PC-21 per chapter (line pass — bounded by chapter so row-level adjudication stays humanly reviewable — a whole-draft PC-21 dump is 400 unadjudicated rows, i.e., F-07 with extra steps); PC-22's battery in batches (continuity pass). Notice what *isn't* machine-run: the scene pass (your G/C/O labels — Ch 4's skill must stay sharp or atrophies; the machine *prepared* the pass with sag-locations, but the labeling reps are yours), the *acceptance* decisions in every other pass (row-veto stated in prompts, exercised by you), and the done criteria below (a machine cannot want your book to stop — only you can declare stop).

## Change logs and done-ness

**The change log** is the pass system's spine — one section per pass, rows *at decision time* (not reconstructed later; a pass's log written from memory is fan fiction). Format (extends the changelog.md from Ch 7 — *mints* at the file's top level, *pass rows* in `logs/pass-N-structure.md` etc., each row linking its mint):

```text
2026-09-26 · PASS 1 (structure) · draft-04 → 05
S1 | PC-20 sag @ ch4-5 (quote: "arranged the transport...") | KEPT |
    cause: want-gap mid-ch5 (tracker agreed) | fix: ch5 rebuilt on
    beat-level want (notary-hours micro-pursuit promoted from
    outline-v3 insert) | mint 05
S2 | PC-20 promises-read: "administrative never resolves cleanly"
    vs. skeleton ending "keys kept" | OVERRIDE-READER | my promise
    IS resolution-with-open-schedule — reader misread §?? → FLAG:
    ending needs one clearer image beat (queued scene pass, not
    structure) | no mint
S3 | stakes-invoice: climax quote MISSING the "Selvagem posting"
    proper noun (planted ch2, absent ch12) | KEPT | fix: restored
    in climax line | mint 05 (with S1)
[... every kept/rejected/waived — k kept of n found, stated]
```

Row anatomy, named because the names matter in adjudication: **ID | finding-with-quote | verdict (KEPT / REJECTED / OVERRIDE-* / WAIVED / DEFERRED-to-pass-X) | one-line reason | action + mint-link.** The verdict vocabulary *forces the taxonomy* the book's been building: **OVERRIDE-READER** (above: the machine's developmental read *disagreed with the skeleton* — you sided with your aims; the row preserves that the challenge *happened* without letting it win by default — the golden rule at developmental scale); **WAIVED** (finding valid, fix declined — "ch7 braid is slow *by design* (storm-foreshadowing needs the slowness)" — waivers prevent re-finding (the next pass's reader will sag there *again*; the waiver row *pre-answers* it — cited in the next prompt's context: "known waists: ch7 slowness — WAIVED pass-1 row S4, don't re-flag")); **DEFERRED-to-pass-X** (the noted-typo rule institutionalized: altitude discipline *with* memory); **REJECTED-with-mechanism** ("suggests adding flashback — rejected: no backstory paragraphs rule §5 + Ch 3 dosing schedule" — rejections cite *rules*, not moods, which both disciplines *your* taste and documents authorship (Ch 15's evidence class, again)). Every pass's log ends with the **k-of-n line** and a **density note** ("structure: 14 found, 9 kept — high keep-rate on want-gaps, low on tone notes (reader over-indexed style at structure altitude — noted for next time PC-20 context: add 'structure altitude — defer style'") — density patterns tune the *prompts* (v2 bumps) and tune *you* (what you keep at scale is your actual editorial priority list, revealed in numbers instead of self-story).

**Done-ness: written criteria, checked like a checklist.** The v14 disease is *vibe-done* ("feels almost there… maybe one more pass") — the cure is a criteria block you write **before Pass 1** (not after, when every criterion would morph to fit fatigue), stored with the project (`done-criteria.md`), and *checked only at Pass 5's exit*:

```text
DONE CRITERIES — "Low Tide" (written pre-pass-1, 2026-09-26)
1. All five pass logs complete, cycle count ≤ 1 full cycle after
   betas (second cycle = STOP → F-07 diagnosis).
2. k-of-n keep-rate across last two passes < 30% (convergence
   signal — the machine and I are mostly agreeing to leave it
   alone).
3. Style sheet: zero banned-list hits in two consecutive full
   read-throughs.
4. Blind voice: I can identify my-vs-machine paragraphs at ≥80%
   on a shuffled 10-paragraph sample — and the four I miss I can
   DEFEND (not just tolerate).
5. Stakes invoice + payoff ledger + arc tracker: zero open flags.
6. One cold full read (≥48h from last edit): no note surprises
   me that I hadn't already seen in a pass log — the draft has
   stopped teaching me new structural things.
7. External gate: Ch 13 beta round returned + triage applied +
   feedback-to-changes table rows all resolved (waived rows cited
   vision).
— Then: DONE. Not "done enough." The document says DONE, or the
  cycle runs again with a named failing criterion.
```

Read criteria 2 and 6 slowly — they're the load-bearing pair. **Keep-rate <30%** (operationalized: when your adjudications mostly say "leave it," the work is *finished being found* — divergence of a converging system; above 50% across two passes = still moving = keep working or F-07 if *the same findings recur* — recurring-findings ≠ productive-findings, the circle's distinguishing feature); **cold-read with no surprises** (the experiential proof — passes are analyses, the cold read is *synthesis*: a draft that still ambushes you structurally has pass-gaps; a draft that merely *bored* you at 60% of its length has a criterion-2 problem, not a quality problem — boredom-without-new-notes = normal, novels stop being new before they stop being *done*). And criterion 4's **defend-not-just-tolerate**: the done standard isn't machine-free prose (Ch 1 never promised that) — it's *ownable* prose: every survivor, either yours or yours-by-adjudication, able to answer "why does this sentence exist?" with *your* sentence. That's authorship as the done-state's definition — which is why the criteria file, like everything else in this book, ends up being less about the machine than about the name on the cover.

The circle stops here: **passes with entry/exit conditions, instruments bounded by cards, adjudications in rows with verdict vocabulary, mints between passes, criteria written before starting and checked at the end.** v14 dies today; `draft-05` with five logs is born. What remains is the gate: whether *your* five logs and *your* criteria are enough — or whether the draft needs readers who *aren't* you or your assistant. That gate has a name and a chapter: feedback, betas, and the triage that turns opinions into tables. Chapter 13.

### The passes, by altitude: what each one may and may not touch

A single reference table ending the "which pass am I in?" ambiguity that produces most F-07 entries — printed here *and* meant to live in the style sheet's margin during revision season:

```text
PASS 1 — STRUCTURE   may touch: beats, order, cuts, joins,
        stakes-lines, POV-per-scene DECISIONS
  may NOT: rephrase paragraphs wholesale (note candidates →
        pass 4 queue), optimize sentences, "fix while here"
  finding species: promises/causality/sags-with-cause/holes
  session shape: reader's posture (PC-20), full scenes, cold
        start, batch = part
  exit: outline amended (v-next minted) OR draft-mint planned
        with structure-rows adjudicated; waivers written

PASS 2 — CHARACTER   may touch: sheet edits (v-bump), speech
        repairs flagged for their scenes, arc-row corrections
  may NOT: redesign structure "while in there," merge passes
        with 3's intimacy work
  finding species: flatness-on-page, unearned warmth, arc-
        prepayment, swap-violations, cast-contract breaches
  session shape: PC-19 tables + hand rewrites of swap rows;
        blind tests at session close
  exit: sheets current vs. prose; change rows adjudicated

PASS 3 — SCENE       may touch: within-scene beat order, entry/
        exit turns, goal-visibility, interiority rungs, scene-
        contract restatements
  may NOT: promote a scene's events into structure-changes
        (route those up: "scene can't fix this — needs pass 1"
        is a LEGITIMATE finding-class, logged as such), touch
        line-polish queue items
  finding species: missing scene-turn, tell-not-stage, distance
        slips (schedule-vs-drift adjudicated HERE), contract
        un-honored
  session shape: 30-minute-per-scene reads with the rung
        schedule in margin; rewrite-mode for repairs
  exit: scene contracts all stated or deliberately broken-with-
        note; rung schedules filled for repair scenes

PASS 4 — LINE        may touch: every sentence-level item — the
        ONLY altitude with sentence rights (PC-21 rows, PC-26
        families, tag/description/tag sweeps)
  may NOT: remove a sentence for STRUCTURAL reasons (its
        structure job was settled in pass 1 — "this paragraph
        is redundant" needs its redundancy-*claim* checked
        against pass-1's ledger first — redundancy is often
        load-bearing repetition wearing a t-shirt; deletion
        authority requires the altitude that owns the load)
  finding species: filter words, doubles, tax-lines, hedge-
        creep, rhythm-band, banned-list hits
  session shape: row-cap batches, style sheet OPEN, [!mine]
        markers set, adjudication per row (rejections final)
  exit: ban counts to sheet thresholds; diagnostic re-run
        logged; count-diff recorded (≤ previous, normally)

PASS 5 — CONTINUITY  may touch: factual/consistency corrections
        ONLY (spellings, dates, props, POV-access violations,
        never-say hits) — the most conservative altitude:
        every fix cites bible-or-sheet as its authority
  may NOT: any change requiring taste (taste-exercises belong
        to whoever's pass owns them — pass 5 catching "I don't
        love this line" must QUEUE it, not act: the altitude
        discipline's purest test: *the lowest pass has the
        smallest*rights*, and remembering that under a
        line-beauty temptation is the whole skill)
  finding species: contradiction rows, name drift, timeline
        breaks, oracle-violations, banned-construction hits
        (routed if taste-flavored)
  session shape: PC-22 batches of 2–3, six tables, quotes-or-
        nothing, uncertainty-as-row-type
  exit: all tables adjudicated; zero open flags; done-criteria
        FINAL CHECK (the only pass allowed to read the criteria
        document as a whole — its checklist's last box)
```

The table's *vertical* lesson (why it's a ladder, not a menu): **each pass inherits its predecessors' adjudicated state as *settled input*** — pass 4's "redundant paragraph" claim checks against pass-1's ledger *because pass 1 already decided what that paragraph is FOR*; pass 5's spellings check runs against a *sheet that pass 4 just bumped* — **skip a pass and its absence isn't felt as a missing activity, it's felt as *authority-with-no-ground*** (findings at higher altitudes referencing decisions that were never made — the "who said this paragraph was load-bearing?" question with no document to answer — which is why the sequence's entry conditions list *prior pass's exit* as a *precondition*: the ladder isn't tradition, it's *dependency-management*: decisions at altitude N create the *facts* that altitude N+1 audits, and reversing the order produces the signature F-07 pathology: line-polish energy spent *stabilizing* prose whose structure is still mobile, sentences lovingly tuned *for paragraphs that pass 1 will later cut* — the saddest waste in revision, loving work *on the way to the graveyard*, entirely avoidable by reading this table's may/may-not columns *before* the session instead of discovering them mid-grief.)

### When the whole book stops: done-criteria at manuscript scale

The done-criteria block from Ch 12's core (per-pass thresholds) needs its *book-level* companion — the completion contract for the entire manuscript, signed when pass 5's last table clears — because "finished" without it is a *feeling* (and F-07's final boss is exactly that feeling's unreliability: writers declaring "done" from a good Tuesday, others *never* declaring it from accumulated perfectionism — the same ambiguity, two costumes, one instrument dissolving both):

```text
MANUSCRIPT DONE-CRITERIA — [title] — signed [date] — v1
(all boxes binary; all evidence-linked; checked ONLY at pass-5
exit — mid-pass checking of this document is F-07 summoning)

GATE A — PASSES COMPLETE
[ ] Pass 1 exit met (outline-v-final minted, structure-rows
    adjudicated — changelog line exists)
[ ] Pass 2 exit met (sheets current-vs-prose; arc tables zero
    prepayment flags)
[ ] Pass 3 exit met (scene contracts stated; rung schedules
    complete for all interiority-heavy scenes)
[ ] Pass 4 exit met (ban counts ≤ sheet thresholds; PC-26
    families ≤ threshold; diagnostic row filed)
[ ] Pass 5 exit met (six PC-22 tables adjudicated; zero open
    flags — the ZERO is the gate, "mostly clear" fails it)

GATE B — RATES & READS
[ ] Final-pass keep-rate ≤ 30% (convergence statement — the
    number from the chapter, still the number)
[ ] Cold read completed by someone outside the project (beta
    round ≥1; their rank-1 findings all triaged — verdicts
    recorded, even when OVERRIDE — *unverdicted* = fail)
[ ] Shuffle test passed (6 machine-sample + 6 self-sample
    paras: ≥4/6 blind-test correct on BOTH sides — ownability
    both directions, per Ch 14)

GATE C — INTEGRITY
[ ] Zero TODO/FIXME/placeholder strings in manuscript (grep
    evidence attached — the build's scan, run early)
[ ] Claim rows current for every printed factual claim (register
    status-column: verified-with-date or prose-hedged-to-row —
    no `stale` rows behind live sentences)
[ ] Provenance: AI-use log complete for the draft's final
    version (Appendix F-style rows exist for this manuscript;
    marking discipline: machine-origin clusters audited, adopted
    or rewritten — evidence line present)
[ ] Canon: Example-/running-facts sheet consistent (continuity
    pass 5's tables attached as evidence)

GATE D — THE DOCUMENT SAYS SO
[ ] This criteria block: all boxes checked, evidence links live,
    signed with date and version
[ ] Changelog: the mint that completes Gate D titled
    "FINAL — [title] v-final" (the last numbered integer
    forever — forward-only law honored to the end)
[ ] A next-step line written for the FIRST time in the
    project's life that is NOT about this manuscript
    ("send to [agent/venue/production]" — the document's own
    ceremony: a done-criteria block whose final field points
    *outside the project* has, by construction, ended it)

SIGNATURE: ______________ date: ________
(Yes — you sign a document. The ceremony is the point: every
"when is it finished?" anxiety this book's readers have raised
in workshops resolves to *the same place* — a signed page or
the absence of one — and the absence was never ambiguity about
craft; it was *avoidance of the moment the page could be
signed* — which is why Gate D's next-step field exists: finishing
isn't a verdict about quality — done-criteria *settled* that
question the moment they were written — finishing is a
*transition* — the project's last act being the *handoff act*,
scheduled in a field, signed in ink, exactly once.)
```

The manuscript-scale block's relationship to its per-pass parent deserves one more sentence: **the per-pass block keeps you from polishing mud; the manuscript block keeps you from *un-recognizing a house you've finished building*** — both are the same instrument at different durations (three-week threshold-checks versus the project's whole life), and both exist because the alternative — remembering your own standards from *mood-memory* — has been demonstrated, across this book's entire revision part, to reliably produce *both* failure species: the too-early "done" (vibes signed) and the never-early-enough (perfectionism unsigned). Paper doesn't have moods. Sign the page.

## Worked example: Example A's revision passes

The system on the running example — **Pass 1 in full transcript** (the altitude where most first cycles *actually* fail), **Passes 2–5 as log summaries** (the shape every subsequent pass takes once the artifacts exist).

**Pass 1 (structure), entry: draft-04 (outline-v3 current, tracker live, done-criteria written).** PC-20 context pack assembled (skeleton + outline-v3 + §5 + "structure altitude — defer style" note from pass-1 v2 of the *prompt* — the density-note loop already in effect); batch: full draft in two halves (two fresh sessions, extract re-pasted — desk discipline); findings (abridged, worst-first):

> 1. SAG ch7–8, quote: "the days passed in small repairs…" — reader-skim zone, WHY: protagonist *receiving* information (Aurelia-tells, Theo-tells) across two chapters — want invisibility (matches tracker stall flag ch7: P/R/E = — — —). [damage: high]
> 2. PROMISE-READ: draft promises "administrative never resolves cleanly" (evidence: Casi's ch2 paperwork speech ×3 return-mentions) vs. skeleton ending "keys kept + return date" — PAY-OFF TRACK: ch11's contract feels *solved* by reading (reader expected one more bureaucratic twist per promise-density). [damage: med — adjudicated below]
> 3. CHARACTER-READ: "Theo: believes he's *maintaining a person* not a building — line: 'kept the runner working' " — CONTRADICTS sheet field 6 dosing (says wound-dose = funeral fortnight *absent* until fight beat — but prose *implies* the maintaining-as-grief reading in ch3 already). [damage: med]
> 4. ENDING DEBT: ending owes the *posting* its named weight (stakes invoice) + Theo's choice *visible*; current last page pays schedule only — debt partially open. [damage: high]
> 5. BLIND SPOTS: needs next part n/a (complete story); needs outline for journal's contents ambiguity (§5 rule vs. three quoted entries in ch10 — bible-check row); genuinely unsure: storm-timing plausibility (label: external — research/ queue, NOT this pass). [...]

**Adjudication (rows, real):**

```text
S1 | sag ch7-8 want-gap (quote) + tracker stall corroborates | KEPT |
    fix: ch7 rebuilt — Mara's *inventory becomes pursuit* (she
    inventories TO find the notary-hours gap — her want visible in
    the labor itself); ch8 Aurelia-exposition converted to
    *deliver-with-cost* (Aurelia names her own price: the
    neighbor's-version of December, not the family's) | mint 05
S2 | promise-density → extra bureaucratic twist at ch11 | REJECTED |
    the promise is *tone*, not plot-contract (reader over-read
    density as setup — ONE honest twist expectation). BUT the
    finding's root kept-in-part: ch2's paperwork speech trimmed
    from 3 mentions to 1 (promise-density was MY excess) | mint 05
S3 | Theo-implication vs. dosing schedule | DEFERRED-pass-2 |
    character-pass owns wound-timing adjudication (interview Q
```
