```text
    queued: "runner meaning — ch3 vs fight beat") — noted, not
    fixed at structure altitude | no mint
S4 | ending owes posting-name + Theo-visible | KEPT |
    ch12 climax-line restored proper noun (same fix as pre-pass
    row earlier — now invoice-verified in prose) + final scene
    gets Theo's doorway-position line (from outline v3's earned
    entrance — was outlined, never drafted) | mint 05
S5a | journal entries: bible §5 says "two max" — draft has 3 |
    KEPT (bible-first): cut entry-2's middle stanza | mint 05
S5b | storm-timing | EXTERNAL — research/ row, not a pass finding
    (type-split honored: this pass checks pages vs. bible, not
    meteorology) | no mint
— k of n: 5 kept/handled of 6 core findings (1 deferred, 1 split,
  1 externalized); density note: reader-notes at correct altitude
  after prompt-v2 (zero style findings — prompt bump worked).
MINT: draft-05 · changelog line · outline: no change (S2 trimmed
prose-side only — outline untouched, two-rule stand satisfied).
```

*Commentary on the loop, five lines:* the **corroboration pattern** (S1's finding *matched* the tracker's pre-existing stall flag — independent instruments agreeing = highest-confidence fix class; when reader-report and tracker fight, usually trust tracker (documents over impressions); that precedence rule is itself a decisions-file line for future cycles); the **reader-rejected-but-root-kept** move (S2: the twist declined, the *density* that invited the misread accepted as self-diagnosis — override rows should still *excavate* their own contribution to the confusion — pure rejections waste reader learning); **altitude discipline verified** (S3 deferred with a *named* pass and queued interview — the noted-typo pattern at character scale; S5b's externalization *is* Ch 5's type-split under fire — the model offered meteorology-adjacent uncertainty; the schema routed it out); **one mint, four fixes** (S1+S2+S4+S5a consolidated into draft-05 — passes mint *consolidated states*, not per-finding snapshots — version hygiene holding); and the **prompt-tunes-itself density note** (style-finding count dropped to zero after the altitude-bump — the cards' v2 discipline eating its own dogfood — every pass system's quiet compounding effect: the *instrument* gets sharper than the operator by iteration 3, while every *decision* stayed yours in rows).

**Passes 2–5 — log summaries only** (the artifacts exist now; the form compresses to what your logs will look like):

- **Pass 2 (character):** n=7 findings — S3's deferred wound-timing *resolved via interview* (Theo: runner = "keeping the lights on for someone coming back" → dosing schedule *amended deliberately* (sheet v2, changelog'd: implication moved *earlier* is truer to prose evidence than schedule was — the system bending backward from pages *on the record*); warmth-audit: 3 clusters (Sunday-smiles confirmed again — drafting-calendar signal now *expected*, pre-flagged in prompt context); blind test: 6/8 exchanges conf 5, 2 swap-violations *in Casi's voice* (speech-rate flattened — the buyer's fingerprint wasn't in the bible yet: fingerprint added §2, lines rewritten by hand) | k=6/n=7, mint 06.
- **Pass 3 (scene):** n=9 — one scene label-less (cut-list executed: the ferry-ride recap *dies into cuts/* with date — the Ch 4 "Before"-material finally filed where it belonged); two double-endings (the classic deleted-explanation pair); three healthy-tells confirmed, one misfiled (backstory-dump dressed as narrative-voice tell → staged flashback *refused* per §5, converted to *one line of status-tell* + the fight beat carries the rest); sequel-beats: ch10's reaction *was* present (journal-reading had its dilemma — good news logged too: pass logs record *confirmations*, not just hits — otherwise every cycle feels equally broken) | k=8/n=9, cuts executed, mint 07.
- **Pass 4 (line):** PC-21 per chapter, row adjudications: acceptance rates per chapter logged (ch1: 41%, ch7: 63% — ch7's *dictated* chapter needed *more* machine-tightening than co-drafted ch1 — mode-log cross-reference pays: your dictation needs the line pass more; your co-drafts need it *differently* — noted in the mode table's notes column); banned-list sweep: zero hits *after* the two flagged-repairs; `[!mine]` markers: 12 present, 11 preserved by the card, 1 *added-back* after a misfire (the card's preserve-rule caught by its own diff display — adjudication visible) | k-rate on rows: 57% accepted (above criterion-2's ceiling → *not done yet* — convergence reading applied HONESTLY: keep going, and the density note shows *what* remains: mostly ch7's dictation-legacy filters — concentrated work, next cycle) | mint 08.
- **Pass 5 (continuity):** PC-22 battery in 3 batches: A=2 real contradictions (scar laterality slipped in ch9's machine paragraph — the *F-06 internal cousin* caught exactly where Ch 5 predicted: machine-draft regions; eye-color in a Theo POV-adjacent Oracle-violation from ch3's dictation — the Ch 4 audits finding their backlog), B=1 name-typo (Conservera Alvor ×1), C=timeline: 1 impossible sequence (storm-day miscount — fixed against §3, not against "feel"), D=4 prop rows (1 uncertain → bible-check → *bible was wrong* (the system's designed humility: documents can err *against pages* — bible §4 versioned, changelog notes "bible corrected from prose — reverse-sync direction used deliberately"), E=0 banned (style sheet current — line pass did its job), F=3 interior-access flags (2 non-anchored → Oracle-fixes by hand, 1 anchored-OK) | k=8/n=10 core + 1 bible-amended, mint 09 = **candidate done → criteria check: 1–3 ✓, 5 ✓, 4 (cold-read shuffle test) scheduled, 6 needs 48h, 7 beta-gate = Chapter 13's job** — the pass system's exit *pointing forward*, not asserting completion. | 

That last bracket is the whole chapter's shape, embodied: **five passes, five logs, five mints, zero circles** — and the honest remaining work (a cold read, a shuffle test, a gate) named as *remaining* instead of wished away. Your project's version starts with Try's three boxes and proceeds exactly this way: instruments, rows, mints, criteria, gate. The machine did *reading, sweeping, and quoting* — at every pass, in bounded cards, on desks you built — while every verdict that shaped draft-09 carried your row-ID, your reason, your name.

## Exercises

- **Try (15 min):** Run PC-22's battery on *two chapters* you already have (bible extract re-pasted above them — if your bible's thin, the names list + one sheet + §5 rules is enough for categories B/E/F at minimum). Count rows: real hits / uncertain / false alarms. Write three lines: which category earned the pass's cost (for most: B + F alone — the typo and Oracle sweeps); which bible section was too thin to audit against (the pass *just audited your bible's completeness* — add the section); whether any *machine-region* paragraph concentrated the hits (the F-06-internal signature — your marking discipline's report card).

- **Stretch (1–2 h):** Complete **one full pass** end-to-end on your real draft — pick by Try's result (thin bible → build §4/§3 first, then continuity; sag-suspicions → structure with PC-20): entry check run, instrument deployed fresh-session with bible pack, *every finding adjudicated in rows* (verdict vocabulary enforced — at least one REJECTED-with-mechanism and one DEFERRED are statistically likely; if everything's KEPT, your frame's too agreeable — re-run with the altitude/sharper context and note the k-of-n delta), kept-fixes applied *by hand* (or explicitly delegated with locks — passes don't outsource verdicts, but fixes may labor-share), k-of-n line written, mint + changelog, exit checklist boxes ticked honestly. Budget: ~30 min context-pack + finding, ~45 min adjudication+fixes, ~15 min logs+mint. The log file you produce *is* the chapter's deliverable — not the fixes.

- **Project (ongoing):** All five passes on your draft, *in order*, with logs — plus `done-criteria.md` written **before** Pass 1 (criteria drafted now, at your draft's *current* state — sevenish lines, your values, signed and dated — you may *re-negotiate* them later only via a changelog'd "criteria v2" row with one-line reason, same discipline as outlines: criteria serve the book, but renegotiation must be *visible*, or they serve fatigue). The gate after Pass 5: Chapter 13 — recruit betas with your PC-23 questions *derived from your open pass-flags* (the blind-spot section of PC-20 already drafted half your beta questions — note the pipeline: passes *produce* the feedback round's agenda). One full cycle (all five passes) between beta rounds max — cycles without new human input past that are F-07 territory by definition.

## Pitfalls

> PITFALL — **Non-converging loops (F-07).** *Symptom:* `draft-14`-style naming; revisions that re-fix what earlier passes fixed (the sister-line/midpoint/confrontation circle from the hook — each fix breaking what the last one held); keep-rates that *don't decline* across passes; the tell: **the same finding reappears after being fixed** (new findings = work remaining; *recurring* findings = the loop itself is the defect); emotionally: revision feels like *whack-a-mole with your own standards* — standards you can't state because they were never written as criteria. *Why it happens:* no pass system (every session is all-altitudes — structure doubts prompt line-tinkering which breaks scenes which *re-raise* structure doubts — the circle's physics: *simultaneous optimization of coupled variables without ordering*, the exact thing "one pass, one job, in order" exists to prevent); done-by-vibe (no pre-written criteria → the finish line *moves* with mood and fatigue → "one more Tuesday" forever — and the machine makes this *worse* unbounded: PC-21-style rewrites available on demand mean the supply of possible edits *never reaches zero* — convergence isn't waiting for suggestions to stop, it's your *accept-rate* crossing your *written* threshold); adjudication abdication at scale (400 unadjudicated PC-21 rows = decisions made *by default acceptance* — the golden rule's mass-violation, which feels like nothing until draft-14); and **fix-echo** — changes made without changelog rows *can't be recognized as already-done* by next pass's instruments (the sister-line gets "fixed" thrice because fix #1 left no row for #3's reader to see — logs aren't bookkeeping, they're the loop's *memory*). *Fix (in diagnostic order — name which of the four you have):* (1) **recurring-findings signature** → you're between passes illegally: restore the current pass's entry condition, finish it *fully* (log + k-of-n + mint), and paste *prior pass logs' WAIVED/kept rows* into the next pass's context (the instrument now *sees* what's done — the waiver-citation protocol, doubled); (2) **keep-rate >50% across two passes** → you're not converging *or* looping — you're **under-revising the early passes** (kept findings keep appearing = structure/character still moving): *re-run pass 1* deliberately (cycles allowed once per beta round) rather than grinding altitude-5 line passes on a moving target; (3) **vibe-done** → criteria file: write it *today* pre-pass if possible, or freeze v1 and stop renegotiating; check ONLY at pass-5 exit; criterion 2's keep-rate threshold replaces the mood's vote; (4) **abdicated-scale** → chunk PC-21 by chapter with row-cap attention (if you can't adjudicate 40 rows in one sitting honestly, the batch was too big — smaller desks), "I accept/reject per row" re-stated in the prompt, and the standing rule restated as arithmetic: *a pass with an acceptance rate you never reviewed is not a pass, it's a paste*. *Prevention* (the system's steady-state): entry/exit checklists (can't start pass 4 with a stale style sheet — the entry conditions *block* the anxious-skipping that starts circles), the inter-pass ritual (fix → mint → log → sync → *then* next altitude), criteria-before-pass-1 (done defined while you still have standards-energies, not during week six's fatigue), k-of-n density notes feeding *prompt v2 bumps* (instrument converges alongside you — the loop's quiet compounding), and one human gate per cycle (Ch 13 — new external information is the *only* legitimate circle-breaker when logs say you're converged but criterion 6's cold-read still surprises; surprises-without-pass-findings = the draft needs *readers*, not another pass).

Related failure for Appendix C: **F-07 (revision loops that never converge)** — full entry here (symptom: recurring findings + keep-rates not declining + vibe-done); cross-refs: F-03 (draft-vs-outline drift — pass 1's diff box), F-06 (external-vs-internal claims — S5b's type-split routing), F-01/F-04/F-09 (caught downstream in passes 4–5 + Ch 14 audits). Prevention stack identical: *ordered passes, bounded instruments, row-level adjudication, mints, criteria, one human gate.*

## Checklist

> CHECKLIST — You're ready to move on when:
> - [ ] All five passes complete **in order**, each with its log: k-of-n line present, verdict vocabulary used (at least the ones that apply — a cycle with zero REJECTED rows deserves a frame-review), entry/exit boxes honestly ticked.
> - [ ] Every machine suggestion is accounted for *in rows*: kept, rejected-with-mechanism, waived-with-reason, deferred-to-named-pass, or externalized-to-research — no orphans, no default-accepts; acceptance-rate columns reviewed for their own patterns (mode cross-refs noted).
> - [ ] Change logs made every pass's fixes reversible: draft numbers minted *at pass exits only*, changelog lines link rows, one reverse-direction sync executed at least once (bible-corrected-from-prose — the humility path used, not assumed).
> - [ ] Done criteria met — or *named as failing*: `done-criteria.md` checked at pass-5 exit with ✓/✗ per line; any ✗ = either one more full cycle (once max) or F-07 diagnosis per the pitfall's four signatures.
> - [ ] Beta gate prepared: PC-20's blind-spots + your open flags *compiled into draft beta questions* (Chapter 13's raw material exists in your logs — the pipeline to feedback is primed, not improvised).

## Key takeaways

- **One pass, one job, in order** — structure → character → scene → line → continuity — each with entry/exit conditions, a checklist, and a mint *after* kept-fixes apply: cheapest corrections first, paid polishing never applied to soon-to-be-cut material, altitude changes on schedule instead of by anxiety.
- **Three bounded instruments:** PC-20 as *first reader* (promises, sags with quotes, blind spots — one reader who can't buy your book), PC-21 as *constrained sweeper* (targets-only, preserve-lists incl. `[!mine]`, **no new information**, row-level veto), PC-22 as *bible-first grep* (batches of 2–3 chapters, uncertainty-as-a-row-type, internal-claims-only — the most trustworthy machine job in the book).
- **Change logs are the loop's memory:** verdict vocabulary (KEPT / REJECTED-with-mechanism / WAIVED-citing-next-context / DEFERRED-named-pass / OVERRIDE-READER / EXTERNAL) makes decisions visible, prevents fix-echo, documents authorship (Ch 15 will call this evidence), and density notes tune the *instruments* alongside your taste.
- **Done is written before you start** — criteria file drafted pre-pass-1 (keep-rate thresholds, cold-read test, voice-ownability, zero open flags, beta gate), checked only at pass-5 exit, renegotiated only via visible version rows: vibe-done is the disease; arithmetic-and-a-cold-read are the cure.
- **F-07 has four signatures and four fixes** (recurring-findings → re-enter passes properly + waiver-citation; non-declining keep-rate → re-run pass 1, stop grinding pass 4; vibe-done → criteria freeze; abdicated-scale → chunk + row-cap). The pass system's steady state isn't perfection — it's a converging, *documented* curve that ends at a gate you built in Chapter 13's direction: human readers, one cycle at a time.


# Chapter 13 — Feedback, Beta Readers & Iteration

## Opening hook

Two beta readers finish your manuscript the same evening. Reader one texts: *"Loved it!! Couldn't put it down 😍"* — you feel nine seconds of warmth and zero information. Reader two texts: *"Not sure it worked for me — the middle dragged a bit, maybe the sister? Anyway, congrats!!"* — you feel six hours of spiraling and still zero *usable* information, because somewhere in "not sure" and "maybe" and "dragged a bit" are three different complaints, at least one of which might be about a chapter that doesn't exist in this draft.

These are the two useless feedbacks — **empty praise** and **unlocated unease** — and they are not reader failures. *You* produced them, by asking badly: "what did you think?" is a question that returns mood, not diagnosis, because mood is the only instrument an unbriefed reader knows how to hand you. A reader with three *specific* questions — "at any point did you skim? name the paragraph you noticed it" / "whose side were you on in the kitchen fight, and where did that shift?" / "what did you believe Mara wanted by the midpoint, and which line told you?" — returns something else entirely: *located testimony*, data you can triage, argue with, and convert into a revision plan with your name on it.

This chapter is the design of that asking. **Briefing humans well** (PC-23's question craft — who to ask, what to send, how to receive), **simulated perspectives as a pre-flight check** (PC-24 — with the caveats this book owes you in full: what an AI "reader" can and *cannot* tell you, stated twice), and **triage** (PC-25 plus the feedback-to-changes table — the instrument that turns contradictory opinions into an ordered revision plan instead of a popularity contest). Example A runs one full round — real-pattern feedback, real contradictions, table and all. By the end: your draft has *questions written for its betas*, one round triaged into rows, and your own stated position on what simulated feedback cannot know.

## Learning objectives

By the end of this chapter, you will be able to:

- [ ] Brief beta readers with questions that yield usable answers (PC-23).
- [ ] Use AI-simulated reader perspectives (PC-24) — knowing exactly what they can't do.
- [ ] Triage contradictory feedback into a revision plan (PC-25).
- [ ] Maintain a feedback-to-changes table and derive questions from your own pass logs.

## Briefing beta readers

**The question design principles** — five, in order of how much they change the quality of what comes back:

1. **Ask for *events in reading*, not *evaluations of the book*.** "Where did you skim?" not "was the pacing good?" — "which line told you what Mara wanted?" not "was her arc clear?" — testimony about *what happened to you* is checkable against the draft; evaluation is a mood with a review's vocabulary stapled on. The rule behind the rule: **every question must be answerable with a location + a memory** (a paragraph, a moment, a line). If the answer can't have an address, rewrite the question until it can — address-less questions are the "not sure… maybe the sister" species being invited in for dinner.

2. **Ask about the *reader's* behavior and belief, never about *your* craft.** Readers are reliable witnesses to themselves (what *they* did, felt, assumed, predicted) and unreliable critics of technique (they'll say "the dialogue was exposition-dumpy" — a craft verdict their evidence doesn't support — when the *real* data was "I skimmed Theo's second speech about the roof"). Design questions that harvest the witness domain: *what did you expect? when did you stop believing? what were you thinking during the slow part?* — then *you* do the craft translation, because you have the pass logs to translate *with* (PC-20's sag-locations meeting a reader's skim-confession: now two instruments triangulate the same paragraph).

3. **Cap the ask, rank the ask.** Ten questions maximum — a fifteen-question beta questionnaire returns *answers to questions 1–3 and vibes about 4–10* (respondents' attention is a budget; respect it or get mush). Within the ten: *ranked* ("if you could fix only ONE thing, what? — the single most valuable beta question ever designed, because forced ranking *cannot* hide behind politeness: whatever tops the list IS their top finding, full stop"). The ranked-one also calibrates everything else they say: if their #1 is something your pass logs already flagged, the human and your instruments agree (corroboration — the pattern Ch 12 taught you to treasure); if it's a surprise, the surprise gets top triage priority (new external information outranks internal consensus — the cycle-breaker rule).

4. **Brief on *context*, blind on *answers*.** Send: the read-before's (what stage this is — "draft post-pass-5, structure locked, line pass done — I want *reader* experience, not proofreading"; a one-paragraph story context only if the story needs it; length expectations + a *deadline* — even a soft one, because betas without dates are betas in the void). Send *without*: your anxieties ("I'm worried the middle sags") — the moment you name a fear, you *buy* a finding of that name from every agreeable reader in your stable (sycophancy's human cousin: you've given them a script); your preferred interpretation ("I *meant* the journal to feel ambiguous") — you'll get your interpretation back, laundered as theirs, and the round's entire information value dies. *What you want is their honest first architecture of your story — which only exists if you didn't help them build it.* Two audiences can be briefed differently (a craft-literate friend may take craft-worded questions; a "reader's reader" gets pure witness questions — matching the instrument to the person is good design, not bias).

5. **What to send, how to receive.** Send the *manuscript file* (not screenshots, not paste-in-chat walls), with a companion one-pager: the questions verbatim, the deadline, "wrong-and-specific beats right-and-vague every time," "please quote line numbers when you can," and explicit permission to be critical ("I will not be hurt by 'I stopped caring on page 40' — I will be hurt by silence"). Receive: in *writing* first (voice notes and calls are richer *after* you've got their written pass — people soften live, and you'll spend the call managing their feelings instead of harvesting data), and receive *gratitude separate from analysis* — thank them warmly in a reply that does NOT ask them to revise their opinions ("really appreciate it" — full stop; "appreciate it — were you *sure* about the sister note?" is how you accidentally negotiate findings into something comfortable). Keep their words *verbatim* in `feedback/round-1/` — the table's source layer, never paraphrased at intake (triage runs on quotes; paraphrase is already your interpretation contaminating the data).

> PROMPT CARD PC-23 — Beta-question designer
>
> ```text
> 
> Role: research-methods assistant for reader feedback — you design
> QUESTIONS, not feedback; nothing about the draft's quality.
> Context: my draft's OPEN FLAGS (pass logs excerpt: PC-20 blind
> spots, scene-pass fixes applied, tracker edge rows) + stage
> declaration (post-pass-5, structure locked) + beta profile
> (readers' reading habits — 1 sentence each).
> Task: draft a questionnaire of TEN questions in this architecture:
> Q1 ranked #1-fix ("if only ONE thing") | Q2 locate-skim ("any
> paragraph you sped through — name it") | Q3 belief-at-midpoint
> (what did you think the story was *about* + which line) | Q4
> prediction check (what did you expect the ending to do — did it)
> | Q5 character testimony (whose side / when it shifted + trigger)
> | Q6 trust moment (one place you stopped believing + the line) |
> Q7 experience-of-my-known-flags (2 questions max — phrased as
> witness questions NEVER as "did the middle sag?" — if my flag
> names the flaw, rephrase to address-free witness form) | Q8
> last-line residue (what image/thought did the book leave you
> with — exact words if you remember) | Q9 who-would-you-tell
> (one person this book is *for* — and why, one line) | Q10
> permission-to-destroy ("quote your harshest line — free
> rein, I asked for this").
> Rules: every question answerable with location+memory; zero craft
> jargon in wording (readers are witnesses, not editors); no
> question may name my anxiety or preferred reading; cap each at
> 25 words. Output: the ten questions, ready to paste into my
> one-pager — plus a 3-line "how to brief" note (stage, deadline,
> wrong-and-specific permission).
> ```

The card's architecture is *your* questions with its craft compressed: Q1's ranking (principle 3), the witness-form constraint on your own flags (principle 2's most important deployment — **your pass-log flags get converted into address-free witness questions at design time, not mid-anxiety later** — "did the middle sag?" would *buy* a yes from every gentle reader; "Q2: any paragraph you sped through — name it" *earns* the truth whether or not it touches your middle), Q6's trust-moment (belief-failure located is the developmental gold — one "I stopped believing at X" beats five star-ratings), Q8's residue (your ending's *actual* afterimage vs. the designed one — the gap is Ch 12's criterion-6 cousin, measured externally), Q10's *permission-to-destroy* (structural honesty-injection: people need explicit license to be harsh — the license itself raises finding-quality more than any rapport trick). What the card will *not* do: write the questions **without your open flags pasted** — questions designed against *nothing* return generic questions; your logs are the raw material, which is why Ch 12's exit criterion pointed here.

**Who to ask (the small stable).** Three to five readers, chosen by *instrument diversity* not affection rank: (a) **one hungry reader in your target audience** (buys your genre/literary niche, reads constantly — their Q4 predictions reflect *market-norm expectations*, invaluable for genre-fit); (b) **one craft-literate reader** (can *locate* their experience even if their craft vocabulary needs translation — their witness-answers come pre-annotated); (c) **one honest stranger-friend** (likes *you* too much — the sycophancy control; brief them extra-carefully, thank them extra-warmly, weigh their Q2/Q6/Q10 hardest precisely *because* agreement from them is cheap); optional (d) **one domain-expert** only if your draft makes load-bearing factual claims in their field (the Ch 5 type-split at human scale — they test *accuracy rows only*, not experience; don't ask a sailor "did it pace well," ask "would the dock scene's sequence of operations happen in this order"). What you do *not* need: ten readers, a writing group's full roster, or anyone you'll be unable to disagree with later (triage's vision-check is impossible against your thesis advisor — choose betas you can *overrule without dread*; the relationship cost of overruling some readers is a real design constraint on who gets to read).

## Simulated perspectives (with caveats)

Now the chapter's most careful section — PC-24, used honestly. **What the tool actually is:** an AI-simulated *reader persona* run against your pages: you instruct the model to respond *as* a specified reader-profile ("avid cozy-mystery reader, 58, hates on-page violence, reads 60 books a year") and report their *simulated* experience using the same witness-forms as your human questionnaire (skim-locations, belief-states, predictions, residue). **The legitimate use — a pre-flight check, only:** (1) **questionnaire rehearsal** — run PC-23's output *against a simulated target-reader* first: if the witness-forms confuse a simulation, they'll confuse humans; revise wording *before* sending (the cheapest possible pilot); (2) **blind-spot hunting on YOUR flags** — simulated readers are *very* willing to skim-report on command (no social cost), so "locate three skim-candidates in chapter 7" returns candidates you then *verify yourself against your pass logs and eyes* — the simulation's value is *proposal generation* (Ch 1's golden rule in feedback-clothes: it proposes, you verify); (3) **prediction-rehearsal** — "as this profile, what would you predict the ending does — now read only the outline's ending line — did the prediction survive?" (a fast way to find *mis-signposted* turns: if the simulated reader's prediction was never made *possible* by your pages, your signs are missing or wrong — then *you* re-read and decide — the check proposes, you adjudicate); (4) **coverage-gap questions** — "what would a reader in profile *notice that my ten questions didn't ask about*?" (questionnaire QA again — meta-questions, not draft verdicts).

**What it cannot do — stated plainly, because the outline demands hedging or cutting and this book hedges *once, hard,* then enforces:** simulated perspectives **cannot read**, in the human sense: they have no *stakes* in your characters (no remembered sister of their own surfacing at your family scene), no *boredom* (a simulated "I skimmed here" is *generated text about skimming* — pattern-completed from what skimming-reports look like, not *produced by attention failing*), no **taste-histories** (your target-reader's 60 books a year built *expectations in a nervous system*; the persona prompt builds *a paragraph of description* of such a person), and no **money, evening, or goodwill** at risk (the one resource honest readers spend — "I gave this my Tuesday night" — cannot be simulated because it's the *source* of testimony's authority). Consequently: simulated findings about *your draft's quality* carry **no independent evidential weight** — they are *distorted mirrors of your own prompt and the model's prior about readers like the persona*, and the model's prior is (Chapter 11's thesis, reader-edition) *agreeable-average*: simulated readers skew kind, skew toward *your implied preferences* (the persona you write is flattered by construction — "well-written literary pacing enthusiast" will *find* well-written literary pacing; you built the confirmation into the casting call), and skew *literacy-forward* (they "read" like fast, verbal, patient people — never like your actual least-verbal target reader). **One field note carries the whole warning:** every simulated finding worth acting on must pass *your* verification against pages and human logs — and if a simulated finding ever *contradicts* a human beta's, the human's testimony is evidence and the simulation's is *chat*; there is no scenario in this book where it reverses.

> ETHICS NOTE — Never *represent* simulated feedback as human in any shared context — not to your writing group ("my readers said…" when your readers were a prompt), not to an agent or publisher's questionnaire ("beta-tested with n readers" — the n *counts humans*), not even in your own session log (the Mode line says `simulated pre-flight`, never "beta"). The reason is Ch 15's seed: readers, collaborators, and venues are owed *accurate provenance* about who experienced the text — and a writing culture where "betas" quietly means "chat" burns the very testimony your craft depends on. Log them separately (`feedback/sim-preflight/`), count them separately, and let their folder name enforce the honesty you'll be tempted to blur at 11 p.m.

> REALITY CHECK — The deepest limit: simulated feedback **cannot surprise you with someone else's life.** Its surprises are *your prompt's* surprises (and the model's priors) — compressible back into things you already half-knew. Human betas surprise you with the sister *they* had, the father-death they skipped their own book tour for, the page where *their* marriage made them look away — irreducible data from outside your system, which is what feedback is *for*. That's the whole asymmetry in one line: **simulations stress-test your questions; humans stress-test your story.** Use PC-24 as the rehearsal room before the audition — never confuse the rehearsal with an audience.

> PROMPT CARD PC-24 — Simulated reader perspectives (with caveats)
>
> ```text
> 
> Role: you simulate a READER PERSONA responding to pages — you
> generate their simulated reading-experience report; you do NOT
> critique as yourself, and you attach no quality judgment of your
> own.
> Persona (I provide): [3–5 lines — reading life, tastes, irritants,
> what they DNF books for — written by me, from real people I know
> *types of*, never from a specific real person's private details
> (Ch 5/15 privacy rule)]
> Witness protocol (mirror of my human Qs): for each chapter-range:
> [1] speed-report: any stretch you'd read slower/faster — locate
> it | [2] belief-state: what you currently think is true + the
> line that did it | [3] prediction: what happens next + confidence
> 1–5 | [4] trust-check: any line you'd not believe IN THIS PERSONA
> + why | [5] residue: last image/thought after the range.
> Then META-3: (a) which of my ten human questions (pasted) would
> YOU answer vaguely as this persona — flag for redesign; (b) one
> witness-question my ten MISSED for this persona; (c) anything
> above where you had to INVENT rather than report from the pages
> — label it "invented" explicitly.
> Closing disclaimer line (mandatory, verbatim in your output):
> "Simulated testimony — no independent weight; verify against
> human reads."
> ```
`

Card design notes: the **persona written by you from *types*, not private people** (privacy rule threaded at design time — the persona's your neighbor? their *shape* generalized, their *details* not pasted — Ch 5's hygiene inside a character prompt); **[c]'s invent-label** (the card *forces the simulation to mark its own confabulations* — same self-audit humility as PC-17's verdict lines; imperfect, but the labeling habit catches the worst overreach); **the mandatory closing disclaimer** (output *ends with its own demotion* — every saved pre-flight file wears its epistemic status like a lab label, so future-you skimming `sim-preflight/` can't mistake it for testimony); **META-3(a) turned back on the questionnaire** (the tool's *strongest* honest use — piloting Q-wording — built into the card itself, so running the card *always* includes the legitimate use and can't drift into the illegitimate one of "simulated verdicts"). Run it **before** betas are briefed (rehearsal order: design Qs → pilot vs. persona → revise → send to humans), never *instead of* humans, never *after* humans to "check their work" (post-human simulations are how you start *weighting* chat against testimony — the reversal the REALITY CHECK forbids; if a human finding needs a second opinion, the second opinion is *another human*, or your pass logs, not the mirror).

## Triaging contradiction

Round one returns. Three readers, ten questions each, thirty answers — and they *conflict* (they always do: one says Chapter 7 is the heart, one says cut it; one read Mara as hero, one as villain-in-waiting — the latter two *thrilling* rather than problematic: **contradiction means your story has voltage** — readers wrestling the same text from different positions is the literature doing its job; unanimity, by contrast, is either a masterpiece or a questionnaire you designed too softly). The instrument that converts thirty answers into a plan:

**Step 1 — Intake verbatim, one row per finding.** `feedback/round-1/` files + the table born: every *located* finding gets a row: `ID | reader (R1/R2/R3) | question# | VERBATIM quote | location | type (experience/belief/prediction/accuracy/rank-1) | first-read`. Unaddressed-feeling answers ("loved it!!") enter as `PRAISE-ONLY` rows — kept for morale and *pattern-counting* (three praise-rows naming the *same* scene = that scene works; praise has data *if you tabulate it*), never for revision. Verbatim rule again: the quote is the evidence your later vision-check stands on; paraphrase at intake poisons every step downstream.

**Step 2 — Cluster by *location*, not by *theme*.** Tempting to group "pacing comments" together; instead group by **paragraph/scene address** — why: the same location accumulating *different complaints* ("dragged" + "beautiful" + "confused me") reveals the real phenomenon (a scene doing two jobs badly or one job controversially), while *theme-clusters* ("three readers mentioned pacing") launder the fact that they meant three *different* sections. Location-clustering is Ch 12's corroboration instinct (independent instruments on same address) at feedback scale: **same-address findings from different readers = highest-confidence rows in the entire round**; same-theme-different-address = separate problems wearing a shared word.

**Step 3 — The vision check (the anti-F-08 core).** Before *any* row becomes a change: hold it against your **stated vision** — the skeleton's stakes sentence, the done-criteria file, the outline's deliberate waivers, style-sheet bans — and assign one of four verdicts:

- **CHANGE** — finding agrees with vision + appears at an address (change is now *execution*: the row drafts its own fix note from the quote+address).
- **DISCUSS/DEFER** — plausible, vision-silent: queue for your own re-read *at that address only* (not a spiral — a located re-read with the row's quote in hand), verdict inside one week or it dies to `parked-feedback`.
- **OVERRIDE** — finding contradicts vision *by design* ("7th reader ever to say 'cut the slow chapter' — vision says the slow chapter *is* the book's argument; waiver from pass 1, cited"): **the row stays in the table with the override reason** — overruling *documented*, never deleted (kindness to future rounds: next beta asks again, the pre-written reason answers them in seconds).
- **FIX-THE-QUESTION** — finding reveals *their* misreading that your *signs* caused (reader thought Mara was villain-in-waiting because chapter 3's POV withheld *one* piece of information the design said they'd get — the finding is a *signposting defect*, not a character defect): verdict changes *what* changes (add the sign, not the trait). This fourth verdict exists because beginners default to the first three and **reader misreadings are overwhelmingly design data** — but the design being wrong and the *reader* being wrong are different diseases with opposite treatments; naming verdict four forces you to diagnose which before prescribing.

**The vision check's arithmetic:** CHANGE-rows sorted by *cluster size × address-criticality* (same-address-multi-reader + climax-adjacent = top), OVERRIDE-rows never re-litigated in-session (the reason cited is closed), and **no round ever reopens frozen structure without a Ch 9-style mint** (feedback proposes; the two-rule stand governs structure edits — beta-driven outline changes go *through* v-next + changelog like any discovery-draft disproof: "reader round 1 suggested X → adjudicated CHANGE at structure altitude → outline v4" — the table *feeds* passes; it doesn't *replace* their discipline).

**Step 4 — The feedback-to-changes table** (the chapter's namesake deliverable — the outline's promised artifact, now fully specified):

```text
FEEDBACK → CHANGES · round 1 · "Low Tide" · 2026-09-2X
| Row | Quote (verbatim) | Addr | Cluster | Verdict | Change planned            | Pass | Status |
|-----|------------------|------|---------|---------|---------------------------|------|--------|
| F01 | "I sped through the ferry bits" R2-Q2 | ch7 ¶2-4 | F01+F04 same addr | CHANGE | compress ch7 30% (dictation-legacy filters — aligns PC-21 note) | scene/line | scheduled pass 3' |
| F02 | "she's terrible and I rooted for Theo" R1-Q5 | ch1-6 arc | alone | FIX-THE-QUESTION | design *wanted* complex-not-villain — add ch5 sign (post-anger pause, sheet-consistent) | scene | drafted next mint |
| F03 | "cut the slowness" R2 rank-1 vs "the heart" R3-Q6 | ch7 | CONTRADICTION | OVERRIDE | vision: slowness is the argument — waiver pass-1 S4 cited; R3's *specific* resonance line noted as doing-what-waiver-intended ✓ | — | closed-with-reason |
| F04 | "middle dragged" R1-Q2 (unlocated) | ? | addressless | DISCUSS/DEFER | their Q2 was answered pre-locating — reply once: "which paragraph?" — verdict by address or dies in 7d | — | awaiting-address |
| P03 | "couldn't put it down" R3-Q?? | ch9 | praise-cluster ×2 | (CHANGE? no —) LOGGED | fight scene: two praise-rows same address = keep current design; freeze as positive-control | — | data |
| S01 | accuracy Q: "dock sequence impossible" (domain-expert R4) | ch8 dock | alone | CHANGE | reorder to actual lock cycle (research/ row: verify official procedure first — type-split) | continuity | research queued |
[... rows continue; k CHANGE / n DEFER / m OVERRIDE counts at foot + rank-1 comparison: R1-R2-R3 rank-1s = {ch7 compress, {unlocated}, ending residue-image} → top-table priority confirmed by 2-of-3 rank-1s agreeing on ch7 — the table's ordering *itself* auditable]
```

Note the table's species: **F01's row *cites its own corroborating instruments** (the PC-21 acceptance-rate spike in ch7 — mode-log cross-ref — *three* instruments now on one address: two humans + a pass statistic = act-now confidence); **F02's verdict *rewrote the problem*** (the change is a *sign*, cheap, sheet-consistent — verdict-four saving a reader's worth of character-surgery); **F03's override *kept the contradicting quote visible*** next to its waiver — future rounds inherit the argument *resolved* (and R3's row quietly *validates* the waiver's intent: the slow chapter *did* work for the reader it was built for — overrides love evidence like that); **F04's status column enforces the deadline** (addressless findings *expire* — the table has clocks, which is how triage paralysis (F-08) gets prevented structurally: paralysis is what happens when rows have no verdict-vocabulary and no expiry — thirty open vibes instead of a week's scheduled work); and **S01 routes accuracy to research-first** (external claim → official source *before* prose changes — the type-split as a *status value*: no dock prose edits until `research/` answers, because *changing fiction to match a wrong simile* is the failure mode nobody detects later). Two rounds maximum per draft-stage (round 1 pre-structure-pass changes; round 2 verifies them), and **every CHANGE row names its pass** — the table *feeds* the pass system (Ch 12's five passes, now with externally-sourced findings queued into their entry conditions) rather than inventing a sixth ad-hoc revision mode — which is exactly how "iterate with feedback" stops meaning "revise in a panic after every email."

> PROMPT CARD PC-25 — Feedback triager
>
> ```text
> 
> Role: triage clerk — you classify and cluster; you NEVER vote on
> vision (that's mine) and you never soften quotes.
> Context: my VISION PACK (stakes sentence, done-criteria headline,
> pass-1 waivers list, style bans — pasted) + intake rows (verbatim
> quotes, reader, Q#, address where answered).
> Task: (1) dedupe/cluster strictly BY ADDRESS (quote col shows
> overlaps); (2) tag each row: located/addressless · witness/verdict-
> form · corroborates-pass-log? (cite the log row if the pack
> contains one) · praise-only; (3) propose VERDICT CANDIDATE from
> {CHANGE, DISCUSS, OVERRIDE, FIX-THE-QUESTION} *against the vision
> pack* — candidate only, with the pack-line that supports it;
> (4) surface contradictions as PAIRS with both quotes side by
> side, never merged; (5) rank-1 findings listed first if my intake
> marks them. Output: table + "pack lines used" + NOTHING resembling
> a revision plan (changes are my drafting).
> ```

The card *cannot* close a row (candidates-only: verdicts are signings, your signature), *cannot* merge contradictions (pairs stay *pairs* — the vision-check's honesty dies if a clerk averages "the heart" and "cut it" into "consider chapter 7"), and *cites the pack-line* per candidate (your vision quoted back at you *next to* each recommendation — the golden rule's final form: the machine's proposal arrives **pre-referenced to your aims**, so accepting or overriding is a two-second lookup instead of a mood). The manual version (spreadsheet, no card) works fine at n=30 — the card's real gift is **rounds 2, 3, and the novel-length future** where intake rows hit three figures and clerical体力 (clerical体力 → clerical *labor*) is where tired triagers start *merging contradictions by accident*. Restate the standing order: **triage proposes, vision disposes, passes execute, table remembers** — four verbs, one owner.

### Sending verdicts back (and knowing when to stop writing to readers)

The intake table's *output-side* has one more discipline: **communication back to the humans who gave feedback** — a craft with its own failure modes, because the temptation runs in both directions (over-communicating every verdict = making your revision *a negotiation you didn't want*; ghosting every reader = the gratitude-debt rotting into avoidance — the chapter's grammar fixed both ends; here's the operational middle):

**The verdict-back templates, by verdict-class:** **CHANGE-rows** (you're taking a located finding): the reply's shape is *thank + the specific action + nothing else* — "Q2's skims at the marina paragraphs — restructuring that sequence this week; your paragraph-name saved me an hour" — note what's *absent*: the *details* of your fix (you owe readers *receipts*, not *methods* — every fix-explanation invites the follow-up opinion you're trying not to schedule: the moment a reader becomes a *consultant-on-your-fix*, F-08's door reopens with their face behind it — **the receipt closes the loop; the method reopens it** — gratitude must be *closing* gratitude: their row is *received, acted, done*); **DISCUSS-rows** (genuine ambiguity — the verdict's *question* goes back, one time, deadline-attached): "your 'Mara seems cold at the funeral' vs. two readers' 'warm — finally' — contradiction logged; before I adjudicate: *which line* read cold to you? (one paragraph's address is all I need — reply by Friday either way)" — the address-request doing the *witness-form* work retroactively (if they can't name a line, the row grades itself: mood-form, verdict *fix-the-question*, **no second request ever sent** — the one-addressless-finding-follow-up rule from the chapter, now at *reply-stage* discipline: *one follow-up per discussable, never two* — the clock on the second request is where writerly-politeness turns into readerly-*management*); **OVERRIDE-rows** (you're declining a located finding): *usually silent* — the override's record lives in *your* table (the chair's verdict, the pack's line), not in their inbox — the exception: **relationship-weighted overrides** (a swap-partner of three rounds, a beta who'll *rejoin* round 2 — for *invested* readers, one line of *aim-revision*, not defense: "keeping Mara's coldness at the funeral — it's load-bearing for her arc's negative-rung; flagging so round 2's questions can aim around it" — the stage-declaration's *update* (Ch 13's briefing, reissued to the *same seats*) — what makes it safe: it *re-points* their instrument rather than *arguing* with their testimony — "your read was accurate; the book *wanted* that read" is witness-affirmation with aim-correction attached, the only override-shape readers can hear as *information* instead of *dismissal*); **FIX-THE-QUESTION rows** (their Q10-was-actually-a-comment artifacts): *no reply needed* — their *deed* (volunteered a question their own reading didn't support) already did the work: it redesigned *your instrument*; writing back to explain would be process-ceremony aimed at someone who asked to be *read to*, not *processed with* — the row closes *internally*, the redesign ships in the *next* round's questionnaire (their unasked-for question, asked better by someone else's future draft — feedback's quietest completeness: **the loop that closes in your table, not in their thread.**

**The knowing-when-to-stop rule** (the craft's boundary, stated with the chapter's usual arithmetic): **every reader gets exactly *one* verdict-communication per *their* feedback-batch** — one reply-cycle (thank-you + any single discussable), *then silence until the relationship's next natural event* (their next round-invite, their own manuscript's swap-turn, an annual "it shipped" note) — the *one-cycle* law existing because the *second* communication is where all four failure-species live: **apology-revisits** (re-explaining an override to *soften* it = the override's verdict *being renegotiated by your discomfort* — discomfort is not a verdict-class), **fix-reporting** (weekly progress notes to betas = the revision *becoming performative* — the work's audience re-narrowing to *people who already told you what they think* — the manuscript's real next-audience waiting *outside* this loop entirely), **gratitude-compounding** (a third thank-you that's actually *guilt-maintenance* — the row closed twice because *you* can't sit with having received without paying — the debt-feeling's purest form: **readers gave you *data*; data was the payment; the thank-you was the receipt — a receipt does not compound**), and **instrument-blur** (reply-threads where Q&A *continues* past the batch = the reader now co-reading your *process*, their future input indistinguishable from *your* decisions' aftertaste — F-08's door *held open by chat-culture's defaults*: messaging apps *want* threads to continue; the triage table *needs* them to end — the tool-affordance fighting the craft-need, every time, which is why the one-cycle law gets *written into the briefing itself* (the sample note's shape: "anything under Q10 I'll triage; *no reply needed either way*" — **permission-to-silence granted at *entry*, so the loop's closure was *contracted*, not *imposed*** — the briefing granting the license the closing needs, the same move as Q10's destruction-permission: *briefings grant every permission the process will later require*, and this one grants the process's final permission: **you may stop writing to me now — the table has everything — thank you for reading; I'll tell you when it's a book.**

## Worked example: Example A's feedback round

The system end-to-end on your running story — intake through table, abridged where the card-section specified the format:

**Setup (the briefing done right):** three betas — R1 (target audience: literary-short voracious, our "hungry reader"), R2 (craft-literate: runs a critique group), R3 (honest-stranger: novelist-friend who owes no one agreement); R4 (domain-expert: retired harbor pilot — dock accuracy Q only, briefed *separately* with just S-type questions, explicit "not a reader-response round — accuracy rows only"). Questionnaire: PC-23's ten, designed against draft-09's open flags (the card's context pack = Ch 12's blind-spot section + tracker edges + the ch7 PC-21 spike — *three* flags hiding in Q2's neutral wording, per the design rule). Simulated pre-flight ran *first* (one persona: "avid literary-short reader, skims anything with explained feelings"): its Q4-flagged question ("Q5's 'whose side' wording — persona answered vaguely") → *reworded* before sending to humans ("at what moment did you know whose side you were on — and what told you"); three meta-missed witness-questions *considered*, one added (Q6-trust moved from pass-derived to persona-suggested pure-witness form). **The pre-flight's entire yield: two questionnaire edits** — rehearsal room used as rehearsal room; the sim folder says so on its face (disclaimer line intact).

**Round returns (day 9 — deadline held):** 3×10 human rows + R4's 2 accuracy rows = intake 32 (3 PRAISE-only, 2 addressless → F04-style follow-up → one *died unaddressed at day 16, logged as expired — the clock worked*); clustering by address produced the table above's real spine (ch7 cluster ×4 rows/different-complaints; arc-sign cluster; contradiction pair F03; S01 external). **The rank-1 comparison** — R1: "the middle's slowness *felt* thematically right but I'd trim the ferry logistics" (located-ish → ch7); R2: "compress the recap" (ch7 address); R3: "the ending residue image — did I need one more beat of Theo's face?" (climax-adjacent) — **2-of-3 rank-1s on ch7** → priority confirmed *by the readers' forced rankings alone*, before any of our instruments spoke (and then the instruments *agreed*: PC-21's 63% acceptance spike + PC-20's original sag-quote, both cited in-row — **four-instrument corroboration** (R1, R2, PC-20, PC-21-stat) on one address = the round's unambiguous top CHANGE, executed as F01's planned 30% compress in pass 3' alongside the dictation-legacy filter cuts the line-pass had already queued — *feedback, passes, and mode-logs converging on the same rows* = the whole system eating from one table).

**The contradictions adjudicated live:** F03's override closed with its waiver-citation *and* the R3 resonance-quote as evidence-of-intent; R3's separate ending-beat question → DISCUSS → re-read at address (the done-criteria's residue image vs. their residue report: their remembered image was *the keys in Theo's hand* (scene 11's) vs. the designed final *tide-table-on-the-wall* — diagnosis: design's last image was *thinner* than an earlier stronger one — **FIX-THE-QUESTION's cousin: the ending's own competition** → CHANGE: final scene gets the keys-image *held in frame a beat longer* + tide-table merges into it (one image inherits the other's voltage — vision-silent, tasteful, mint through line pass); **R1's "rooted for Theo" (F02) survived vision-check as sign-defect, not character rewrite** (sheet unchanged — ch5's added pause *did* the work: round 2 will measure whether verdict-four's fix *worked*, the table's rows feeding round 2's questions like clockwork); **R4's dock sequence → research-first status** honored: no prose touched until `research/` filed the official lock-cycle source (dated, primary per Appendix E), *then* the reorder lands in continuity pass — accuracy changing prose only via verified channels, the type-split's full loop visible in one row).

**Round-close ritual:** all rows statused (k CHANGE scheduled into passes / n DEFER with clocks / m OVERRIDE closed-with-reason / expired counted honestly); thank-yous sent *without* negotiating findings (the standing rule, now proven cheap: three warm messages, zero follow-up questions, R3's harshest-line reply met with pure gratitude); `feedback/round-1/` frozen (intake layer read-only from here); table versioned (`v1 · after-round-1 · feeds pass 3' and 2'`); session log block written with the finding that matters most: **"triage complete: no F-08 signature (zero unverdicted rows over 7 days) — the clock and the vocabulary did their jobs."** The round didn't finish the book; it *scheduled* the book's remaining work into the pass system with external evidence attached — and built round 2's agenda from its own verdict-four rows (did the ch5 sign land? did the ending's inherited image *stick* in a fresh reader's residue?). One round of good questions did what "what did you think?" never could: **produced testimony you could argue with, in writing, with your vision quoted back at every proposal** — readers *and* assistant kept in their proper, documented, non-authorship roles.

## Exercises

- **Try (15 min):** Write ten beta questions for your current draft with PC-23 — but *paste your actual open flags first* (the card fails without them: even hand-written, Q7's conversion of "my known sag" into witness-form is the card's most transferable trick: *flag → "any paragraph you sped through — name it"*). Self-audit: every question must survive the location+memory test; Q1 must force ranking; zero anxieties, zero preferred readings, one permission-to-destroy. Time-box at 15 — the drafting speed *is* the discipline (over-designed questionnaires get under-answered).

- **Stretch (1–2 h):** Run PC-24 on a chapter — persona written from *types you know* (3–5 lines, privacy rule applied), witness protocol mirrored to your ten, **label every "invented" row it volunteers** (the card's [c] — count them; that count is your trust calibration for next time), harvest only: (i) questionnaire redesign flags, (ii) one meta-missed question, (iii) skim-*candidates* you then verify *yourself* at the address with your own eyes + pass logs (the golden rule's full sequence: propose → verify → only then consider). Write five lines afterward: what it caught that your eyes missed, what it confidently invented, and — the actual assignment — **one sentence stating what simulated feedback cannot tell you about your story** (the outline's honest-hedge requirement, in *your* words, saved with the sim files so your future self doesn't drift).

- **Project (ongoing):** Recruit 2–3 betas (instrument-diverse, overrule-able, one honest-stranger) + optional domain-expert for accuracy rows only; brief per the principles (stage/deadline/wrong-and-specific/no-anxieties); run **one full round**: intake verbatim → cluster by address → vision-check verdicts (all four verdict types will likely appear — that's the vocabulary working) → feedback-to-changes table v1 with statuses, clocks, and rank-1 comparison → schedule CHANGE rows into named passes → close the round with the no-unverdicted-rows check. Then *stop* (round 2 only after your scheduled changes land — re-polling betas mid-execution corrupts both the plan and their patience). The table file itself is this chapter's milestone — everything else was preparation for rows you can sign.

## Pitfalls

> PITFALL — **Pleasing every reader (F-08's root).** *Symptom:* contradictory notes applied *blindly* — both readers' fixes attempted, producing a draft that hedges both ways (Chapter 7 cut *and* expanded, ending image both trimmed *and* added — the compromised mush that reads as no one's decision); or the adjacent paralysis: thirty findings, zero verdicts, the table rotting as "still reviewing" for a month — revision anxiety wearing *thoroughness*'s clothes; or its mirror failure: *one* loud reader's taste colonizing the book (the most agreeable beta becomes de-facto co-author because contradicting them felt rude). *Why it happens:* **no stated vision to check against** (without your stakes sentence + criteria + waivers *written down*, "the reader wants X" has no counterparty — their opinion fills the vacuum by default — F-08 isn't a reader problem, it's an *empty-chair* problem: the chair is *yours*, and you weren't sitting in it); **no verdict vocabulary** (rows that can only be "do / not do" force binary panic on findings that are *four different kinds of problems* — the vocabulary is what turns thirty vibes into a week's schedule); **no clocks** (addressless and expired findings live forever in an ungoverned table — paralysis is *stasis by no-expiry*, the mirror of blind-pleasing's *motion by no-vision*); and **kindness without separation** (thanking *after* negotiating, following up "just to check if they were sure" — every soft re-contact re-opens verdicts that needed closing; the *relationship* instinct deployed at the wrong *stage* of the pipeline). *Fix (operational, in pipeline order):* (1) **pre-write the vision pack** *before intake* — stakes sentence + criteria headline + active waivers + bans, one page, pasted at the top of every triage session (the chair gets sat in *before* the guests speak — PC-25's design enforces it by making the pack a required context); (2) **triage with the four verdicts *only*** — every row leaves a session with CHANGE/DISCUSS/OVERRIDE/FIX-THE-QUESTION or an expiry date stamped — the two failure states are structurally impossible once verdicts *and* clocks exist (unverdicted >7 days = the F-08 alarm trips in your weekly review — Ch 7's audit catches it like any instrument); (3) **contradictions stay pairs through the vision check** (never averaged at intake — PC-25's merge-ban; the *vision* decides which side serves the book, and often neither verbatim: verdict-four's "the fight needed a *sign*, not a side" pattern); (4) **rank-1 arithmetic over loudness** (forced rankings *democratize* the table — three quiet readers' aligned rank-1s outweigh one vivid monologue — and the corroboration-count column means *no single reader's prose style* can intimidate the plan); (5) **stage-separated gratitude** (thank at intake-close — warm, complete, *question-free*; re-contact only with the addressless-finding follow-up protocol, once, deadline'd; never to *relitigate* — a verdict re-opened by charm is a verdict that doesn't exist); and (6) **the round-two verification design** (verdict-four fixes and sign-changes get *fresh-reader questions* next round — because the deepest form of pleasing-everyone is *assuming you fixed what they felt*: measure, don't assume — the table's Status column closes on *evidence*, not on effort). *Prevention* (the steady state): the vision pack *versioned alongside done-criteria* (aims documents co-evolve at deliberate mints only — criteria-v2 discipline from Ch 12); triage as a *scheduled block* with the weekly audit counting unverdicted rows (instrumentation again — F-08 is invisible without a denominator); and the standing identity-check: **betas are instruments with feelings you owe kindness to, not *audiences whose approval you need* — the moment their verdict feels like a verdict *on you*, close the table, walk, re-open when the sentence "I decide what this book is" is remembered rather than performed.** The golden rule, at feedback altitude: *readers testify, the table triages, vision disposes — you decide, they propose.*

Related failure for Appendix C: **F-08 (feedback triage paralysis)** — full entry here (symptoms: unverdicted rows >7 days / contradictory-rows applied blind / one-reader colonization; fix stack: vision-pack + four-verdicts + clocks + rank-1 arithmetic + stage-separated gratitude); cross-refs: F-05's agreeable-framing (Q7's witness-conversion prevents *buying* findings at design time), F-09 (beta residue-image findings feed Ch 14's diagnostics), and Ch 13's verify-row (simulated-feedback quality claims — this chapter's hedge: *no independent evidential weight, humans outrank mirrors, always*).

## Checklist

> CHECKLIST — You're ready to move on when:
> - [ ] Beta questions exist for your draft — ten max, PC-23-shaped: every one passes location+memory, Q1 ranks, flags converted to witness-form, zero anxieties embedded, one destroy-permission included — designed *from your open flags*.
> - [ ] Simulated pre-flight (if run) is filed with its disclaimer intact, inventoried for "invented" rows, and reduced to its legitimate yields (questionnaire edits / verified candidates / prediction-rehearsal) — you can state in one sentence what it *cannot* tell you (the honest-hedge line, saved).
> - [ ] One feedback round triaged *fully* into a changes table: verbatim intake, address-clusters, all four verdict types used where applicable, contradictions preserved as pairs with vision-cited overrides, rank-1 comparison computed, every CHANGE row naming its pass, every DISCUSS row carrying a clock, expired rows counted honestly.
> - [ ] The vision pack was in the room for every verdict (stakes + criteria + waivers quoted in rows), and zero rows sat unverdicted past seven days (F-08's alarm silent).
> - [ ] Gratitude separated from negotiation; round frozen; round-2 agenda derived from verdict-four/sign-fix rows — the iteration loop *specified*, not improvised — and the gate points where Part III always pointed: your pages, now revised through passes *and* testimony, are ready for Part IV — voice, law, and the career those finished pages walk into.

## Key takeaways

- **Unbriefed readers return mood; briefed readers return testimony.** Design questions for *events in reading* — answerable with location + memory, ten max, forced ranking at Q1, your own known flags converted to *witness-form at design time* (never "did it sag?" — "name the paragraph you sped through"), and one explicit permission to destroy; brief on stage, blind on your anxieties and preferred readings.
- **Simulated perspectives are rehearsal, not audience:** legitimate yields are questionnaire pilots, verified skim-candidates, prediction-rehearsal, and meta-questionnaire QA — findings carry *no independent evidential weight* (generated about skimming ≠ produced by attention failing; personas inherit agreeable-average priors *and your own casting*); humans can surprise you with their lives, mirrors cannot — **if a simulation contradicts a human, the human is evidence and the mirror is chat.** Provenance stays honest in every log (sim ≠ beta), and privacy rules bind persona-writing like all character work.
- **Triage is a vocabulary plus clocks:** intake verbatim → cluster *by location* (same-address corroboration is the round's gold) → four verdicts (CHANGE / DISCUSS-with-deadline / OVERRIDE-with-cited-reason / FIX-THE-QUESTION — misreadings are *design data* more often than reader error) → feedback-to-changes table with statuses, passes named, rank-1 arithmetic over loudness → passes execute what the table schedules.
- **Contradiction means voltage, not failure** — the vision pack (stakes, criteria, waivers, bans — written *before* intake) is the chair you sit in while readers speak; without it, any reader's prose becomes policy; with it, even your favorite beta gets overridden *documented, kindly, and finally*.
- **Iteration is a designed loop, not a panic:** round 1 pre-passes, round 2 verifies verdict-four fixes with *fresh* readers, praise-only rows tabulate as positive controls, and the round closes with the only metric that matters — zero unverdicted rows over seven days. You decide; they testify; the table remembers — and the finished pages now carry external evidence into Part IV: your voice to protect (Ch 14), your rights to declare (Ch 15), your path to choose (Ch 16).


# Chapter 14 — Voice, Style & Originality in the Age of AI

## Opening hook

Open two files. The first: `baseline-2026-09-23` — the 200 words you wrote in Chapter 1, unaided, before any of this book touched your process. The second: three pages of your current draft — co-drafted, anchored, line-passed, beta-triaged. Read them cold, in that order.

If you did the intervening chapters honestly, the gap will embarrass you in *both* directions, and that double embarrassment is the chapter's opening gift. The baseline will read *rawer*: the tics obvious (four sentences starting the same way; a metaphor straining at the end where you ran out of room), the confidence uneven, the rhythm sometimes nervous. The current pages will read *smoother* — and if you squint, something in the smoothness will flicker: a hedged sentence that says nothing with great care ("it was, in a way, a kind of farewell"); an adjective that arrived from *somewhere else* ("a *palpable* silence" — you have never once in your life thought a silence was palpable); two paragraphs whose rhythm you'd swear you didn't write, because *you didn't*. The baseline's problem was craft. The new pages' problem is **voice erosion at the edges** — the machine's house register, colonizing your sentences wherever the anchors thinned.

This chapter is the repair-and-build program for exactly that gap, in three movements: **diagnose** (a checklist for "generic AI prose" you can run on any page in four minutes), **de-AI** (PC-26's sentence-level surgery that keeps what structure the machine did well and replaces *whose* it sounds like), and **build** (the imitation → variation → voice ladder — PC-27 — plus a personal style sheet, the living document that makes your voice *deliberate* instead of accidental). By the end: the diagnostic has run on your real pages, style sheet v1 exists, and you can name three voice features that are *yours* — which is the sentence Chapter 1's baseline was always going to be measured against.

## Learning objectives

By the end of this chapter, you will be able to:

- [ ] Diagnose "generic AI prose" in your own draft with a four-minute checklist.
- [ ] Run the de-AI pass (PC-26) without losing what AI did well.
- [ ] Practice the imitation → variation → voice ladder (PC-27) deliberately.
- [ ] Keep a personal style sheet — and name three voice features that are yours.

## Diagnosing generic AI prose

First, the honest framing: "AI prose" is not a *look*; it's a **cluster of habits** that happen to co-occur in model-default output because they're high-probability completions (Chapter 5's mechanism: nothing about a generated paragraph *proves* a machine wrote it — *your* unanchored sentences can carry the same cluster, and machine sentences under tight locks sometimes don't). The diagnostic below therefore says "generic-register symptoms," not "AI tells" — you're auditing *the habits*, wherever they came from, including your own tired Tuesdays. (Inverse honesty: hand-written prose fails this checklist all the time; the checklist is *style-neutral about tools* and *specific about patterns* — which is what makes it usable in the Ch 12 line pass, where provenance is irrelevant and the sentence either works or wears the symptoms.)

The four symptom families, each with a **test you can run on a page**:

1. **Hedging inflation.** *Test:* highlight every qualifier — "seemed," "somewhat," "in a way," "a kind of," "almost as if," "perhaps." Then *delete every one* and read again. If the passage lost precision, some hedges were load-bearing (real uncertainty earns its word); if the passage got *sharper* — the hedges were **confidence-laundering**, sentences hedging so they can never be wrong, which is machine-safe-completion's signature move (and yours, when you're writing to avoid critique). Density tell: more than one hedge per three sentences in narrative voice = audit for laundering. The cure isn't *always* assertiveness (some narrators genuinely wobble — a *chosen* wobble is style; a *diffuse* wobble is default).

2. **Thesaurus weather.** *Test:* read only the adjectives, adverbs, and figurative phrases aloud, skipping the nouns and verbs. Generic register *shows* instantly in this stripped channel: the synonyms arrive *wrong-register* for your book (your plainspoken fishing-town story suddenly has "ethereal" light and "cacophonous" gulls — the thesaurus reached for *fancier* because fancier *reads* as effortful), or *safe-familiar* ("old," "faint," "gentle" ×6 per page). Two signatures: **upgrade-itis** (words dressed above the narrator's register — diction *disconnected from voice* — the sharpest single detector, because *your* voice has a known class-level and machine-default operates a class above or below it) and **repeat-drift** (the same "nice" intensifier installed three pages running — "deeply" doing quiet unpaid labor — a pattern *within* one author's range is tic (style-sheet territory), *outside* it is drift).

3. **Rhythmic monotony.** *Test:* mark sentence lengths as a column of numbers down the margin of one paragraph-heavy page. Generic register clusters: sentences stacking in a narrow band (the 14–22-word corridor — model-default's comfort zone), paragraph after paragraph — *because cadence averages* (Chapter 5: generation regresses toward typical, and typical prose is *evenly medium*). The failure isn't medium sentences (you need them — they're the tram tracks); it's **the absence of the irregular**: a paragraph where *nothing* is 6 words, nothing is 34, no sentence breaks the paragraph's own emerging pattern *on purpose*. Human voices are *uneven in characteristic ways* — your Chapter 1 baseline's rhythm, for all its craft-problems, was *yours* (mark its length-column too: you'll see *your* signature imbalance — that's what you're trying to keep while smoothing the rest).

4. **Empty lyricism / reflection-tax.** *Test:* circle every sentence whose job is *commenting on the scene's feeling* rather than advancing or sensing it ("and in that moment, she understood what loss truly meant"). Then ask, per circle: did the *scene* already carry this — was the understanding *performed* two lines earlier by an action? Generic register **pays a reflection-tax**: a closing meditation appended to emotional beats *by default*, because emotional completions pattern-match to *moral-summary* completions in its diet (the trailing-summary ending from Ch 4's double-ending list, promoted here to a whole-family symptom). Related sub-signature: **symmetrical balance** — clauses arriving in matched pairs ("not just X, but Y" — and again — and again): balance by *default* rather than by *meaning* is oratorical emptiness wearing craft's clothes; real balance earns itself once per page at most, at the moments that deserve ceremony.

**The four-minute diagnostic (the checklist, runnable on any two pages):**

```text
GENERIC-REGISTER DIAGNOSTIC (2 pages, cold read)
[ ] Hedges: count > 6 → strip-all-and-retest (precision lost = keep
    some; sharper = laundering found)
[ ] Diction class: underline 10 modifiers aloud — any arriving from
    a class your narrator wouldn't use? (upgrade-itis / safe-familiar)
[ ] Rhythm column: sentence-length numbers — narrow-band stretch of
    3+ paragraphs flagged; missing extremes (any 6-word or 30+
    sentence in view?)
[ ] Reflection-tax: circled commentaries vs. performed beats — any
    appended after the scene already showed it? any symmetrical
    "not-X-but-Y" pairs beyond one per page?
[ ] Swear-level honesty: could *anyone's* narrator have written
    these pages? (the existential test — if yes, the other four
    found why)
→ Output: symptom counts + the WORST offender sentence quoted
  (the quote feeds PC-26's context — diagnosis produces the
  surgery's first target, not a vague malaise)
```

Run it on the *current-draft sample* from the hook now, before reading on — counts in your margin. (If the page came back clean: either your anchors have held beautifully — good; go run it on your *machine-heaviest chapter* anyway, the one the mode-log's `co-draft` rows name — diagnosis belongs where generation density lives.) Two calibration notes before tools: the diagnostic is *quantitative on purpose* (counts and columns beat "feels generic" — the felt version is unfalsifiable and demoralizing; six hedges is a *finding*, an atmosphere is a mood), and **severity scales with your register's tightness** (a deliberately ornate literary voice gets *more* lyricism budget than your baseline-minimalist — the style sheet, coming below, is what makes the diagnostic's thresholds *yours* rather than universal — the checklist without the sheet is other people's taste).

## The de-AI pass

Diagnosis in hand, surgery: **PC-26** — the pass that keeps structure and replaces *voice*, the inverse of Chapter 10's anchors (there: your voice established *before* generation; here: your voice re-established *after*, at the sentence level):

> PROMPT CARD PC-26 — De-AI prose pass
>
> ```text
> 
> Role: sentence surgeon executing MY revoicing — you remove
> symptoms and preserve skeleton; you do not add content, images,
> or opinions; when unsure whether a line is load-bearing, LEAVE
> IT (flag instead).
> Context: my STYLE SHEET (banned constructions + my rhythm notes +
> diction-class statement + tics-to-keep — pasted) + my diagnosis
> output (worst-offender quote + symptom counts) + the passage.
> Targets (from the four families — apply only where flagged):
> H1 hedge-strip (keep load-bearing uncertainty — list kept ones)
> H2 diction-demote (upgrade-itis → my narrator's class; safe-
>    familiar → my class at *specificity*, not fanciness)
> H3 rhythm-break (mark narrow-band stretches; propose ONE
>    split/merge/fragment per stretch — minimum intervention to
>    restore irregularity, not maximal)
> H4 reflection-tax cut (delete commentaries the scene performed;
>    break symmetry pairs at ONE side per pair — which side I decide
>    from my sheet)
> Preserve absolutely: plot facts, proper nouns, locked POV/tense/
> distance, [!mine] markers, any line I've style-sheeted as a KEEP.
> Output: (a) table: ORIGINAL → EDITED + symptom-tag + keep-flags;
> (b) clean passage; (c) counts: hedges −n, class-changes n,
> rhythm-breaks n, reflections cut n. I adjudicate rows as in PC-21;
> rejections final.
> ```

Read the surgery's *ethics of editing*: **leave-if-unsure + flag** (the conservative bias that keeps this pass from deleting *your* deliberate oddities the machine can't recognize as deliberate — the `[!mine]` and KEEP-list do the recognizing *for* it; unknown-odd lines get *flagged to you*, which is the pass's core asymmetry: it's allowed to *find*, barely allowed to *change* — adjudication-heavy by design, like every pass whose mistakes are invisible in reverse); **symmetry broken at ONE side, you choose** (the balance-symptom's fix isn't *anti-balance*, it's *your* balance — which side of the pair survives is a taste decision the sheet encodes and the machine *cannot* infer — golden rule at clause scale); **demote-to-your-class, not to blandness** (H2's careful wording: the fix for upgrade-itis is *register-matching* — "palpable silence" → "a silence with *weight* you could feel in the room" if weight-at-that-class is your narrator's idiom, NOT → "a quiet silence" — the pass *serves the sheet*, and a pass run *without* a pasted sheet degenerates into a house-register *launderer* (the failure mode to name once: de-AI without a style sheet just installs *different* generic prose — the sheet is the instrument; the card is only its executor); and **counts at the end** (the diagnostic's numbers re-run post-surgery — the pass's completion signal is *symptom counts trending toward your sheet's thresholds*, not a vibe of improvement — convergence measurable, circle impossible: two runs with <10% delta = the passage is done *for this pass*, next passage, next symptom family — bounded, like Ch 12 demands).

What the pass deliberately does *not* touch: **structure, beats, and adjudicated machine contributions** — the scene's architecture (the skeleton, the composite from Ch 10's three-ways) was *yours via decisions rows*; de-AI is *re-voicing*, not re-deciding. The flow to internalize (the full pipeline the last five chapters built): *anchor before → locks during → mark at drafting → blind-test voices → diagnose generically → de-AI surgically → style-sheet the survivors* — each layer cheaper than the last, each gate owned by you, and by the end of the chain the machine's fingerprints on the page are **either gone or adopted** — nothing left *ambiguous*, which is the actual definition of voice-owned prose this book has been converging on since Chapter 1's taste lecture.

> TRY IT — Run the four-minute diagnostic on your machine-heaviest chapter now (mode-log named), then PC-26 on its single worst paragraph with your nascent sheet-list in context (even three banned lines count as a sheet for this test). Compare counts before/after. Read both versions aloud — *your* ear, deciding which sentences you'd sign: that signature-test, repeated paragraph by paragraph for the next week, *is* the de-AI pass becoming a habit instead of a card.

## Imitation → variation → voice

Diagnosis and surgery maintain the voice you have; the ladder below *builds* the one you're still becoming — the oldest program in creative education, updated for a tool that makes each rung cheaper:

**Rung 1 — Imitation (copy with intent).** Select a living-or-dead writer whose *specific* features you covet — not "good prose" (unselectable) but "how she handles clause-length under pressure," "how *he* uses monosyllables after polysyllables" — and **copy a page by hand, then from memory.** The hand-copy teaches *decisions you can't see from reading alone* (every sentence you retype *is* a decision you've now physically agreed with); the memory-copy immediately reveals what you *didn't* internalize (you'll paraphrase — and your paraphrase's *drift* is diagnostic: the places you "misremembered" are where your habits resisted theirs — gold for the ladder).
