Four paths, presented as **trade-off structures** — every bullet a design feature, *not* a promise (outcomes vary enormously within every path; survivorship bias — named here once, applied forever — contaminates almost every success-story you'll hear *from any path*: the advice-givers are the survivors, and the same inputs with median luck produce different stories; this book's tables therefore describe *what each path makes you do and give up*, which stays true whether your outcome is median or miraculous):

**Traditional (agent → publisher).** *You get:* advance against royalties (sums vary *widely* by genre, debut status, and market moment — **no figure printed;** check current agent-interview aggregations *dated at your query time* if you want the order-of-magnitude), professional editing/design *funded by the publisher*, distribution into physical stores and libraries' standard ordering systems, and a form of vetting that still signals to parts of the industry. *You give up:* timeline (months-to-years of querying *before* any of the above, then typically a year-plus to shelf *after* an offer — the hook's eighteen months is *typical in shape*, not a statistic), control (cover, title influence, schedule, price: publisher's levers), rights granularity (territories, audio, film options get *licensed through* the deal's terms — Ch 15's contract-clause reading applies at professional stakes), and *volume of output*: this path favors *one careful book at a time* more than rapid release. *Best fit when:* physical-store presence, prestige-benchmark goals, or "I want to write and be *handed* a machine's worth of production I don't run" — you're buying *infrastructure* with *control + time*. *Ch 15 relevance:* contracts + warranties meeting your provenance logs (the long statement's first real customer).

**Indie (self-publishing).** *You get:* speed-to-reader measured in *weeks* after completion (the hook's eight weeks is a *schedule you design*, not a guarantee of attention), control of every lever (price, cover *direction*, series continuity, wide-or-exclusive platform choices, backlist reactivity), royalty share *minus* costs, and rights retained (you license *none* away by default — Ch 15 boxes still apply: your registration, your disclosure lines on platform forms). *You give up:* **you become the production company** — editing, cover, typesetting, metadata, and *marketing* are your *paid or learned* line items (realistic *order-of-magnitude* costs for a beginner-quality-but-professional bar: professional editing for a novel commonly lands in the four-to-five-figure range of your currency depending on length/market, covers in the mid hundreds to low thousands, *all perishable — verify quotes at your commissioning time*; the **list price minus platform share minus ads minus services** is *your* margin's arithmetic — run it before committing, honestly, *on your own numbers*), and discoverability work (ads, newsletter, retailer algorithms — each with their own four-surface-rules discipline) becomes a *second skillset* competing with writing time. *Best fit when:* control priority, catalog-speed ambition (rapid-ish release *compounds* — the path's one statistical-sounding truth, stated as mechanism not figure: more titles × time = more surfaces for discovery, *with quality floors or it fails*), niche audiences the majors underserve, or nonfiction-platform synergy — you're *buying control + speed* with *money + ops labor + risk*.

**Serial (platforms, Patreon-class subscriptions, newsletter-first).** *You get:* *immediate* audience feedback loops (the chapter is careful: feedback loops, not *craft authority* — Ch 13's witness-testimony rules apply *harder* in comment sections, where rank-1-question discipline is rare and cheer-squads are loud), recurring-reader *rhythm* (the practice-benefit is real: deadlines weekly, pages accumulating, avoidance structurally impossible), and monetization from day one *if* an audience forms (tip/subscription economics vary wildly by platform — **no figures**). *You give up:* drafting privacy (the draft *is* the product — no pass system's quiet middle; revision-after-publication becomes a *continuity* act under readers' memory — F-03's public cousin), platform-dependency (algorithms, fee structures, and content rules change under you — Ch 15's artifacts-and-dates habit aimed at *terms of service* now), and *formal* editing time budgets (the cadence squeezes passes — the writers who survive it either *bank* drafts ahead (batch-then-release: the pass system *pre-loaded* — genuinely the recommended shape if you serialise) or accept serial-grade line work and *novelize later* honestly). *Best fit when:* feedback-fueled temperaments, web-serial-native genres, and practice-stage writers who need *external rhythm more than quiet* — you're buying *rhythm + audience-in-progress* with *privacy + platform risk*.

**Small press / indie-friendly imprints (direct-without-going-it-alone).** The hybrid often *forgotten* in the two-culture war: smaller publishers (including digital-first imprints) who take rights for a term, *usually* lighter advances, *sometimes* more author input on cover/schedule than majors, distribution partly their problem, *ops partly yours* (marketing labor frequently shared-or-yours — read the actual contract's reversion clauses: rights *coming back* on out-of-print/sales triggers is small-press contract-craft 101 — Ch 15 clause-mapping again, at this tier's stakes). *Best fit when:* you want *some* infrastructure + vetting without majors' timeline lottery — and *always*: verify the press's reputation at writer-forums *and* submission-tracker databases (dated crowdsourced data beats press *marketing* pages — the source-priority table aimed at imprints).

**The scoring matrix (the Try does it live):** rows = the four paths; columns = *your* weightings — timeline tolerance, control hunger, capital available, ops appetite, distribution need, feedback rhythm need, rights attachment — each cell 1–5 *by your goals* (not by culture-war allegiance: "indie = freedom" and "traditional = legitimacy" are *brand slogans* your matrix is designed to *dissolve into your own numbers*). The exercise's deeper curriculum: watching a path you *disliked on vibes* score *well on your stated columns* (or vice versa) — the spot where ideology meets your actual constraints — and the honest follow-up: columns you *can't score yet* ("ops appetite: unknown — never ops'd") get *experiments*, not guesses (Ch 15's Try was a 15-minute policy hunt; this tier's experiments: shadow one ad dashboard for an hour, read one *public* publishing contract-clause breakdown, lurk one query-success-and-failure tracker month) — because the matrix's real output isn't a winner; it's **your next research question with a date on it.**

## Querying and self-publishing checklists

Two executable checklists — each *the procedure compressed*, cross-referencing the chapters whose disciplines they metabolize. Use as **gates, not gospels**: boxes get ticked in order; a box you can't tick is *the actual work item*, not a box to argue with.

```text
QUERYING CHECKLIST (traditional / small-press-with-agent routes)
PRE-FLIGHT
[ ] Manuscript FINISH = done-criteria v-final signed (Ch 12) + round-2
    beta rows resolved (Ch 13) + proof-read exists (fresh eyes OR
    full-distance read + Ch 12 pass 5) — querying a draft still
    learning is how reform-querying starts
[ ] Package components drafted: query letter (hook/synopsis pitch
    from your Ch 8/9 logline + stakes sentence — the elevator
    machinery repurposed), synopsis (spoilers-all, 1–2pp — the
    skeleton's plot-half told straight), first pages (the Ch 10
    voice-anchor'd opening THAT passed the diagnostic)
[ ] Market research done current & dated: agents/publishers WHO
    REPRESENT your category (their #MSWL/wishlist posts dated;
    their closed-status checked THIS month — submission trackers
    + agency sites = source priority), comparable titles ("comp
    shelf") published within ~3–5 years YOU have actually read
[ ] Provenance ready: disclosure statement mid-length filed
    (Ch 15) IF the agency's submission form asks (hunt the FOUR
    surfaces per Ch 15 — agencies' forms have checkboxes too)
THE SEND
[ ] Personalization per agent from THEIR actual wish-list/recent
    sales (one line beats fawning paragraphs — agents skip the
    flattery, read the comp)
[ ] Materials EXACTLY as their page requests (sample pages count,
    file formats, query-manager fields — a rules-violation here is
    the hook's email's quieter cousin)
[ ] Tracker row per send: who/when/status/personalization-note —
    spreadsheet suffices (the Ch 7 logs' discipline, submission-
    sized)
THE WAIT (design it — the worst box for humans)
[ ] Response-window noted per agent (their stated times — NOT
    your anxiety's); follow-up ONLY at window+grace, one nudge max
[ ] While waiting: NEXT PROJECT goes in `manuscript/` (the Ch 8
    parked file's itch re-rated; or continue YOUR practice queue) —
    querying-with-one-book-in-the-world is career-slot-machine
    behavior; querying-with-a-second-draft-starting is *practice*
[ ] Form-rejection ≠ quality-verdict (their list-fit, their slush
    pile, their year — Ch 13's lesson at career scale: instruments
    produce DATA you adjudicate, not verdicts you absorb; collect
    any personalized feedback as rows, answer never from the wound)
RE-ROUTE ON SCHEDULE (pre-decided, not mid-spiral)
[ ] Pre-written decision date: "if X months / X sends: reassess →
    small-press direct window / indie plan Ch 16's matrix" — the
    path-reassessment CLOCK set BEFORE waiting, the done-criteria
    pattern at career altitude: plans-without-dates become moods
    with inboxes
```

```text
SELF-PUBLISHING CHECKLIST (indie path — the hook's Writer B)
MANUSCRIPT GATES (all prior chapters' exits, non-waivable)
[ ] done-criteria signed + ALL FIVE pass logs + proof pass (a
    clean final read at *reading* speed — typo-hunt pass distinct
    from line pass: Ch 12's altitude rule honored to the end)
[ ] Ch 14 diagnostic PASS at sheet thresholds (your voice, on
    record — the book's authorship-argument closes here)
[ ] Beta round(s) rows resolved (Ch 13 table statuses = closed)
PRODUCTION LINE (each a *decision* with vendor artifact, not a vibe)
[ ] Cover: BRIEF written by you (category-convention research:
    what top-20 in your subgenre's covers *do* — legibility at
    thumbnail = the medium's physics) → commissioned (pro designer
    OR vetted premade — verify portfolio + contract credit terms;
    your Ch 15 statement-of-process applies to *your* brief's
    originality) → file formats from the platform's spec sheet
    (their spec page = another "official source" tier)
[ ] Editing: developmental/structural = your passes + one EXTERNAL
    cold reader if budget allows (Ch 13 instrument: hire the gap
    your self-passes structurally can't see); line + proof from
    vetted pros (quotes compared, sample-edit requested — *three
    quotes, dated*, perishable-prices rule applied to services too)
[ ] Interior: typeset/format per platform requirements (their
    conversion tools vs. a formatter's file — choose by your ops
    appetite column), metadata decided BEFORE upload: title,
    subtitle, description copy (your Ch 8 logline's marketing
    cousin: 1–3 sentences + hook line — *written to readers, not
    to judges*), categories/BISAC-or-platform-taxonomy researched
    (what your actual readers browse), keywords honestly descriptive
    (Ch 15's accuracy ethic at metadata scale: clickbait-category
    fraud is platform-rule violation + reader-trust violation —
    the same dish, two sauces)
[ ] Rights & paperwork: ISBN/identifier decision (platform-assigned
    vs. purchased — jurisdiction-specific rules: check YOUR
    market's registrar guidance, dated; US/UK/etc. differ — box
    discipline again), copyright registration *considered* (Ch 15's
    US guidance: your human-authored work — file accurately with
    AI-assistance disclosure per official guidance IF/when you
    register), Ch 15 disclosure statement prepped for any platform
    form that asks (four surfaces: guidelines, dashboards, form
    fields, support-docs — platforms have all four)
[ ] Launch *plan sized to reality*: pre-order mechanics only if
    you understand the platform's rules for them; review-gathering
    ONLY within each platform's policy letter-of-the-law (review
    swaps/asks-for-positives = policy violations — Ch 15 hunt
    aimed at review systems: READ the guidelines page), ad accounts
    tested SMALL (first budget = tuition, expected loss, dashboard
    read weekly for exactly-what-was-learned rows)
POST-LAUNCH (the indie half of the chapter's thesis)
[ ] Backlist behavior watched (sell-through > launch spikes —
    mechanisms, not figures: one book sells the *others*; the
    catalog IS the strategy's unit)
[ ] Monthly ops review slot (Ch 7's audit rhythm aimed at the
    store: what earned, what cost, what to stop — stop-decisions
    logged like revisions: reversible, changelog'd, un-attached)
[ ] Next manuscript starts by [DATE YOU WRITE TODAY] — the
    checklist's true last box: indie-without-backlist is a
    single-product business; writers-without-next-books are
    people waiting for permission from statistics
```

Both checklists share their deepest line: **every box was previously a chapter** (finish = Ch 12's criteria; packages = Ch 8–9's compression machinery; provenance = Ch 15; the waiting-box = Ch 13's verdict-vocabulary aimed at your own hope; next-project = Ch 8's parked itch, *this time with a skeleton*) — which is why they can be *executed* rather than *feared*: you've been rehearsing each box's skill for fifteen chapters, and the checklists' only new content is *sequence and artifacts*. The remaining unknowns are the *genuinely* external ones (an agent's taste, an algorithm's quarter, a market's mood) — and the book's last structural argument is that external unknowns are *weather*: you don't control them, you *dress for them* (matrix + checklists + date-stamped research) and you *keep walking* (the 90-day plan, next).

## Your practice: the 90-day plan

Everything in this book — every anchor, log, pass, and verdict — ultimately serves one asset: **a practice that produces finished manuscripts on a schedule *you* sustain without a book telling you to.** The 90-day plan is the hand-off instrument: three months, one project-focus, your *actual* capacity numbers (Ch 7's two-week review *already measured* them — the fantasy 1,200 w/day dies here; the 660-planning-number method gets *built*), review rhythm pre-set, and drift-prevention designed *in* (Ch 14's cause-rows, Ch 11's audit cadence, Ch 12's cycle caps — the *maintenance* layer of everything you built, now scheduled instead of advised). The template, then the worked example:

```text
90-DAY PLAN — [name] · [dates] · v1 (revisions: changelog'd only)
FOCUS (one): [e.g., "Draft novel v1 through pass 1" | "Revise
collection to done-criteria + query package" | "Build practice:
3 finished shorts + indie experiments"] — ONE focus; the other
ideas are in the parked file being *append-only patient*
CAPACITY (from my logs, not vibes): maker-blocks/week: [n] ×
planned words or pass-pages per block: [real number × 0.85 safety]
= weekly target [T]; 90 days ≈ [13 weeks] → quarter goal: [T×13
with one recovery week at 60% built in — calendars always claim
100%; plans that assume it are fiction]
MONTH 1 (build + prove): weeks 1–4 themed — [e.g., M1: premise→
beat outline (PC-11 pass logged) → first 4 chapters drafted under
mode-table discipline]; EVERY week: [ritual row: Sunday rhythm-
audit + beat-list-night-before (Ch 7 review's fixes *in calendar*)
]; M1 exit gate: outline frozen + 4 chapters' pass-logs OR
equivalent focus-gate — gate fails → month 2 recalibrated from
gate (the plan serves reality; recalibration is a CHANGELOG row,
not a shame spiral)
MONTH 2 (deep middle — where plans die; design for it): weeks 5–8
[bulk drafting / passes 2–4 scheduled per-chapter-batches]; TWICE:
[Ch 11 tracker fills; Ch 14 diagnostic sample; F-09 layer spot-
check]; mid-quarter REVIEW (week ~6, 45 min): capacity vs. plan
(adjust DOWN first — plans recover from honesty faster than from
inflation), mode-column pattern check, ONE process change max
(two changes = no changes — focus law)
MONTH 3 (finish + hand-off): weeks 9–12 [draft completes → pass 5
→ done-criteria check → IF criteria met: path-next executes (beta
round scheduled OR querying-pre-flight boxes OR indie production
line begins — THE CHECKLISTS ARRIVE *HERE*, not before)]; week 13
= RECOVERY + quarter review + NEXT 90-DAY PLAN DRAFT (the cycle's
closing act — planning the next quarter IS this system's definition
of "practice": four plans a year, each with one recovery week, each
fed by the last one's honest numbers)
COMMUNITY (one scheduled action — the Ch 16 closing requirement):
[one critique-exchange application OR beta-recruit OR event per
month — calendar rows, not aspirations]
MEASURES (beyond word count — five dials, one glance weekly):
[1] maker-blocks kept/n · [2] words-or-pages vs. T · [3] adjudic-
ation rows (taste reps happening?) · [4] F-09 layer compliance
(anchor/audit rows — voice preserved?) · [5] gates passed on
schedule (the ONLY scoreboard that compounds: gates → focus →
quarter → year)
ANTI-DRIFT (designed-in, from this book's own failures): cause-row
pattern triggers an UNAIDED session (Ch 14); F-07 signature →
pass-system diagnosis BEFORE more polish (Ch 12); keep-rate >50%
×2 → re-run PASS 1 not pass 4 (same); path-envy spike (social
feeds) → matrix re-score with this month's numbers, then close the
feed (the pitfall below, automated); "the plan feels wrong" →
writes a CHANGELOG row or stays quiet — feelings file; changes
version
```

**Worked example — our reader finishing this book** (the outline's promised sample, annotated): *Focus:* "Revisions + package: take 'Low Tide' collection (3 finished shorts incl. the Ch 4 milestone) to done-criteria + querying pre-flight." *Capacity from logs:* 8 maker-blocks/week averaging 640 net (post safety-factor ≈ 544/block) → weekly T ≈ 4,350 words-equivalents (passes convert words→pages at Ch 12's rates — the *unit* is focus-appropriate: revision-quarters measure in *pass-pages and rows*, drafting-quarters in *words* — **one focus, one unit** — mixing units is how plans start lying). *M1:* criterion-writes (v1 done-criteria signed TODAY per Ch 12) + pass 1–2 on all three stories (PC-20 runs batched; gate: three structure logs + mint) — *why the annotation matters*: the plan didn't invent new machinery — it *scheduled existing cards*: PC-20's fresh-session discipline shows up as *three calendar blocks*, the adjudication-ritual as *Sunday rows* — the plan's only new words are *dates and gates*. *M2:* passes 3–4 + Ch 14 diagnostic *sampled at week 6's review* + one external line-editor quote-gathering (three quotes, dated — the indie-or-traditional *research question* from the matrix getting its experiment instead of a guess) — *mid-quarter review's pre-commitment to "adjust DOWN first"* is the plan's immune system: the quarter where reality returns 70% of plan, *honest adjustment keeps the gate dates* while *heroic adjustment guarantees the recovery week gets eaten by panic* (calendar biology: every plan is optimistic; good plans *budget the optimism out* ahead of time). *M3:* passes 5 + cold-read + criteria-check (Ch 12's gate — *and here's the fork the plan deliberately does NOT pre-choose*: IF criteria met → querying pre-flight boxes (checklist A) because the *matrix* scored timeline-tolerance 4/5 and control 2/5 for this writer; the indie production line (checklist B) sits *untouched but ready* — **the plan schedules the *gate*, the matrix already chose the *branch*** — plans that hardcode paths before criteria-met are essays about a future self; plans that schedule gates *and carry the branch-decisions in their appendix* are instruments. *Community row:* "Week 2: apply to the regional critique exchange (their submission window: first week of month — *checked their site, dated*); Month 3: recruit two betas from the exchange for round-2 with PC-23's *revised* ten" — note what kind of sentence that is: **a calendar row with a URL behind it**, not "get feedback someday" — the whole chapter's thesis in one line: paths, checklists, and plans are all just *decisions that found their calendar.)* *Anti-drift rows written:* the writer's known cause-pattern (meeting-week mornings — Ch 7's worked review found it) triggers "Mon/Wed = unaided-outcome-only sessions"; social-feed path-envy triggers a matrix re-score *with this month's numbers* — *then* the feed closes — the design principle showing: **the book's failures have all been named by now (F-01…F-10); a 90-day plan is what named failures look like *scheduled out of existence*.**

The five-dials glance deserves its own sentence because it's the *only* progress-measurement this book will bless: **blocks kept, target-vs-actual, adjudication rows, layer compliance, gates passed** — notice what's absent (raw words as a *score*, platforms watched, others' output compared): words without blocks are noise; rows without layers are drift with receipts; and *gates passed on schedule* is the one compounding dial (each passed gate is a *finished thing* — the asset that makes next quarter's plan *start further along* — the anti-mush definition of progress: **not faster, not louder; gated.**)

## Community and what next

The plan's COMMUNITY rows need a *destination*, so here's the map's last quadrant — **where writers actually stand in 2026** (structural roles, platforms-as-of-dating rather than platform-recommendations, which would be stale on arrival): **critique exchanges** (membership-based mutual-reading groups — regional in-person chapters and online forums both; *the* ladder-climb for serious practice: give-more-than-you-take culture, application+participation gates — find via your region's writers'-center or genre-guild directories, checked *dated*); **beta-swap partnerships** (the two-or-three-regulars arrangement Ch 13 assumed — formed *inside* exchanges or across shared-beta recruitment; the relationship *is* the infrastructure — five years of swap partners outlast any platform); **genre/niche organizations** (guilds and associations for your category — craft programming, agent/editor Q&A archives, *marketplace-of-paths* intelligence like the matrix's research questions answered *by people who filed them first* — dues are the cheap version of the education no single book can finalize); **events** (con conventions, lit festivals, workshops — the *in-person compounding* space: a fifteen-minute real conversation with a working agent beats forty query-blog hours *as market research*, and a workshop-met critique partner beats an algorithm's match *as collaboration*; budget them like the capital they are); **online communities** (forums, Discords, social threads — *ranked last deliberately* (the Ch 13 cheer-squad/verdict-confusion problem in permanent session): use them as *bulletins and finders* (calls for beta readers, exchange openings, event dates — *instrument* uses), not as *craft-authority or worth-measurement* feeds (their failure mode is the pitfall below in its purest drip-feed form — if a feed *consistently* makes you feel behind while *measuring nothing you track*, it's not community; it's a path-envy IV — cut the line, keep the exchange)); **pay attention to who gives specific notes vs. who gives vibes** — the community-trust heuristic is Ch 13's witness-vs-mood distinction *wearing a name tag*: the person who says "page 12's line tripped me — the pronoun flipped" has *demonstrated the instrument*; keep them; the person whose every note is "loved it!" or "didn't work" is *kind and vague* — lovely; not a critique partner. One more role worth naming for later-chapter readership: **mentors-with-boundaries** (an established writer's office-hours, a teacher's feedback tier, a paid mentorship *vetted like a contract* — Ch 15's "read the actual terms, check the track record, never pay for guaranteed representation/access" applies *verbatim* to the mentorship market; specific-names avoided here for the sourcing rules, but the *vetting procedure* transfers whole).

And now the part the book owes you — **the closing letter**, because sixteen chapters of second-person *you* deserve to end face to face:

> *You started with a weak premise and a 200-word baseline you were half-ashamed of. Look at what the folder on your machine now contains: a skeleton with a stakes sentence you can say from memory; character sheets whose chains reach their climaxes; a beat outline that survived a real adversarial pass; a draft with your anchors in its hinges and your signatures in its changelog; five pass logs with verdicts in your handwriting; a done-criteria file you signed like an oath; beta rows that contradict each other, triaged without flinching; a style sheet whose bans you caught *yourself* violating; a venues dossier; a disclosure statement; a matrix; two checklists; and a ninety-day plan with week 1 — if we've done our jobs — already on your calendar. That folder is not "a book assisted by AI." It is the *evidence file of an author* — and the machine's fingerprints in it are all either gone or adopted, which was the only promise this book ever made: not that the tool would write for you — it proposes, endlessly, forgetfully, fluently, wrongly — but that **you would decide**, at every altitude where decisions live: taste, structure, voice, law, path, and next. The desert from the preface didn't shrink; you learned the crossing. Now go write the next sentence — the one nobody's model has your particular reasons for wanting there — and then the one after that. We're very much looking forward to reading what you do with your name.*

### The quarter's review questions (four, dated)

The 90-day plan needs its *closing ceremony* specified or "quarter review" decays into a vibes-recap — so the four questions, asked in order at week 13's slot (45 minutes, answers *written into the next plan's header*, the instrument-chaining pattern every review in this book has used):

**Q1 — Gates: which passed on schedule, and what did the misses teach the *capacity model*?** (The arithmetic first: plan-said vs. actual for each month-gate; every miss gets its *cause-row* — the usual suspects ranked from your own history: optimistic-T (the ×0.85 factor wasn't enough — next quarter: ×0.80, *or* fewer gates), life-event (calendar truth, absorbed not辩'd — the recovery week's *location* was wrong, move it earlier next time), drift-signature (the anti-drift rows didn't fire — *why*: trigger not visible? rule too vague? — fix the *instrument*, never the character), or gate-too-loose (gate passed suspiciously easy — thresholds inflated? — the *opposite* failure, logged with equal honesty: **gates that can't fail don't gate**); the Q1 output feeding the next plan's *capacity-number* and *gate-count* — the model's self-correction, one quarter's data at a time.)

**Q2 — Measures: which of the five dials moved, which lied, and which got ignored?** (The glance-table pulled for the quarter: blocks-kept (calendar-truth), words/pages-vs-T (the arithmetic), adjudication-rows (taste-reps happening?), layer-compliance (voice-preserved?), gates-passed (the compounding one) — *lying-dials* diagnosed as hard as missed-gates (a dial that read "fine" all quarter while something downstream suffered = *the dial's design* at fault: T measured in words while the quarter's focus was revision → *unit-mismatch* — the next plan's one-focus-one-unit rule gets its evidence from exactly this question; an *ignored*-dial — never opened during weekly glances — either earns its way back with a *decision-date* or retires to the annual list: the five-dials set is *maintained like a style sheet*, bump-or-retire with reasons, not tattooed.)

**Q3 — Community: did one step *actually happen* each month, and did the people improve?** (The row-check brutal in its simplicity: three months × one step = three calendar-actions; 0–1 completed = the *aspiration-colon* diagnosed — next quarter's row gets *harder constraints* (the application window's *actual* date booked at plan-time; the swap-turn *scheduled like a gate* — the community row's historic failure being exactly Ch 16's thesis: aspirations die in prose, steps survive in calendars); 2–3 completed = *quality-audit* replaces *existence-audit* (did the specific-notes-giver *give* specific notes? did the exchange's feedback reach a *table* with verdicts? — community-as-instrument measured by *instrument-output*: contacts that produced *rank-1-findings or swap-turns* stay on the roster; contacts that produced only *warmth* rotate to the acquaintance-tier — not coldly: *by design* — the roster's job is the practice's instruments, warmth is what *happens anyway* when instruments run in public.)

**Q4 — Path: did the matrix's columns move this quarter, and does envy have *new data*?** (The re-score, run with *this quarter's actuals* — the pitfall-section's scheduled defense: any column whose score shifts gets its *evidence-line* ("ops-appetite 2→3: ran first ad test, learned X — the *experiment* the last matrix's research-question asked for earned its answer"), any envy-spike that occurred during the quarter *re-run through the columns* (was it information — a column scored lazily — or weather? — logged either way: **the envy-row's existence is the habit; its content is the bonus**); and the Q4-only existential check, asked once a year: *is the focus-still-yours* — the matrix's priorities-line re-read against the quarter's lived days ("I said 18-months-and-keep-control; did this quarter's *calendar* look like someone who believes that?" — the question beneath all four: **plans are priorities with dates; the review is where dates get to *contradict* priorities — and the contradiction, written down, is the whole reason we scheduled this 45 minutes.**)

The four questions' shared exit-condition (the ceremony's actual completion): **next quarter's plan-draft started *in the same session*** — the review that produces *only insights* was a journaling session (fine! — but *this* instrument's contract requires the next plan's header filled: focus-line, capacity-from-Q1's-number, gate-count from Q1's lesson, community-row from Q3's roster, matrix-header from Q4's columns — the chain's links *visible in one document*, the year becoming four plans in a trench-coat that somehow walks — the walking being *your Tuesdays*, Q1-measured, Q2-dialed, Q3-peopled, Q4-honest, until the practice needs the review no more than it needs the book: **which is the exit-condition this letter-heads toward — four reviews ago you needed the template; today you'd catch its absence in a heartbeat; the instruments' final grade is the homesickness you *won't* feel when they become unlabeled furniture.**

- **Try (15 min):** Score the four paths against *your* goals using the matrix: seven columns (timeline tolerance, control hunger, capital, ops appetite, distribution need, feedback rhythm, rights attachment), 1–5 each from *your stated priorities for the next three years* (write the priorities first — three lines — or the scores will smuggle in someone else's), then total per path and — the actual assignment — **write two sentences: (1) the path your *gut* prefers, (2) what the *numbers* said and where they disagreed.** Disagreement = your next research question (one of the column's unknowns — name it, date it: "ops appetite: shadow one ad dashboard by Oct 15"). Gut-vs-matrix friction, made visible in fifteen minutes, is worth more than a hundred industry hot-takes: the matrix is *yours*, and it updates when you do.

- **Stretch (1–2 h):** Draft your complete 90-day plan from the template — every field filled *from your real logs* (Ch 7's review numbers, not memory's favorites; the ×0.85 safety factor applied *without negotiation*; one recovery week at 60% *built in before you need it*), gates dated, one COMMUNITY row with an actual URL-and-date behind it, anti-drift rows drawn from *your* known failure signatures (F-07's? F-09's cause-patterns? path-envy's feed?), and — if your focus reaches it — pre-tick the *chapter-checklist* your plan's M3 hands off to (A or B from above: the branch the matrix already chose, its rationale one line in the plan's header). Then do the plan's first *box*: **schedule week 1's maker blocks on your actual calendar, today** — a plan without calendar blocks is a short story about yourself; with them, it's a production schedule — and the book's Project-tier promise ("measurable next-12-months plan") closes on that note: dates exist now, or the plan doesn't.

- **Project (ongoing):** Execute week 1 — every block, logged, the five-dials glance run Sunday with *honest* numbers (a skipped block gets a row, not a rewrite of history — instrument integrity is the whole religion at this point); one community step *taken* (the application sent, the event's registration bought, the swap-partner's first pages *exchanged* — taken, not researched: the row's verb was never "look into"); and the quarter's instruments armed (rhythm-audit recurring, mid-quarter review date-set, anti-drift triggers *written where you'll trip them*). If week 1 survives contact with your Tuesday, the system is *running* — and "running" is this book's actual definition of a writing career's first frame: not published, not viral, not even finished — **in motion, measured, gated, and scheduled again for next Sunday.** Everything after that is arithmetic plus luck, and you've finally built the part that isn't luck.

## Pitfalls

> PITFALL — **Path envy.** *Symptom:* your matrix re-scores itself overnight after *someone else's announcement* (their traditional deal makes indie feel like failure by noon; their bestseller-list screenshot makes querying feel like theater by dinner) — the plan's columns quietly re-weighted to *photograph well*, the COMMUNITY row's tone shifting from *partners* to *competitors*, and the distinctive psychic signature: **wanting what a path *signals* while being unable to name which of its *column-costs* you'd happily pay** (asked "would you trade cover-control for their advance?" the envy *stalls* — because it was never about the path; it was about the *feeling of being behind someone specific*, a feeling, not a plan). *Why it happens:* **comparing against announced-*outcomes* while living your own *process*** — the deepest asymmetry available in a public era: you *know your entire* ledger (every F-row, every skipped block, the v14s of your past) and you know *their highlight* (the offer, the list, the trophy) — comparison across that information-gap isn't unfair to *them*; it's *mathematically guaranteed to produce your own demoralization*, whatever the facts. Layer on: survivorship bias already named this chapter (you're comparing your *median-tuesday* to their *selected-success* — even accurate announcements are *biased samples of joy*), the culture-war overlay (path-*identity* politics — "real writers query / smart writers own" — slogans that convert *logistics* into *tribes* so the decision stops being a *column-scoring exercise* and starts being a *loyalty test* your matrix was explicitly designed to dissolve), and attention-economics (feeds *rank* announcements because announcements *engagement-rank* — the medium *selects* the envy-trigger content into your day the way generation selects completions toward the center: **the feed is a prompt-engineered sycophant for inadequacy** — Chapter 5's structure aimed at your calendar). *Fix (the book's whole apparatus, pointed at the feeling):* (1) **re-score, don't refeel:** the moment envy lands, *run the matrix with this month's actual numbers* (the exercise's instrument — envy cannot survive contact with columns you *pre-committed* before knowing whose news it would be; if the re-score *genuinely* moves — their deal revealed a column you'd been scoring lazily — that's *information*, log it as a matrix v2 row with the date and the trigger: **even envy gets adjudicated in writing, never obeyed in mood**); (2) **return to your five dials** (blocks kept, rows written, gates passed — the *only* scoreboard the plan accepts; envy *lives* in metrics you don't track — the cure isn't perspective-*aphorisms*, it's *looking at your actual instruments* — ten seconds of "gates passed: on schedule" out-hours any feed-scroll); (3) **audit the feed as a *tool*** (Ch 7's instrument-discipline: what does this account/community *measure* for you? bulletins → keep with scheduled check-ins; consistency-of-behind-feeling → cut the line like the vague-beta, *kindly, permanently* — community rows survive the purge; envy-IVs don't — the Ch 16 quadrant's trust-heuristic running on *your nervous system's output* now); (4) **name the column-cost when talking to yourself** ("their advance: +capital; their cover-control: −; their timeline: −years; their rights: −granularity…" — speak the *whole ledger* out loud, because envy *only ever reads line one* — the matrix's columns exist *philosophically* as much as numerically: to keep the exchange-rates *visible* when theAdvertisements glitter); (5) **schedule the envy-check like the voice-check** (it *will* recur — the pull never turns off, F-09's structure at career scale: quarterly re-score, trigger-logged, feed-audited — prevention again = *instrumentation + calendar*, the book's last invocation of its oldest move). *Prevention (the identity-level answer, earned by sixteen chapters):* you are not a *path*, you are a *practice with a ledger* — paths are *logistics your practice rents* the way tools were *labor your taste rented*; the announcement that *should* move you is the one about **someone whose ledger you'd respect *inside* — the writer whose logs you'd swap for theirs *including their failures*** — find those people (the exchanges, the swap-partners, the specific-notes-givers — the community quadrant was building toward exactly this shelf) and *measure against their Tuesdays*, which — the beautiful inversion — you're now finally in a position to *see*, because you keep yours in the open too: **the golden rule, one last altitude: you decide, they propose, everyone's ledger stays visible — and envy, adjudicated monthly against seven columns, becomes what it always secretly was: your next research question with a date on it.**

Related failure for Appendix C: no F-number — path-envy is *career-layer* drift, prevented by the matrix/feeds/audit stack above (the chapter's pitfall *is* the entry); cross-refs: survivorship bias (stated openly in the paths section — the bias the matrix exists to *neutralize with columns*), Ch 13's witness-vs-mood (the community-trust heuristic), Ch 7's audit rhythm (the quarterly re-score's slot), Ch 8's parked file (the "other project whispering during your quarter" variant — one focus, one unit, append-only patience).

## Checklist

> CHECKLIST — You're ready to move on when:
> - [ ] You've scored the four paths against YOUR goals — priorities written first, numbers totalled, gut-vs-matrix disagreement *named with a dated research question* behind it (and the matrix filed where next quarter's re-score finds it).
> - [ ] Your 90-day plan exists with: one focus · one unit · capacity from *real logs × 0.85* · three month-gates dated · one recovery week built in · five-dial glance scheduled · anti-drift rows from *your* failure signatures · **week 1's maker blocks on your actual calendar — today** (the Stretch's final box: plan-without-blocks doesn't count as done).
> - [ ] One community step is *taken*, not researched — application sent / pages exchanged / registration bought — with a specific-notes-giver identified as your first swap-partner candidate (the trust heuristic already *applied* once).
> - [ ] If your plan's M3 reaches it: the branch is chosen (checklist A or B, matrix-cited, one line in the plan header) and the *chapter-family* knows where it's handing off — querying pre-flight OR indie production line, each *ready as a document* — paths as *logistics your practice rents*, chosen at the gate, not worshipped in advance.
> - [ ] You read the closing letter twice — once for the list of what your folder now contains (check it: it's all really there), once for the sentence you're going to go write — because the book is done when *your* next sentence starts, and this box has no prerequisites except the eighteen that came before it.

## Key takeaways

- **Paths are trade-off structures, not verdicts:** traditional buys infrastructure with control+time; indie buys control+speed with money+ops+risk; serial buys rhythm+early-audience with privacy+platform-dependency; small press hybridizes — scored by *your* weighted matrix (gut-vs-numbers friction = the next dated research question), with survivorship bias *named* so no announcement's line-one can spend your quarter.
- **The two checklists are chapters in sequence** — finish-criteria, packages from your compression machinery, provenance from Ch 15, waits governed by verdict-vocabulary, next-project from the parked itch — executed box-by-box at *your plan's* M3 gate, never before criteria-met: the gate schedules the branch the matrix already chose.
- **The 90-day plan is the hand-off:** one focus, one unit, capacity from *real logs × 0.85 with a recovery week in*, three dated gates, five dials (blocks/rows/layers/gates — *gates passed* being the only compounding scoreboard), and anti-drift rows cut from *your* named failures — plans that hardcode paths are essays; plans that schedule gates are instruments.
- **Community is instrumentation with faces:** exchanges, swap-partners, guilds, events, bulletins-ranked-last — trust the *specific-notes-givers*, cut the *consistently-behind-feeling feeds*, and keep one calendar-backed community row alive per quarter forever (the infrastructure outlasts every platform's redesign).
- **And the closing stance, the book's argument at final altitude:** a finished folder of *evidence — skeleton, sheets, logs, criteria, rows, dossier, matrix, plan — is what "author" has meant all along, machine in the loop or not; the tool proposes endlessly and forgets everything, and you — trained at taste, structure, voice, law, and now *next* — decide, at every altitude, forever: **go write the next sentence nobody's model has your particular reasons for wanting there.**


# Appendices — Write with AI

---

## Appendix A — Prompt Library

**Purpose:** every reusable prompt from the book, copy-paste ready, in one place. Format per card: **ID · title · when to use · the prompt (fenced, versioned) · knobs to adjust · see also.** Cards are model-generic; any card depending on a specific model capability carries a `tested` line and is re-verified pre-publication. **Maintenance rule:** behavior changes bump the version (`v1 → v2`) with a one-line delta; fill-only edits don't bump. Prompts introduced in Chapters 6–14 appear here in ID order; the *teaching* versions in the chapters include commentary — the library versions are the clean instrument.

### PC-01 — Scene drafter (voice-anchor)

**When:** drafting a scene from beats you've written (Ch 10, co-draft and expand modes). **Knobs:** anchor prose (refresh per scene), beats, POV/tense, length band, end-shape, banned list. **See also:** Ch 6 (anatomy), Ch 10 (modes), PC-14 (locks companion).

```text

Role: you draft fiction scenes for me to revise; your text is raw
material, not final prose.
Context: [story situation in 2–3 lines: who wants what here, what
opposes]. Anchor prose (my voice — match rhythm and diction, do not
continue it):
[1–3 paragraphs of my prose]
Task: draft the next scene from these beats: [beats as short lines].
Constraints: POV [X], tense [past/present], [N]–[M] words; conflict
unresolved at the end; no backstory paragraphs; no words from my
banned list: [list].
Output: scene only after a line "DRAFT:" — no preamble, no titles.
```

### PC-02 — Critique my draft (structured)

**When:** first critique leg of any draft loop; scene-level review (Ch 6). **Knobs:** the five questions (swap for structure-altitude variants: paste "structure altitude — defer style" when in a Ch 12 pass-1), max-findings count. **See also:** Ch 6, Ch 12 (PC-20 for whole-draft scale).

```text

Role: skeptical fiction editor; specific, unsentimental, no praise
unless a line earns it with a reason.
Task: critique the passage below against these five questions only:
1) What does the POV character want in this scene, and is it visible
by the midpoint? 2) Where does telling occur that should be staged?
3) Which sentences are generic (could appear in anyone's story)?
4) Where does POV/distance slip? 5) Does the ending turn or fade?
Format: numbered findings, each as [location quote] → [issue] →
[concrete fix]. Max 12 findings, ranked by damage.
Passage: [text]
```

### PC-03 — Explain-my-options brainstormer

**When:** any decision point with more than three live alternatives (Ch 6; scene outcomes; Ch 9's outline choices). **Knobs:** decision statement, option count, cost-dimension labels, cast-open flag. **See also:** Ch 6, Ch 1 (options widen, you select).

```text

Role: options-broker, not co-writer; you generate choices; I choose.
Context: [story situation / decision point, 3–5 lines].
Task: give me 10 options for [decision], each labeled with:
(a) what it costs the protagonist, (b) which story shape it makes
most likely, (c) one risk it creates for pacing.
Rules: no option that requires new characters unless I mark
"cast open"; at least 3 options must be uncomfortable; no ranking,
no recommendation — I decide.
```

### PC-04 — Few-shot style transfer (my prose)

**When:** re-voicing a passage toward your own anchor (Ch 6; Ch 10 rewrite-mode companion). **Knobs:** anchor (1–3 best paragraphs), target, dimension list. **See also:** Ch 6 (few-shot), Ch 14 (PC-26 for symptom surgery — PC-04 is style *matching*, PC-26 is style *repair*).

```text

Role: stylistic apprentice to the anchor prose below.
Task: rewrite the TARGET passage to match the ANCHOR's voice.
Abstract and carry: sentence-length rhythm, contraction habits,
interiority method, image-source range, punctuation tics.
Do not carry: the anchor's content, names, or plot.
Do not improve ideas — only manner.
ANCHOR (my prose): [1–3 paragraphs]
TARGET: [passage to re-voice]
Output: rewritten target only.
```

### PC-05 — Constraint imposer ("write with…")

**When:** line-level drills and constraint tests (Ch 6; Ch 14's ladder variation rungs). **Knobs:** constraint set (length caps, object demands, dialogue caps), preserved-fields list. **See also:** Ch 6, Ch 14.

```text

Role: constraint executor for a drafting exercise.
Task: rewrite the passage under ALL of these simultaneously:
- no sentences longer than 18 words
- no abstract nouns from this list: [list]
- every paragraph must contain one concrete object handled by a hand
- dialogue lines ≤ 8 words
- keep: POV, tense, proper nouns, and the sequence of events
If a constraint makes the passage impossible, keep the closest lawful
version and list which lines you strained at, after the text.
Passage: [text]
```

### PC-06 — Prompt debugger

**When:** output misbehaves and re-rolling has failed (Ch 6; Ch 5's four forces). **Knobs:** your prompt / output / wanted-triple. **See also:** Ch 5 (field guide), Ch 6 (single-variable rule), Appendix C F-10.

```text

Role: prompt engineer for fiction workflows.
Below: MY PROMPT and THE OUTPUT I got, plus WHAT I WANTED.
Diagnose in this order, stopping at the first confirmed cause:
1) vague/buried instruction  2) internal contradiction (check my
examples vs. my rules)  3) context pressure (what in my pasted
material pulls against my instruction)  4) stacked tasks.
For each confirmed cause: quote the offending line, then give ONE
revised prompt (full text) fixing only that cause. No praise, no
general advice. End with: what to test first and what result
would prove the fix worked.
MY PROMPT: [...] OUTPUT: [...] WANTED: [...]
```

### PC-07 — What-if ladder

**When:** premise ideation from a seed (Ch 8's constrained ideation). **Knobs:** seed, rung-rule set (cost-landing, genre bans), rung count. **See also:** Ch 8, PC-08 (test the survivor), PC-10 (fusion alternative).

```text

Role: ideation partner executing a ladder, not a list.
Seed: [one sentence situation].
Produce 10 successive WHAT-IF rewrites. Rules for the ladder:
- Rungs 1–3: change ONE external condition each (place, era,
  profession, constraint of the situation) — keep characters intact.
- Rungs 4–6: each rung must give the protagonist a harder WANT
  (something they can fail at visibly).
- Rungs 7–8: each rung must add a cost that lands on a specific
  other person.
- Rungs 9–10: each rung must make the obvious genre version
  impossible (state which genre you just banned and why).
Format: numbered rungs, one line each, ending with: which rung you
would develop and one sentence of why — do not choose for me; flag
the choice as a suggestion only.
```

### PC-08 — Premise stress-test

**When:** one four-part premise ready for a months-long commitment decision (Ch 8). **Knobs:** premise text, author-proxy bullets (from your why-me file), declared constraints. **See also:** Ch 8, Appendix B (sample adjudications), Ch 12 (done-criteria cousin).

```text

Role: ruthless development editor with no stake in your feelings;
you are testing a premise for a multi-month commitment.
Premise: [four-part premise — protagonist, goal, obstacle, stakes].
Run these checks, IN THIS ORDER, in this format — for each: FINDING
(one line), DAMAGE (high/med/low), REPAIR (one concrete change, or
"none available — flag for parking"):
1. STAKES CHECK: is the cost-of-failure concrete, climbable on the
   stakes ladder, and worse than inconvenience at every rung?
2. NOVELTY AUDIT: state the three most-predictable versions of this
   premise; then state what makes MINE not those — if you cannot
   tell them apart, say "center-of-distribution" loudly.
3. WHY-ME / WHY-NOW: what in ME (author-proxy below) makes this mine
   — and what in the present makes it urgent? If either is empty,
   mark the premise.
4. CAPACITY CHECK: against my declared constraints (length target,
   POV exclusivity, single-timeline), name the structural cost this
   premise will charge.
5. KILLER QUESTION: the one objection a skeptical first reader will
   raise by page 10 — phrase it as the reader's exact words.
No praise. End with: verdict — DEVELOP / PARK / PARK-AND-STEAL-
[element] (name the element worth cannibalizing later).
Author-proxy (why-me fuel): [3–5 lines]
```

### PC-09 — Logline refiner

**When:** compressing your hand-written logline (Ch 8; Ch 16's query package reuses it). **Knobs:** premise, *your* draft logline (always yours first), anti-word list. **See also:** Ch 8, Ch 16.

```text

Role: logline editor; compression specialist; no new plot elements.
Input premise: [four-part premise]. Input draft logline (mine):
[your sentence].
Task: (1) DIAGNOSE my draft against the anatomy — name which slot
is missing, vague, or overloaded (protagonist / goal / obstacle /
cost / tonal signal). (2) Offer 6 REFINEMENTS of MY draft only —
each must keep my plot's events and characters; vary which slot
gets emphasis. (3) For each, note what it HIDES (every logline lies
by omission — name the omission).
Anti-rules: no words like "must confront"; no "battling
[abstraction]"; if my premise can't support a concrete cost, say so
instead of decorating it.
Output: diagnosis, then 6 sentences with their hidden cards.
```

### PC-10 — Idea combiner

**When:** fusing parked premises or a premise + formal constraint (Ch 8; the parked file's mining tool). **Knobs:** elements A/B/C, attempt count, anti-rules. **See also:** Ch 8, Ch 16 (the parked file as long game).

```text

Role: synthesis engineer; I provide elements; you fuse, you don't
average.
Elements: A) [parked premise one-liner]  B) [parked premise
one-liner]  C) [optional formal constraint: one location / told in
documents / no direct dialogue / etc.]
Task: 5 fusion attempts. For each, state: (1) the NEW conflict that
exists only in the fusion (not A + B side by side); (2) which
element you had to distort to make room, and how; (3) the first
scene's goal in one line.
Anti-rules: no title-generation; no "best of both worlds" language;
if an element refuses to fuse, say so and give the nearest honest
version.
Output: 5 attempts, numbered, then stop.
```

### PC-11 — Outline critic (structure pass)

**When:** first adversarial read of a level-2 outline, pre-drafting (Ch 9). **Knobs:** skeleton paste (required), structure choice + length target, prior waivers (paste known-waived rows on re-runs). **See also:** Ch 9, Ch 12 (pass-1's outline-vs-draft diff), Appendix C F-03.

```text

Role: structure critic for fiction outlines; adversarial by
assignment; praise is a failure of your job.
Context: SKELETON (stakes sentence + climax preview + arc, pasted)
+ LEVEL-2 OUTLINE (numbered change-lines) + my declared structure
choice and length target.
Task: run these six checks IN ORDER — for each: FINDING (quote or
name the beat range) → WHY IT MATTERS (one line, tied to the stakes
sentence or scene anatomy) → DAMAGE (high/med/low) → SUGGESTED
DIRECTION (a class of fix: "front-load motive," "merge beats,"
"cut" — do not rewrite my beats for me):
1. STAKES LADDER: which beats move personal/relational/existential
   rungs? Name any 3-beat stretch with zero rung movement.
2. WANT VISIBILITY: does the protagonist want on-page every 2–3
   beats? Flag stretches where things merely happen TO them.
3. MIDPOINT & CRISIS: does something irreversible change at the
   midpoint (map change, not just incident)? Is the crisis forced
   by accumulated costs rather than coincidence?
4. CAUSAL CHAIN: list any pair of adjacent beats connected only by
   "and then" where "therefore/but" is missing.
5. SETUP/PAYOFF LEDGER: every object, fact, and skill planted —
   matched to a payoff; every payoff — matched to a plant. Two
   columns, gaps listed loudly.
6. CLIMAX DEBT: does the climax preview require any character,
   rule, or prop that no prior beat introduces?
Output: six sections, numbered findings, damage-rated; end with
the THREE highest-damage findings only. No summary paragraph. No
praise. No rewritten outline.
```

### PC-12 — Pacing checker

**When:** the six checks came back clean but the read still feels off (Ch 9). **Knobs:** outline + *your* word-share estimates, structure's teaching defaults. **See also:** Ch 9, Appendix C (sag diagnostics feed PC-20).

```text

Role: pacing analyst; you measure tempo, you don't judge ideas.
Input: LEVEL-2 OUTLINE with each beat's approximate word-share
estimate (my numbers, my responsibility) OR chapter word-targets
if drafting has begun.
Task: (1) Build a tempo table: beat | space allotted | pressure type
(pursuit/refusal/revelation/reaction/transition) | rung moved
(P/R/E/—). (2) Flag: any 3+ consecutive 'transition' or '—' rows;
any revelation cluster within 2 beats; any rung-movement gaps longer
than 4 rows. (3) Compare my midpoint's share of total space to my
structure's teaching default (three-act ≈ 40–50%; note deviations
without correcting them). Output: table, then flags, then stop — no
recommendations beyond flag classes.
```

### PC-13 — Subplot weaver

**When:** subplot inventory exists and needs structural audit (Ch 9). **Knobs:** main-line outline, subplot inventory with functions. **See also:** Ch 9, Ch 11 (foil presence rows).

```text

Role: subplot engineer; every subplot must pay rent in the main line
or leave the document.
Context: LEVEL-2 OUTLINE (main line) + my subplot inventory below
(name · thematic echo of main want · structural function).
Task: for EACH subplot: (1) ENTRY: name the earliest beat where its
absence would be noticed — if none, mark VESTIGIAL; (2) TOUCH
BEATS: every main-line beat where the subplot must surface (min
every 4–5 beats — flag longer silences); (3) PAYOFF: the exact beat
where it changes stakes or meaning of the main line — if you can't
point at one, mark RENT-FREE and recommend parking; (4) COLLISION:
the beat where subplot and main line physically share a scene.
Output: per-subplot cards + inventory of VESTIGIAL / RENT-FREE
items. I decide what to park.
```

### PC-14 — Scene expander (beats → prose)

**When:** drafting under full locks with a bible pack (Ch 10's scene-beat prompting; the workhorse). **Knobs:** bible extract, anchor, charged beats, lock list (POV range, tense, banned *constructions*, end-shape, speech caps). **See also:** Ch 10, PC-01 (lighter sibling), PC-16 (post-draft description repair).

```text

Role: drafting apprentice; output is material for my red pen.
Context (bible pack): [relevant names/spellings, story rules, the
scene's stakes line] + ANCHOR (my prose, voice target — match
rhythm/diction, do not improve):
[your hand-written paragraph]
Beats (change-lines, in order — dramatize in this order, no new
plot turns):
1. [...]
LOCKS (violate none): POV [close third, X only]; tense [past];
distance [2–4, deepest only at the outcome]; length [N–M words];
no backstory paragraphs; banned constructions: [emotion-as-weather,
etc.]; banned words: [style sheet]; dialogue carries subtext — no
character states the scene's conflict outright; END BEAT [#] must
land as [action / refusal / unresolved question], no summary
sentence after it.
Output: prose only after "SCENE:" — no titles, no notes.
```

### PC-15 — Dialogue-first drafter

**When:** relationship-first scenes; voice differentiation work (Ch 10; Ch 11). **Knobs:** per-speaker voice notes (from sheets), situation, beats, line caps. **See also:** Ch 10 (attribution pass stays yours), Ch 11 (PC-18 tests the result).

```text

Role: unattributed dialogue generator; speakers distinguished ONLY
by their speech, never by my telling you who talks.
Context: two speakers' VOICE NOTES (diction, syntax, verbal habit,
never-say) + situation + beats 1–N.
Task: draft the exchange with NO dialogue tags and NO action beats —
just alternating lines, each speaker marked only as A:/B:.
Locks: each line advances, refuses, or deflects — no greetings, no
small talk openings; every A-line must be unlike a line B could say
(swap test); the scene's conflict never stated in plain words; end
on B's non-answer.
Then STOP — attribution pass is mine.
Output: A:/B: lines only.
```

### PC-16 — Description sharpener

**When:** post-draft description pass at constant count (Ch 10; Ch 12's line pass tools). **Knobs:** banned-simile list, sensory-channel gaps, count-retention rule. **See also:** Ch 4's ladder, Ch 10, Appendix C F-01.

```text

Task: DESCRIPTION PASS on the scene below — diagnostic first.
1. List every descriptive sentence. Tag each [owned] (could only be
   in this story) or [generic] (any story's room — rung 2).
2. For each [generic]: propose ONE replacement at the SAME length,
   spending: this scene's pressure, one sensory channel not yet used
   in the paragraph, and no new information the scene hasn't learned
   yet.
3. Keep [owned] untouched, verbatim.
Constraints: total descriptive word count may not increase; no
weather indoors unless rule-allows; no similes drawn from: [list].
Output: tagged list, then the revised scene, then a count diff.
```

### PC-17 — Character interviewer

**When:** situational character mining before a pressured scene (Ch 11). **Knobs:** sheet fields, scene pressure, question count, flaw-first rule (never remove). **See also:** Ch 11, Ch 3's sheets.

```text

Role: interviewer for a FICTION character IN SCENE — you ask the
questions; the CHARACTER answers in first person; you also note
what the answer refuses.
Context: character sheet below (fields 1,5,6,10 minimum) + the
specific upcoming scene's pressure.
Rules for answers: (1) every answer must include ONE concrete detail
a camera could film; (2) no abstract self-diagnosis words; (3) if
the question could be answered kindly by anyone, the character must
answer it specifically unkindly or specifically wrongly — their flaw
speaks first; (4) 60-word cap per answer.
Ask me 8 questions, ONE AT A TIME — wait for my reply roleplaying
the answer, then judge: "in-character / drifting to intern /
contradicts sheet field __" and correct before the next question.
After Q8: output the THREE answers most usable for the scene and ONE
refusal the character wouldn't touch (and why they wouldn't).
```

### PC-18 — Voice differentiation test

**When:** blind-attribution audit of dialogue (Ch 11; Ch 12's character pass). **Knobs:** cast voice-notes, exchange sample size. **See also:** Ch 11, Ch 3's field 10.

```text

Role: blind attribution examiner.
Input: 6–10 dialogue EXCHANGES from my draft, speakers labeled only
A/B (strip tags first).
Task 1: for each exchange, guess the speaker from character sheets
(paste voice notes: diction / syntax / habit / never-say). Show
confidence 1–5 per guess.
Task 2: list every line that could have been said by EITHER speaker
without meaning changing (swap-violations), quote them.
Task 3: for each cast voice, output a ONE-LINE "fingerprint."
Output: guess table, swap list, fingerprints. NO rewrites — fixing
is my pass.
```

### PC-19 — Arc tracker

**When:** every draft mint; Ch 12's character-pass input (Ch 11). **Knobs:** sheet (arc type, false belief, pattern), chapter notes or pasted passages. **See also:** Ch 11, Ch 12 pass 2.

```text

Role: continuity auditor for CHARACTER CHANGE (not plot).
Context: character sheet (arc type, false belief, flaw-pattern,
climax choice) + my chapter-by-chapter notes + any drafted chapters
pasted (sheet FIRST).
Task: for each chapter-note/passage, extract in a table:
chapter | pressure applied | pattern response (quote the triggering
line if pasted) | false-belief status (reinforced / challenged /
unaddressed) | cost logged? (Y/N — which cost) | rung moved
(P/R/E/—).
Flag: (a) 3+ consecutive chapters with pattern UNADDRESSED; (b)
growth-behavior appearing BEFORE any cost paid (arc pre-paid);
(c) any row contradicting the sheet's arc type. NO recommendations —
flags are my inventory to adjudicate.
```

### PC-20 — Developmental reader (full pass)

**When:** structure pass, whole draft or per part (Ch 12). **Knobs:** skeleton + outline-current + §5 + target (required context), altitude note ("structure altitude — defer style" after v1's density lesson), batch size. **See also:** Ch 12, Ch 13 (blind-spot section feeds beta questions).

```text

Role: developmental reader — FIRST READER, not editor: you
experience the draft as a reader and report; you do not rewrite.
Context: SKELETON + outline-current + story rules + length/structure
target — pasted from the bible pack. Draft below (batch: [part —
full scenes, never summaries]).
Task: reader's report in this order, each finding location-quoted:
1. PROMISES: what does the draft seem to promise? Quote evidence.
2. PAY-OFF TRACK: where did you PULSE or SAG? Chapter/scene numbers
   + exact paragraph. Sag = where you'd skim — WHY (pressure /
   confusion / familiarity).
3. CHARACTER READ: what you believe now about each major character +
   the line that created it. Flag contradictions vs. pasted sheet.
4. ENDING DEBT: what does the ending owe given the promises — does
   the last quarter start paying?
5. BLIND SPOTS: three things you CANNOT evaluate from this batch
   (label: needs next part / needs outline / genuinely unsure).
Numbered sections, quotes required, worst first. NO praise section.
NO rewritten prose. Empty section: "none found — checked: X."
```

### PC-21 — Line editor (constrained)

**When:** line pass, chapter-by-chapter with row-level adjudication (Ch 12; Ch 14's de-AI overlaps — PC-21 *sweeps mechanics*, PC-26 *re-voices symptoms*). **Knobs:** style sheet paste, `[!mine]` markers set, target list, length-retention. **See also:** Ch 12, Ch 14.

```text

Role: line editor under STRICT constraints; changes must be
justifiable against my style sheet (pasted: banned list, rhythm
preferences, voice notes) — pretty-for-its-own-sake is a failure.
Chapter below. Rules:
1. TARGETS ONLY: filter words, redundant doubles, passive where
   agent known, dialogue-tag adverbs, sentences >25 words WITHOUT
   deliberate rhythm justification (list any you keep and why).
2. PRESERVE ABSOLUTELY: proper nouns & spellings, POV/distance,
   tense, lock-list constructions, every fingerprint speech line,
   my unusual syntax if marked [!mine].
3. NO NEW INFORMATION: delete and tighten only — no new images,
   metaphors, or intensifiers. Word count: ≤100% of original.
4. OUTPUT: (a) diff list: ORIGINAL → EDITED + reason tag, (b) full
   clean chapter, (c) counts: changes n, words before/after.
I accept/reject per row; rejections are final.
```

### PC-22 — Continuity checker

**When:** continuity pass in bible-first batches of 2–3 chapters (Ch 12; Ch 5's type-split made executable). **Knobs:** bible extract (re-paste each batch), batch size (never grow it), battery categories. **See also:** Ch 12, Appendix C F-06.

```text

Role: continuity auditor — bible-first, quotes-or-nothing.
Context: BIBLE EXTRACT (names list verbatim + relevant sheets +
timeline slice + rules + never-say lists) pasted ABOVE the chapters
— the extract is your ONLY source of truth for "correct."
Chapters: [batch of 2–3 full chapters — never more].
Battery, separate tables per category:
A. CONTRADICTIONS: passage | bible fact | chapter — only where the
   pasted extract covers the fact. Uncertain → "needs bible check"
   row, NOT a guess.
B. NAMES/SPELLINGS: every proper noun vs. names list.
C. DATES/AGES/TIME: reconstruct timeline slice; flag impossible
   sequences.
D. PROPS/TRAITS: scars/handedness/objects vs. sheets — quote both.
E. RULES: banned words/constructions/assurances — hit lists.
F. POV/TENSE: interior-access sentences — list; flag non-anchored.
Six tables, quotes mandatory, no commentary, no suggestions.
Uncertainty is a ROW TYPE, not a failure.
```

### PC-23 — Beta-question designer

**When:** before briefing human betas (Ch 13; your open flags are its required context). **Knobs:** open-flags paste, beta profiles, the ten-question architecture. **See also:** Ch 13, Appendix B (question keys' spirit).

```text

Role: research-methods assistant for reader feedback — you design
QUESTIONS, not feedback; nothing about the draft's quality.
Context: my draft's OPEN FLAGS (pass logs excerpt) + stage
declaration + beta profile (1 sentence each).
Task: draft a questionnaire of TEN questions in this architecture:
Q1 ranked #1-fix | Q2 locate-skim ("name the paragraph") | Q3
belief-at-midpoint (+ which line) | Q4 prediction check | Q5
character testimony (side + trigger) | Q6 trust moment (+ line) |
Q7 two witness-forms of my known flags (NEVER "did it sag?" — if my
flag names the flaw, rephrase to address-free witness form) | Q8
last-line residue (exact words if remembered) | Q9 who-would-you-tell
+ why | Q10 permission-to-destroy ("quote your harshest line").
Rules: every question answerable with location+memory; zero craft
jargon; no question may name my anxiety or preferred reading; cap
each at 25 words. Output: the ten questions + a 3-line "how to
brief" note (stage, deadline, wrong-and-specific permission).
```

### PC-24 — Simulated reader perspectives (with caveats)

**When:** pre-flight only — questionnaire pilot, verified candidate-hunting, prediction rehearsal (Ch 13). **NEVER** as: substitute for humans, post-human "second opinions," or anything called beta in shared contexts. **Knobs:** persona (from *types*, privacy rule), witness protocol mirroring your Qs. **See also:** Ch 13's REALITY CHECK and ETHICS NOTE — reread both before running.

```text

Role: you simulate a READER PERSONA responding to pages — you
generate their simulated reading-experience report; you do NOT
critique as yourself, and you attach no quality judgment of your own.
Persona (I provide): [3–5 lines — reading life, tastes, irritants,
what they DNF for — written by me, from real people as TYPES only,
never a specific real person's private details]
Witness protocol (mirror of my human Qs): for each chapter-range:
[1] speed-report with location | [2] belief-state + the line | [3]
prediction + confidence 1–5 | [4] trust-check (+ why) | [5] residue.
Then META-3: (a) which of my ten questions would YOU answer vaguely
— flag for redesign; (b) one witness-question my ten MISSED;
(c) anything where you had to INVENT rather than report — label
"invented."
Closing disclaimer line (mandatory, verbatim):
"Simulated testimony — no independent weight; verify against human
reads."
```

### PC-25 — Feedback triager

**When:** after intake, before any revision decisions (Ch 13). **Knobs:** vision pack (required), intake rows verbatim, pass-log excerpts for corroboration-cites. **See also:** Ch 13, Appendix C F-08.

```text

Role: triage clerk — you classify and cluster; you NEVER vote on
vision (that's mine) and you never soften quotes.
Context: my VISION PACK (stakes sentence, done-criteria headline,
pass-1 waivers list, style bans — pasted) + intake rows (verbatim
quotes, reader, Q#, address where answered).
Task: (1) dedupe/cluster strictly BY ADDRESS; (2) tag each row:
located/addressless · witness/verdict-form · corroborates-pass-log?
(cite the log row if in pack) · praise-only; (3) propose VERDICT
CANDIDATE from {CHANGE, DISCUSS, OVERRIDE, FIX-THE-QUESTION} against
the vision pack — candidate only, with the pack-line that supports
it; (4) surface contradictions as PAIRS with both quotes, never
merged; (5) rank-1 findings listed first if marked. Output: table +
"pack lines used" + NOTHING resembling a revision plan (changes are
my drafting).
```

### PC-26 — De-AI prose pass

**When:** symptom-driven re-voicing after diagnosis (Ch 14). **Knobs:** style sheet (required), diagnosis quote + counts, `[!mine]` markers set *before* pasting. **See also:** Ch 14, Ch 12's line pass (altitude split: PC-21 sweeps mechanics; PC-26 re-voices families).

```text

Role: sentence surgeon executing MY revoicing — you remove symptoms
and preserve skeleton; you do not add content, images, or opinions;
when unsure whether a line is load-bearing, LEAVE IT (flag instead).
Context: my STYLE SHEET (banned constructions + rhythm notes +
diction-class + tics-to-keep) + my diagnosis output (worst-offender
quote + symptom counts) + the passage.
Targets (apply only where flagged):
H1 hedge-strip (keep load-bearing uncertainty — list kept)
H2 diction-demote (upgrade-itis → my narrator's class; safe-familiar
   → my class at specificity, not fanciness)
H3 rhythm-break (mark narrow-band stretches; ONE split/merge/fragment
   per stretch — minimum intervention)
H4 reflection-tax cut (delete commentaries the scene performed;
   break symmetry pairs at ONE side — I choose from my sheet).
Preserve absolutely: plot facts, proper nouns, locked POV/tense/
distance, [!mine] markers, style-sheeted KEEPs.
Output: (a) table: ORIGINAL → EDITED + symptom-tag + keep-flags;
(b) clean passage; (c) counts per family. I adjudicate rows as in
PC-21; rejections final.
```

### PC-27 — Style imitator → variator

**When:** voice ladder runs (Ch 14, rungs 1–2). **Knobs:** target author's page, feature axis chosen, subject-matter swap-ins. **See also:** Ch 14 (acquisition stays in your hands — the *copying* is never delegated).

```text

Role: style-analyst and exercise-partner — analysis and LABELED
DRAFTS ONLY; the hand-copying rung stays mine.
Part A — FEATURE INVENTORY (on the page I paste below): list the
techniques with numbers where possible: sentence-length pattern,
image sources, hedge frequency, balance usage, paragraph
architecture, dialogue-to-action ratio. Features only, no quality
judgment, no praise.
Part B — VARIATION DRAFTS (labeled exercise-only, 150 words each):
(a) same techniques, MY subject matter: [swap-in]; (b) same content
of my target passage, OPPOSITE their tendencies; (c) their page at
half-length with my banned list applied.
Output: inventory, then three drafts each prefixed "EXERCISE — not
for manuscript," then one line: which axis you think is most
load-bearing in the original and why (I will disagree or agree in
my notes).
```

**Library maintenance (recap):** drafts promote to cards only on second-run evidence; every card carries knobs + a failure note; retirements get autopsies (Ch 6's lifecycle); `tested` dates re-validated at tool switches and pre-publication (Appendix F). Your file should eventually embarrass this one with your own cases.

---

## Appendix B — Workbook Keys & Extra Exercises

**Purpose:** sample solutions for selected `Try`/`Stretch` exercises + extra drills. **Keys are sample responses, not correct answers** — they show *shape of engagement*, not the one true reply; your version differing from ours is usually a good sign, not a failure. `Project` exercises are never keyed (they build your own story). Because shapes teach better than summaries, the most-requested keys are printed at full worked length below, then the briefer markers for the rest.

**Ch 1 keys.** *Stretch (unaided vs. AI revision):* a strong log-note names one improvement **and** one casualty — e.g., "specificity up (added the broken gate detail the machine proposed — I rejected three adjective-y versions first); casualty: my second sentence's odd rhythm got smoothed — I restored it; net: submitting revised, but keeping original's sentence 2." The *casualty* line is the key's whole point: revisions without casualties are unexamined. *Baseline purpose check:* if your 200 words were "fine," good — Ch 14 needs *representative* you, not worst you.

**Ch 2 keys.** *10 premises in 20 minutes — quality markers (not content):* ≥3 genres represented; every premise has a checkable goal verb (find/get/sell/prevent — not "understand/realize/be happy"); ≥1 premise's stakes sentence names a *relational* loss; the two circled ones typically share one trait — *the obstacle welded to the protagonist's profession or wound* (pattern to notice, not rule to force). *Stakes-ladder sample (for a generic "heist" premise):* personal: he keeps the crew's respect / relational: his daughter's wedding invitation arrives revoked / existential: the code he lives by ("I finish what I start") dies — note rung 3 phrasing the *self-story as infrastructure*, the shape from the chapter.

**Ch 3 keys.** *Want/need for three known characters — shape check:* (1) want verb ≠ need verb in all three; (2) at least one need is *relational* not self-improvement-shaped ("learn to trust himself" is fog — "call his brother" is a need with an action built in); (3) one pair where the *want's success harms the need* (the friction column — seeing this once in *known* characters makes it buildable in *yours*). *Flaw-to-climax chain — link quality markers:* each choice *defensible in-character* (you can imagine the character justifying it aloud), each cost lands on someone/something named, climax line contains the flaw's *own instrument* turned (the pen, the schedule, the procedure).

**Ch 4 keys.** *Summary→scene labelling:* your labels should make the *conflict* visible before the prose does — if the prose reads conflict-free but labels claim one, the conflict is in your head (add an opposition beat: the door sticks, the offer is refused, the tea is poured for two and drunk by one). *Two-POV compare — what to notice:* each POV's * ignorance list* (what it can't know) should be doing structural work — irony, suspense, or misdirection; if both versions came out nearly identical, you anchored both in *events* rather than *minds* (re-do with one character's wound in the foreground of their version).

**Ch 5 keys.** *Five broken prompts (Ch 6's Try mirror):* (1) needs task+output shape+stakes context ("sad about the sea" → whose sea, what lost, how long, what shape — 60-word scene or 6-line stanza, and *why you want it*); (2) "better" needs criteria (paste original + name 2 targets: tighten / cut clichés / — never "make it better"); (3) missing context entirely (attach the story + the voice anchor + say what "continue" means: next scene? next paragraph?); (4) four adjectives doing zero checkable work → constraints: setting's pressure, each line carrying a lie, ≤6-word speeches, no guns; (5) no chapter, no questions → PC-02's five questions with the chapter pasted. *Common thread:* every fix adds **task + context + shape**; none adds politeness.

**Ch 6 keys.** *Five broken prompts (the chapter's own Try):* same anatomy as above — expected fixes add all five slots and convert ≥1 adjective; a full-credit fix to #5 also adds "critique structure first, altitude noted" (the altitude habit previewed).

**Ch 8 Try key (what-if ladder ×10):** shape markers — rungs 7–8 each name a *person* who pays; rungs 9–10 *state the banned genre* (an unlabeled ban is a wish, not a constraint); if your best rung is #1–#3, the seed was better than you rated it (the early rungs' job is *condition-swapping*, not profundity — don't rank rungs by impressiveness; rank by *pressure*).

**Ch 13 Try key (ten beta questions):** the graded dimensions — Q1 ranks; ≥8 questions pass location+memory; zero questions contain your anxieties (search the text for your known worries — if found, rewrite to witness-form); one question grants destruction. *A strong Q7 conversion:* "Does the middle sag?" → "Any paragraph you read faster or slower than the rest — name it, even if you don't know why."

**Worked keys — the ones readers ask about most (full samples):**

*Ch 2 Stretch (outline one premise at three granularities — sample for a generic "storm-warning sirens go silent" seed):* **Level 1:** "The town's storm sirens stop mid-alarm one Tuesday — and the engineer who could fix them has been lying about why for a year — so the council's vote to decommission the system (saving the budget that funds her father's care) lands the same afternoon the next real front forms." (Four parts audible: goal *keep the system live until the vote*, obstacle *her own buried fault-report*, stakes *care-home money vs. the coast's warning* — note the obstacle welding again: her *profession* and her *wound* are the same object.) **Level 2 sample lines (six of ~14):** "1 · Sirens die at 2:47 p.m.; Marta logs it as *grid flicker* — first false line in the ledger she'll have to walk back. → 2 · Council email confirms Friday's decommission vote; the attached budget table highlights her father's facility line. → 3 · Old fault-report draft surfaces in her files, timestamped the week before the first silence — she deletes it, then undeletes: something won't let evidence die. → 4 · (neighbor-test ✓ pressures 5) Teenager's hobby weather-station posts the Tuesday gap publicly; three replies ask the exact question Marta's report answers. → 5 · Trial balloon: Marta suggests 'scheduled maintenance' at the pre-vote briefing; the mayor's aide repeats it word-for-word on local radio — her lie now has a broadcast voice she didn't give it. → 6 · Father, mid-visit, asks why the sirens 'went shy last week' — he heard them stop from the care-home window; the question costs Marta the visit's last twenty minutes." — what to notice: every line *changes* something (status, exposure, cost — the neighbor-test's product), rungs move in clusters (2: personal-care pressure;
